# Issues

**The register of everything open in this repo.** Defects, gaps, and questions
awaiting a ruling. One id per item, stable forever.

**This file is the source of truth for what is open.** Nothing is open unless it
is here. Other documents no longer carry their own open-item lists — they link
here. Long analysis may stay where it was argued (`packages/*/PROJECT_STATE.md`,
`IDEAS/*.md`); the **Detail** column points at it. What must
never happen again is an item existing in exactly one document that nobody reads.

**What belongs here:** shipped behavior that is wrong, a gap that blocks
something, a question that needs a decision. **What does not:** features and
roadmap (`IDEAS/`), settled rulings (`DECISIONS.md`), traps that are correct
behavior you must know about (`CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards), history
(git, `FJS-D675`).

**Closed items age out to [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).**
§ Closed below keeps the recent ones; the archive keeps the rest, verbatim.
Together they hold every id ever issued, and an id is never reused — so a
`FJS-###` named in a comment, a handoff, or a package file resolves in exactly
one of the two files. Search both before concluding an id is unknown.

## Conventions

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Id** | `FJS-###` for a defect or gap, `FJS-D##` for a decision. **Never reused, never renumbered.** Closing an item moves it to § Closed with its id. |
| **Sev** | `S1` blocker — data loss, a security hole, or cannot ship · `S2` high — silently wrong, costs a day to find · `S3` medium · `S4` low or cosmetic |
| **Status** | `open` — verified open on the date shown · `stale?` — inherited from an older audit and **not re-verified**; probe before acting · `contested` — the recorded diagnosis is doubted, see the note |
| **Verified** | The date somebody last ran it. A `stale?` row's date is when it was *written*, not confirmed. |
| **Blocked by** | A row that cannot start until another closes says `blocked by FJS-###` in its own text, and nowhere else. `fli next` takes it out of the ranking until that row closes and weighs the blocker up. Write it only where the order is real — a row that merely relates to another cites it. |
| **By hand** | A row a headless session cannot finish — a run longer than a session lasts, a check only the owner can make — says `by hand` as its own segment of its links cell. `fli next` keeps it ranked and marks it; `fix:loop` skips it. |

**A row has exactly the columns its table's header names**, and `fli
register:check` fails on anything else (`row-shape`). Both ways of getting it
wrong are silent: a row with FEWER columns is one pasted from the other table,
and the reader infers a shape from the cell count, so it is given a status
nobody wrote and no other rule can see it; a row with MORE has the excess
**dropped when the file is rendered** — in § Closed that is the *How* column, so
the row displays with no citations while they sit in the file. Links belong
inside the last declared column, joined with ` · `, and a `|` that is part of a
sentence is escaped `\|`.

`stale?` is load-bearing. The eight rows inherited from the 2026-07-31 audit
(now `IDEAS/review-coherence.md`) were re-probed on 2026-08-10 and **five of the eight were wrong**:
two were already fixed (conduit's secrets, `admin:generate`), one was never
reproducible (conduit's version skew), and two were right about a defect and
wrong about what it was — sierra's prefetch is not an auth *bypass* but a
cached 401, and caravan's row named three bugs of which two were fixed.
**Treat a `stale?` row as a lead, not a fact** (`VERIFYING.md`).

**The register also goes stale in the closing direction, which nothing here was
watching for.** `FJS-043` was fixed in the tree with a comment describing the
old defect in the past tense, and stayed `open` for eight days because closing
is a second action nobody owed. A row's `Verified` date is evidence about when
somebody last *looked*, not about whether the defect survives — 22 rows carried
`open` at 2026-08-02 when this audit ran, which is the same fiction `stale?`
exists to name, wearing the other label.

| <a id="fjs-2175"></a>FJS-2175 | junction | **bearerClaim({ from: cookie() }) refuses a dead cookie with 401 on a request that also signed in, so a seller who once opened a customer's portal link to check it is refused on every call of their own session once that link is replaced, for the cookie's life** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 4 (Q10), [portal.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/portal.test.ts) over HTTP: GET /api/invoices with a member's session token AND a dead lago_portal cookie → 401 'This link does not work'. authorization() passes on a token the request signed in with (FJS-2150's fix); cookie() has no such rule, and a cookie is ambient: the seller's browser sends it on every request to the origin, path /. In a billing product the seller opening the customer's link first is the ordinary check, and issuing a fresh link revokes the old one, so the seller's dashboard dies at the next link they send. App workaround: the composed principal skips the portal claim when user is set ([principal.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/core/principal.ts)). Wanted: cookie() passes (claims {}) on a signed-in request, or bearerClaim answers a dead ambient credential with no claim rather than a refusal |
| <a id="fjs-2171"></a>FJS-2171 | litestone | **_sum and _avg over a @money(field: currency) column add unlike currencies with no on that currency: three invoices of $49, €45 and ¥7,000 answer one bare 16,400, which a dashboard prints as $164.00** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 4 (Q4), [currencies.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/currencies.test.ts): `invoice.aggregate({ _sum: { totalAmount: true } })` over USD, EUR and JPY rows (cents, cents, whole yen) answers { _sum: { totalAmount: 16400 } }, as the system and as a member through the scoped client, and _avg 5466.67; nothing refuses it and nothing in the answer names a currency. docs/exact-numbers.md:253 admits it ("Aggregating across currencies is not prevented … Group by the currency column"), so the rule is written in prose and enforced nowhere, while the column already names its currency column (x-money: { field }). groupBy({ by: ['currency'] }) answers right. The schema knows enough to refuse: a _sum/_avg/_min/_max over a @money(field: f) column is unlike unless `by` includes f or `where` pins f to one value; a @money(USD) column is always like. It reaches every caller through junction's aggregate verb (FJS-D226), which a browser calls. The app's answer: revenue grouped by currency in one function ([revenue.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/domain/billing/revenue.ts)). FJS-1643 is the view half |
| <a id="fjs-2170"></a>FJS-2170 | litestone · junction | **A commitment on a day is read in ONE zone per tenant database, so a period that ends in the customer's zone closes on the sweep's clock: Lago's grace period (on: periodEnd + graceDays) fired nine hours late for a Tokyo customer and seven hours early for a Los Angeles one, and the only spelling that fires right is an instant the schema cannot check** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 4 (Q8), [zones.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/zones.test.ts), control kept live on a schema copy with the Phase 0–3 line put back. Invoice.periodEnd is a @date in the customer's zone (Lago stamps invoices.timezone). due({ by, timeZone: 'UTC' }) is empty at 2026-10-03T15:00Z, when Tokyo's grace ended, and answers at 2026-10-04T00:00Z; Los Angeles's ends 2026-10-04T07:00Z, so the same fire closes it seven hours early and the last seven hours of grace-period usage miss the invoice (they are trued up a month later). commitments({ timeZone: (tenant) => zone }) resolves per tenant DATABASE, and Lago's organizations share one, so even a per-organization zone has no spelling; due() takes one zone per call, and a sweep in Tokyo's zone is right for Tokyo and 16 hours early for LA. The workaround shipped: `closesAt DateTime @immutable`, computed at creation as startOfDay(periodEnd + graceDays, customer.timeZone), and `@@commitment(close, on: closesAt)`; SubscriptionPeriod.endsAt likewise. Its cost: the schema no longer says WHAT the deadline is, and nothing can check the instant agrees with the day and the grace (SQLite has no zones, so no @@check), pinned: a row with closesAt 2030 for a 2026 period is accepted. Wanted: a zone the commitment reads per row, e.g. `@@commitment(close, on: periodEnd + graceDays, zone: timeZone)` or FJS-D512's `@zoned(path)` on the @date read by dueSql, with due() grouping by zone. Related: FJS-D512, FJS-1214, FJS-1234 · [schema.lite Invoice.closesAt](../fjs-prototypes/lago/db/schema.lite) · [calendar.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/domain/billing/calendar.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2163"></a>FJS-2163 | junction · litestone | **A @@commitment whose move @seals hands its hook a row the move has already sealed, so nothing the document owes at its seal can be written there, and a hook that re-reads the period for its draft finds none, writes a second document, and the fire answers fired** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 3 Q6: Invoice finalize: draft -> finalized @seals @system with @@commitment(finalize, on: periodEnd + graceDays, while: status == 'draft'). The hook runs after the move inside the move's transaction (FJS-D368), which on a sealing move is after the seal: refreshing the draft from events (Lago's grace-period refresh) and writing the number were each refused SealedDocumentError, and a refresh that looked up the period's draft found none, drafted a second September invoice, and fireCommitment returned 'fired' — that draft owes its own finalize, so September would have sealed twice. Boot, fli check and fli advise said nothing of a hook keyed to a sealing commitment. The app's spelling is a state that is never committed: close: draft -> closing @system carries the commitment and the hook refreshes, numbers and seals in one update, plus @@unique([subscriptionId, periodStart], where: …) against the second draft; example's per-period row (FJS-D367) works around the same seam. Options: a hook tier that runs before the move, a move that takes the hook's data, or a boot refusal of a hook on a @seals commitment · [issue.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/domain/billing/issue.ts) · [invoice-run.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/invoice-run.test.ts) · [commitments/index.ts](packages/junction/src/plugins/commitments/index.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2148"></a>FJS-2148 | litestone | **An @@allow or @@deny on a model the schema declares for a driver trail database parses and is never enforced, so any caller reads every row of the trail that model holds** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09 fixing FJS-2139. The parser refuses @@gate/@@allow/@@deny on a driver jsonl model (FJS-1920) but checks nothing on a driver trail one, and the jsonl table runs no row policy. A probe with a declared AuditRow carrying @@allow('read', auth().id == actorId): caller b read caller a's row. @@gate is now graded there (FJS-2139), and a row policy is not. Wanted: refuse the row policy by name, as FJS-1920 does, or compile one for the jsonl reader · [parser.js jsonl refusal](packages/litestone/src/core/parser.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2084"></a>FJS-2084 | litestone · auth · cli · repo | **The tree writes `driver trail` / `@@trail` and the published litestone and auth still speak `logger` / `@@log`, so CI's `scaffold` phase is red, and `registry` is green only by allowance, until both are republished** | open | 2026-10-09 | `FJS-D661` renamed the trail family with no alias. `fli new --source npm --auth` (the `scaffold` phase) installs the PUBLISHED `@frontierjs/litestone` and runs `db push` against a schema the tree's templates now write with `driver trail` — refused: *driver must be 'sqlite', 'jsonl', or 'logger'*. The `registry` phase refuses `@frontierjs/auth@1.0.7`'s `db/user.lite` and `db/auth.lite` (`@@log`) under the tree's parser; that half is allowed in `scripts/ci-allowances.json` `knownPublishedSchemas`, keyed by version so it goes stale on the publish. **No user is broken today**: every published package still agrees with every other. The hazard is a PARTIAL publish — a new cli with the old litestone, or the reverse. **Fix: publish litestone and auth together** (cli and create-frontier with them), then drop the allowance; the scaffold phase goes green on its own. Re-probed 2026-10-08: npm and the tree both hold litestone 1.1.9 and auth 1.0.7, and the published `src/core/parser.js:260` still refuses `trail`; the tree needs no edit, only the owner's publish. Re-probed later 2026-10-08: the `registry` phase passes, through the `knownPublishedSchemas` allowance and nothing else — a passing registry is NOT this row closing; it closes on the publish, when the allowance goes stale and is dropped. · by hand · **The working tree's own apps, 2026-10-09 (lago stressor).** 12 of the apps in fjs-prototypes still declare `driver logger` (ksite chatwoot portal lago quo connectteam transit linear calendly remnant jazzhr notion), linked to the tree, so each one's next `fli db:migrate` fails with *driver must be 'sqlite', 'jsonl', or 'trail', got 'logger'*. The refusal names the three legal words and not the rename, so nothing says logger became trail or that `@@log` became `@@trail` with it (lago had 15 `@@log(audit)`); fli check passes the schema meanwhile (FJS-1242). Wanted: the parser names the old word as renamed, as it does for other retired spellings · [lago schema.lite](../fjs-prototypes/lago/db/schema.lite) |
| <a id="fjs-2062"></a>FJS-2062 | litestone · mcp · junction | **A caller over JSON or /mcp cannot supply a File column at all: the tool schema asks for the stored reference object and the ORM refuses every object for a File column, so an app whose core flow is an upload cannot do that flow through its own agent** | open | 2026-10-08 | Measured 2026-10-08, base44 stressor Phase 8 M9 (`runs/2026-10-08-connect.json`, oracle 17-immich, `Photo.original File @accept("image/*")`, FileStorage `local` installed). The agent, at USER(4), sent `photos_create` with `original` as the reference object the tool schema describes (`key`, `bucket`, `provider`, `size`, `mime`, `uploadedAt`) twice. Both were rejected 400 with *original was given an object where a value was expected. The atomic operators …*: an object that is not bytes is not `isFileValue`, so FileStorage passes it on and it reaches `refuseUnbindable`. Sent as a path string, it was refused by the tool's own input check (*data/original must be object*). 0 rows. Over JSON the only value FileStorage takes is a server path ([FJS-2061](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-2061)). This stops Immich's M9 and any upload flow over /mcp. The refusal also names atomic operators, which have nothing to do with a file. Fix: a JSON-shaped file value (base64 or a data URL with a name and type, bounded by size) that FileStorage takes as bytes, with the tool schema describing that value instead of the stored reference, or an upload route that hands back a reference the create then accepts · [FJS-1824](#fjs-1824) · [base44 PLAN § Phase 8](../fjs-prototypes/base44/PLAN.md) · blocked by `FJS-D666` |
| <a id="fjs-1987"></a>FJS-1987 | junction · litestone | **A signed-in user who also presents a bearer grant is ONE principal holding both: bearerClaim merges the grant's claims onto the session, and a column defaulted from a claim (@default(auth().contactId)) then stamps the GRANT's value on a row the USER's own rule admitted, which FJS-D576 does not grade as a read — so an agent of one org opened a thread in their own inbox naming another org's contact, which naming it by hand is a 422** | open | 2026-10-08 | **Measured** 2026-10-08, chatwoot stressor Phase 3 (Q6, an agent who is also a visitor). An org-A agent's request carrying their session AND an org-B widget grant (`x-visitor-token`): `POST /conversations { inboxId: <A's inbox> }` → **201**, contactId and identityId B's (stamped by `@default(auth().contactId)`/`auth().identityId`), admitted by the agent's `check(inbox, 'read')`. The same contactId named by hand is a 422 *names no Contact* (FJS-D576's read). And every message the agent wrote, reply or note, named two senders (`senderContactId @default(auth().contactId)` beside the hook's senderUserId) and was a 400 from the @@arc. Merge site: [litestone.ts](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) `freezeUser({ ...user, ...claims })`. D576 exempts a stamp because a stamp is the caller's own; a merged principal's claim is a different standing's. Nothing states a precedence (FJS-1450's amend: no array states one). The app refuses both on one request (400) and on one socket (close 4001) with a hand-written wrapper that has to carry bearerClaim's `describe` through, or the socket's per-frame header is no longer admitted · [chatwoot api/src/core/visitor.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/core/visitor.ts) · pinned [notes.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/notes.test.ts) · [FJS-1450](#fjs-1450) · `FJS-D576` · `FJS-D343` · blocked by `FJS-D664` |

| <a id="fjs-1799"></a>FJS-1799 | oracle | **Oracle's container lets any member write it, and its membership delegates every write to that, so any member adds anyone, removes anyone and re-roles anyone, themselves to admin included** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, membership phase, by emitting test/fixtures/hiring.json: Company is @@gate("4.8.4.8") with @@allow('update', memberships.some(userId == auth().id)), and Membership's create, update and delete are each check(company, 'update') under @@gate("4.4.4.4") with no write guard on role. So an interviewer can make themselves recruiter, add a stranger, or remove the recruiter. The reference the CHANGES entry names (2026-10-06, 'Organization is the reference's shape') says otherwise on both counts: references/Organization.lite is @@gate("4.8.5.8") ('an administrator renames it') and guards Member.role with @allow('write', …admin), 'the one line every instance got right'. The emitter writes 4.8.4.8 from accessFor's m.members branch, which pushes the member test onto update and leaves the rung at 4. The guard the reference writes reads auth().memberRole, which a multi-org app cannot resolve (see the references row filed beside this one), so the fix wants a spelling the emitter can use: the container's update as members.some(userId == auth().id && role == <admin>), which keeps the delegation and makes it mean admin. quo closes it by hand that way · [emit.js](packages/oracle/src/emit.js) · [CHANGES.md](docs/changes-archive/oracle.md) · [Organization.lite](packages/litestone/references/Organization.lite) · [quo schema.lite § Who belongs](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/schema.lite) · The references row is [FJS-1800](#fjs-1800) · blocked by `FJS-D623` |
| <a id="fjs-1743"></a>FJS-1743 | junction | **A room is not bound to the row it is for. Nothing grades a channel join, so admission is the app's own call, graded once, and a reader whose grant is revoked stays in the room: presence frames and channel.send reach them in both directions until they close the tab** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured in the notion stressor, PLAN § Q4 ([probes/q4/run.ts](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q4/run.ts), [out.txt](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q4/out.txt)), five junction clients over real sockets. A client cannot ask to join: `subscribe` only publishes meta for a channel the server already joined it to (transport/channels.ts, *Server owns channel membership*), so the app's `pages.enter` reads the page AS THE CALLER and joins on a hit (a guest's enter of an unreadable page is a 404, nothing joins). That join is the last time anything asks. The owner revokes gus's share on D while gus is in `page:D`: the room's roster keeps him on every screen, olivia's cursor reaches both his tabs and his reaches her, and one fragment sent with `channel(room).send` reaches him. A comment created on D in the same second does NOT reach him, because a service frame is graded per recipient. So the leak is exactly the two ungraded paths, presence and a raw send. The same holds for a page moved out from under an inherited grant (gus and wren both stay). The app's fix (`NOTION_Q4_EVICT=1`: the share and move services take each person who lost a page out of its room) works for presence and the fragment, but it restates access in a hook (Invariant 6), sees only person grants (the copy), runs before the transaction commits, and the evicted person is told nothing: a leave sends no frame to whoever left, so gus's own screen still shows five people in a room he has left, and his own cursor announcements are dropped silently. The room half of [FJS-1738](#fjs-1738). It is also why [4.20](IDEAS/html-over-the-wire.md)'s *uniform* fragment channel is unsafe today, since a uniform channel is only as safe as its membership · blocked by `FJS-D596` |
| <a id="fjs-1738"></a>FJS-1738 | junction · litestone | **A broadcast is graded against the row that was WRITTEN, so a write that changes who may read OTHER rows reaches no live store: revoking a Share leaves the page and its blocks on the revoked reader's screen, and granting one never shows it to the grantee. Nothing in the framework knows which rows a policy's relation (shares.some, check(page)) makes depend on the written one** | open | 2026-10-08 | notion Phase 4, PLAN § Q2 (fjs-prototypes/notion probes/q2/run.ts, out.txt run 1). The real app over the small seed, three junction clients over real sockets (olivia W1 owner, gus W1 guest with read on page D, max W1 member), each holding resource('pages').load() (the sidebar), pages.record(D) (the open page) and resource('blocks').load({pageId: D}); the app announces Page/Block/Share on workspace:<id> with channels({ claims }) read per frame (FJS-D191, FJS-D472), so every frame is graded per recipient. Page read is `shares.some(userId == auth().id)` (FJS-D566, one hop); Block read is `check(page, 'read')`. REVOKE, owner shares.remove(gus's share) over the socket: gus's sidebar holds D, the record holds D's row, the blocks store holds 3, while his own re-read is 404/0/0. The one frame he gets is 'shares removed <id>', which no page or block store listens to. GRANT back, shares.create: gus receives 'shares created', and the sidebar, record and blocks stay empty while a re-read shows 1 page and 3 blocks. FJS-1425's fix (an update that narrows the WRITTEN row sends 'removed') is correct here and does not reach this: D was never written. A later write to D does heal it (graded, 'pages removed'). The same shape at every relation a policy follows: a Member row deleted (guest off the roster) leaves every row it admitted; a Block's check(page) means a page revoke strands its blocks too. Under Notion's rule (a grant flows down the tree, PLAN § Q1 spelling A) one revoke strands a whole subtree: amended with that measurement. Not a store bug: a store can only act on frames, and no frame names the rows. The honest answers are a regrade the Data boundary drives (the policy graph knows Share → Page → Block), or a 'changed' on the dependent models' channels when a row a policy reads is written. Neither exists. · Under Notion's rule (PLAN § Q1 spelling A, adopted in the app for person grants: PageAccess copy, maintained in the shares/pages services' transaction), measured over the same sockets. Small tree: revoke on D → gus holds 6 of 6 pages a reload refuses; grant back → 0 of 6; move C (2) out → C 'removed', 1 under it stale; move M (8) in → M 'patched', 7 under it missing. --demo tree (2,000 pages): revoke 11 of 11 stale; move the 187-page fixture in → 1 arrives, 186 missing. A writes N COPY rows (PageAccess, @@gate 8, no service) and 0 rows of any model a store holds, so PLAN's guess that A 'broadcasts N frames' is wrong: it broadcasts none. The app's only working regrade is a pages.patch through the SERVICE per affected page (arm 'service'): sidebar and record right in every step at N frames per recipient (11, 11, 5, 23, 187), the 187-page move 342 ms vs 214, each frame an audited no-op update, and the window of 100 counts 98 on stale. BLOCKS stay wrong in every arm (3 held after the revoke, 0 of 3 after the grant, 0 of 9 after the big move): no Block is written and its read is check(page). Writing the pages with asSystem() instead reaches no socket (FJS-1741). After a reconnect the lists heal and the record does not (FJS-1740). · The room half, notion PLAN § Q4: [FJS-1743](#fjs-1743). A revoke also leaves the reader in the page's presence room, where nothing is graded: cursors in both directions and a `channel.send` fragment keep reaching him. A graded comment frame stops at once, so per-frame grading is the half that works · blocked by `FJS-D597` · Seen again 2026-10-08, chatwoot stressor Phase 2: a contact merge. The host identifies the visitor on her phone as the user who chatted from her laptop on Monday; the phone's grant is re-pointed at the surviving contact (VisitorGrant, @@gate("8"), no service). Over HTTP the phone now lists Monday's thread; its open socket was sent nothing about it, because no row of that thread was written, and the next agent reply on it arrives for a thread the phone's store never held. The rows the merge DID write (the phone's own thread, re-pointed) arrive as 'conversations updated'. Pinned in widget.test.ts · [widget.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/widget.test.ts) · [contacts/merge.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/contacts/merge.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1725"></a>FJS-1725 | litestone | **A grant inherited down a self-relation has to be a copy (access-control.md: 'a column every row carries'), and no hook the framework offers hears every write with its rows, so the copy goes stale and fails OPEN: a revoke made with share.deleteMany leaves the guest reading every page it covered** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05 in the notion stressor (PLAN Q1, spelling A, 2,000 pages to depth 10): with the copy kept by the service that writes, every read is right (5 readers, sidebar/page/blocks/search, $readAs and findMany agree on 2,000 pages and 2,036 blocks, no AUTOMATIC index) and a 187-page move writes 1 Page row + 374 PageAccess rows in ~190 ms. Kept by a Plugin's onAfterWrite: update/create/delete arrive with the row; updateMany, deleteMany and createMany arrive as nothing (result null, no where), leaving 374, 11 and 40 copy rows stale; a throw in the hook leaves the write committed without its copy. $tapEvents hears all four but names no row for a collection write (where + count only; createMany neither) and fires a tick after commit, outside the transaction. So a complete copy is a full recompute on every bulk write (8 ms here, the whole tenant) with a stale window, or a convention every writer keeps. Not a ruling: Q1's B–D are parked, and whether the framework owns a tree grant waits for them · fjs-prototypes/notion/PLAN.md § Q1 · fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q1/A/out.txt · [plugin.js](packages/litestone/src/core/plugin.js) · blocked by `FJS-D588` · Phase 4 (notion, over real sockets): the copy's WRITE side is also invisible to every live store. A share or a move rewrites N PageAccess rows and announces only the row the caller wrote (the Share, or the moved Page), so a revoke leaves 11 of 11 pages on the guest's screen and a 187-page move shows 1 of 187 (FJS-1738). Adopted in the app as a service-transaction maintainer (api/src/core/access.ts); the generated migration created the table and nothing else, so an existing database lost every grant until a hand-written recursive-CTE backfill (agrees with the JS maintainer: 0 rows apart on 2,000 pages). The seed's createMany needed an explicit rebuild, as Phase 2 measured. |
| <a id="fjs-1660"></a>FJS-1660 | litestone | **An @@export CSV writes a text cell starting with = + - @ as is, so a spreadsheet runs landed or user data as a formula** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen by Transit Phase 3. export.js csvCell() quotes by content only: a value =HYPERLINK("https://x","Refund") or =cmd\|' /C calc'!A0 reaches the file bare, and Excel, Numbers and Sheets evaluate it when a staff member opens the extract (CWE-1236, OWASP CSV injection). An @@export is taken at the caller's standing over rows other people wrote, which is the case the guard exists for. An Event created 10 ms into a 100 ms finalize transaction on the same client waited on the FIFO lock, committed after it, and was not read by it; its receivedAt was ~90 ms EARLIER than the finalizedAt the transaction stamped. So 'events received after the invoice was issued' never finds it and it is never billed. Cross-process adds the busy wait. FJS-D152 moved the clock into the client for testability, which is right; this is when it is read. @sequence is the counter that does order by commit (bumped inside the write; a refused duplicate leaves no gap), and lago uses Event.seq as the mark. Wanted: take generated stamps after the lock is held, or say in docs/concurrency.md that now() is not commit order and point at @sequence. Isolation holds: 40 MB to a sleeping reader left a healthy reader at 200/200. **Owner ruled 2026-10-09: delete the 1013 claim** (the code, the client branch keyed on it and any doc naming it) rather than send an overloaded frame first, because a full buffer cannot carry that frame either · [send-queue.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/send-queue.ts) · [client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [audit § 3.7](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |
| <a id="fjs-2142"></a>FJS-2142 | toolbelt | **toolbelt/units has no integer divide-and-round, so an exact rate × quantity past 2^53 hand-rolls its own BigInt rounding, and the float path roundMinor is built for loses half-cent ties: 9,780 calls at $0.00025 bills $2.44, not $2.45** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor. units at @scale(6) × a rate at @scale(9) sits at 15 places; 1,234,567 calls at $0.00025 is 308,641,750,000,000,000, past 2^53. `roundMinor(value)` takes a Number with a fraction, so it cannot be handed a BigInt numerator and a power-of-ten divisor; lago's exact.ts writes `divRound(n, d, mode)` itself. The float alternative, `roundMinor(units * rate * 100)`, disagreed with the exact result on 134 of 2,000,005 swept inputs (7 rates × whole-call units to 2M), every one an exact half-cent tie where the float lands at …4999999: `roundMinor(9780 * 0.00025 * 100)` is 244. Lago computes in BigDecimal and gets 245. Wanted: `divRound(numerator: bigint, divisor: bigint, { mode })` (or `roundMinor` accepting a bigint and a scale) beside allocate, ruled under FJS-D154 · [exact.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/domain/billing/exact.ts) · [billing.test.ts § a half cent](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/billing.test.ts) · [units.js roundMinor](packages/toolbelt/src/units/units.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2141"></a>FJS-2141 | litestone | **@gte/@lte/@gt/@lt on a @scale column bound the STORED integer and nothing says so, so @scale(4) @unit("%") @lte(100) caps a percentage at 0.01%** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor: `percentageRate Int? @scale(4) @unit("%") @gt(0) @lte(100)` refused a 10% coupon (stored 100000) with *percentageRate: must be at most 100*. The bound compares minor units, where a reader of the line reads 100%. Neither `fli db:explain @lte` (example `pct Int @lte(100)`) nor docs/exact-numbers.md says which side of the point a bound is on. These bounds also emit no table CHECK: changing them needed no migration, so a raw write or a migration is not held to them. Wanted: read a bound on a scaled column in declared units (`@lte(100)` means 100.0000), or refuse a bound whose reading is ambiguous and name the stored figure · [lago schema.lite § Coupon](../fjs-prototypes/lago/db/schema.lite) |
| <a id="fjs-2140"></a>FJS-2140 | litestone | **Changing a column's @scale writes no rescale and no warning: a table rebuild copies every value verbatim (worth 1000x after @scale(9) → @scale(6)), and where the DDL is unchanged the migration has no statement at all** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor, moving `units` from `@scale(9)` to `@scale(6)` on Fee and ChargeTier. Every stored value now means a thousand times more. docs/exact-numbers.md says *changing n is a migration that rescales every stored row, and nothing here does it for you*; the migrator is the one tool that sees the old and new n and stays silent, while it does warn about a dropped table on the same run. Wanted: refuse a scale change without an operation that says what to do with the rows (`--operations` already carries row-keeping steps), or emit the multiply/divide. The tables were empty here · [migration 20261009153917](../fjs-prototypes/lago/db/migrations/main/20261009153917_migration.sql) · [exact-numbers.md](packages/litestone/docs/exact-numbers.md) |

| <a id="fjs-2118"></a>FJS-2118 | caravan | **dispatch({ unique }) is a no-op while the keyed job is RUNNING, so a recompute asked for after the running one read its inputs is dropped and the newest change never reaches the row** | open | 2026-10-09 | Found porting ELA's crew budget (stressor #24): crew-budget is dispatched under crew-budget:<account>:<crew>:<day> by each jobs sync that changes a job on that day. A sync landing while the previous recompute is mid-run (inputs already read) dispatches into the live key and gets the running job's id back, so the day keeps the budget of the jobs as they were. The partial index jobs_unique_live covers status IN ('pending','running') · [db.ts](packages/caravan/src/db.ts) · [index.ts](packages/caravan/src/index.ts) dispatch dedup · README 'unique is a lock on work in flight'. What a recompute wants is coalesce-while-pending: a dispatch into a PENDING key is absorbed, one into a RUNNING key queues exactly one follow-up. The app's only outs are dropping unique (every change a run) or a time-bucketed key; neither is the framework saying it. Pinned in ela api/src/jobs/crew-budget.job.ts, not worked around. |

| <a id="fjs-2102"></a>FJS-2102 | notifications | **A registered driver's send() answers nothing, so what the vendor said about a delivery — its message id, and whether a failed send may still have gone out — can reach the caller only as a thrown error dug out of NotificationDeliveryError.failures** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09 in sstime Phase 5 (the work-available text, Q6). NotificationDriver.send is Promise<void> (packages/notifications/types.ts:80) and notify() returns void, so an sms driver over Telnyx cannot hand back the message id Telnyx answers — a later delivery receipt has nothing to match — and the three outcomes a text has (sent; queued, nothing reached the vendor so a retry is safe; failed, refused or possibly sent so a retry may text twice) can only travel as an app-defined error class the caller finds in failures[].error. sstime's providers/telnyx/sms.ts does exactly that (TextNotSent, textOutcome()). The README's Custom drivers section shows a driver that POSTs and never says what to do with the answer. A driver returning a receipt (unknown, carried on notify()'s result per transport) would hold it · [sms.ts](../fjs-prototypes/sstime/api/src/providers/telnyx/sms.ts) · Second half, same drive: notify() console.errors every failed transport with the whole error before it throws (packages/notifications/notify.ts:124-133), so a caller that handles the outcome — a landline Telnyx refuses, an account with no number — still prints an error and a code frame per text: a 30-person batch with three landlines is three stack traces for an expected answer. The line names the recipient by id ?? email ?? '(unaddressed)', so a recipient a driver addresses by phone is logged as unaddressed though it carries one |

| <a id="fjs-2072"></a>FJS-2072 | css · sierra | **A widget's type scale follows the HOST page's root font size: every kit rem resolves against the host's html, so on a host with html { font-size: 62.5% } a widget's text and controls shrink to ~10px** | open | 2026-10-08 | Found by stressor ela, Phase 5 (the public widgets), 2026-10-08. | <a id="fjs-2068"></a>FJS-2068 | cli | **`fli make:desktop` writes `tauri = "2"` and no Cargo.lock, so the first build resolves whatever Tauri is newest — on 2026-10-08 that was 2.12, which needs rustc 1.90, and a 1.89 toolchain refuses before compiling anything** | open | 2026-10-08 | Found scaffolding `packages/basecamp/desktop/`: `cargo build` failed with *tauri@2.12.1 requires rustc 1.90* on the stable 1.89 the machine had. `example/desktop/` builds because its committed Cargo.lock pins 2.11.5, and copying that lock into basecamp's shell was the workaround. | <a id="fjs-2052"></a>FJS-2052 | litestone | **test:mutate kills no @@allow drop in the membership shape oracle emits — members.some(userId == auth().id) on the container and check(org) on everything under it survive deletion 0 of 5, where an own-row policy is killed 2 of 2** | open | 2026-10-09 | Found 2026-10-08, lago stressor (#7) Phase 0. fli test:mutate on fjs-prototypes/lago/db/schema.lite (Organization + Member in quo's shape, every model under it gated by check(org) / check(org, 'update')): 208 mutants, and all 39 allow-drop mutants SURVIVED — every row policy in the app can be deleted and the derived checks (verifyGateLadder, verifyConstraints, verifyFieldProtection, verifyRowPolicies) see nothing. The run's footer says two survivor kinds are expected (nullable @unique, create-only @@allow); these are read and update rules. Control, one script calling mutationScore({ kinds: ['allow-drop'] }) with createTestEnv on two minimal schemas: (A) Note with @@allow('read'\|'update', userId == auth().id) — graded 2, killed 2; (B) Org with members.some(...) read/update rules, Member with userId == auth().id \|\| check(org), Note with check(org) / check(org, 'update') — graded 5, killed 0, including Org's own members.some rules, which delegate to nothing. So it is the relation-valued predicate, not only check() delegation. FJS-513 (closed) fixed 'a delegated model reported as skipped'; this is the same blind spot reached by a to-many .some(). Cost: the shape @frontierjs/oracle emits for every container (FJS-D600/601), quo and lago both use, is the one whose access rules the mutation tool cannot grade, and nothing in the output says the rules were never exercised. · **Phase 1 control, 2026-10-09, lago stressor: the same schema, graded both ways.** `fli test:mutate --kinds allow-drop` over the Phase 1 schema (22 Lago models, all in the membership shape): 59 mutants, 3 killed, 56 survived, 430 s. A hand-written drive on real members of two organizations (lago-access.test.ts, 64 tests: read, create, update, delete, transitions, and `<parent>.orgId == orgId` ties) run as the mutation target by mutate-access.ts: 63 mutants (allow-drop and tie-drop), 62 killed, 0 survived, 1 invalid, 15 s. The first pass of that control left 4 survivors, each a case the drive had not written (three post-update ties, an applied coupon's terminate), which is the control doing its job · [mutate-access.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/mutate-access.ts) · [lago-access.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/lago-access.test.ts) · 2026-10-09, lago Phase 2: the drive grew a bearer standing (an API key's holder, a STRANGER carrying apiOrgId) for Event's create-at-0 and BillableMetric's read-at-0; the control re-run: 64 mutants, 63 killed, 0 survived. The derived checks have no principal to stand for a claim-only caller, so neither rule is in their reach at all · [lago-access.test.ts § an API key's holder](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/lago-access.test.ts) · 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 3: the app's control had weakened a policy only by cutting it after its first &&, one mutant per policy. One mutant per top-level conjunct found three holes Phase 1's drive had all along (Charge's metric.orgId, Subscription's plan.orgId, AppliedCoupon's coupon.orgId: each swap test moved only one of two parents) and one implied conjunct (CreditNote's customer.orgId, implied by invoice.customerId == customerId), now 81 of 81 killed. An operator for fli test:mutate: drop each conjunct alone · [mutate-access.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/mutate-access.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2050"></a>FJS-2050 | litestone | **fli explain @immutable describes only the non-sealing meaning — 'written once, at create, and never again', readOnly in the update schema — and says nothing of FJS-D167, where on a sealing model it means frozen at the seal** | open | 2026-10-08 | Found 2026-10-08, lago stressor (#7) Phase 0, by following the discovery skill's 'read the language, never your memory' rule. The entry (packages/litestone/src/core/catalog.js:505) says a column is written once at create and reaches the client as readOnly in the UPDATE schema; its see-also is 'system guarded version' and names neither sealed nor transitions. FJS-D167 rules that on a model with a @seals move the same word means frozen AT THE SEAL, moves the refusal into the WHERE, drops readOnly for x-litestone-kind 'immutable-until-seal', and survives asSystem(). Measured on a scratch schema (draft -> finalized @seals @system, total @system @immutable): asSystem() updated total in draft (ok), the move ran, the update after it was refused ('it is at finalized, which is sealed'); on a non-sealing model the same update was refused at create's rule. So the behavior is right and the description is half of it. Cost in the run: the draft schema marked Lago's long-lived draft invoice totals as 'WANTED: frozen after a STATE' and wrote them @system without @immutable, i.e. editable after finalize — the schema the explain text produced was the weaker one. Neighbors: FJS-1482 (explain has no entry for @seals), FJS-1512. |
| <a id="fjs-2049"></a>FJS-2049 | litestone | **A per-unit price finer than its currency's minor unit has no spelling that names its currency — @money is whole minor units, @money with @scale is refused, and @scale alone carries no currency** | open | 2026-10-09 | Found 2026-10-08, lago stressor (#7) Phase 0, before any app code. Lago and Stripe both price usage below a cent: $0.00025 per API call is a fortieth of a cent, and Stripe's unit_amount_decimal carries 12 places. Measured at parse (litestone parser.js, one field per probe): @money(USD) @scale(9) and @money(field: currency) @scale(6) are refused ('@money derives the scale from the currency, so state one'); @scale(12) and @scale(10) are refused (max 9); @scale(9) @big and @money(USD) @big are refused. @scale(9) alone parses, so fjs-prototypes/lago/db/schema.lite ChargeTier.perUnit and Fee.preciseAmount are bare Int @scale(9) in MAJOR units: the column says how exact it is and nothing says which currency, so formatMoney, x-money and the money-rendered-raw check all lose it. FJS-575 (closed) measured the same Lago Fee columns and deferred this as 'a separate ruling' because candidate C did not need it; a metered billing app is the caller that does. A second limit rides on it: at @scale(9) in major units a column tops out near 9,007,199.25 (2^53), so Lago's preciseAmountCents numeric(40,15) for an accumulated fee has no exact home either. Owed: a ruling on whether money can carry extra places (e.g. @money(USD, places: 9) as minor units plus n) or whether a rate is never money. · **Phase 1, 2026-10-09, lago stressor: the units are the binding ceiling, and FJS-575's premise does not hold in Lago's source.** (1) `Fee.units` at `@scale(9)` holds 9,007,199 units, which an API-call meter passes in a month; moved to `@scale(6)` (9,007,199,254). (2) units × perUnit sits at 15 places: 1,234,567 calls at $0.00025 is 308,641,750,000,000,000, past 2^53, so every intermediate is a BigInt and the toolbelt has no BigInt rounding (filed separately). (3) FJS-575 concluded a precise total is an un-rounded intermediate, never stored. Lago stores `precise_amount_cents numeric(40,15)` and READS it back: the minimum-commitment true-up sums it across the period's fees (commitments/minimum/calculate_true_up_fee_service.rb:43-64), credit-note proration divides by it (credit_note_item.rb:44), and `taxes_precise_amount_cents` weights the invoice tax split (invoice.rb:669). So a running total of precise amounts exists, and at nine places it tops out near 9,007,199 of the currency · [billing.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/billing.test.ts) · [rating.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/domain/billing/rating.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-1995"></a>FJS-1995 | junction | **What a @@transitions move owes beyond itself has an owner only when a commitment fires it: the same move made by a service, a receiver or a system client is each caller's to follow up, and one that forgets leaves the row's dependents wrong with nothing saying so** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-08, chatwoot stressor Q4 (FJS-D368 scopes commitments({ hooks }) to the fire). A conversation's reopen must resume its paused SLA clock. | <a id="fjs-1955"></a>FJS-1955 | junction | **bearerClaim has no socket half: a header() bearer's broadcasts are graded with no claim unless the app hand-writes channels({ claims }) repeating bearerClaim's lookup and liveness, the token then rides the upgrade URL that FJS-D486 moved the session out of, and a revoked grant's open socket has no disconnectSession** | open | 2026-10-08 | Measured 2026-10-08, chatwoot stressor Phase 2: a chat widget on the host's origin, so the grant is a header (no cookie crosses, FJS-1503's cookie() form is out). A service call over the socket works: bearerClaim reads the per-frame meta.headers its describe() admits. A broadcast has no frame, the visitor's connection is principal null, and every 'messages' frame to them was refused until the app wrote channels({ claims }) re-reading the grant by digest on every frame (that per-frame read is what makes a merge's re-pointed grant reach the open socket on its next frame, pinned). The token for it has to be on the upgrade: a browser sets no header there, and the subprotocol slot D486 gave the session is read by extractToken as a session, so the visitor's rides /ws?visitor=<token>. Revoking a grant (an agent's merge) left its socket open and silent until the app walked app.channels.connections for a digest it stashed at connection; disconnectSession matches conn.user.sessionId and a bearer's conn.user is null. About 90 lines, three copies of bearerClaim's liveness rule · [core/visitor.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/core/visitor.ts) · [widget.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/widget.test.ts) · FJS-D486 · FJS-D548 · [FJS-1503](#fjs-1503) · **The browser half**, 2026-10-08, chatwoot Phase 3: junction's browser client opens its socket at a fixed `/ws` with the SESSION in a subprotocol (`client/index.ts` _connect), so a widget whose only credential is a grant cannot use it and hand-writes fetch + WebSocket + reconnect + ping/pong (~100 lines, [chatwoot widgets/src/lib/desk.js](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/widgets/src/lib/desk.js)). Measured working on a host page on another SITE (127.0.0.1 vs the app's localhost): the reply arrives live and a note never reaches the shadow DOM |

| <a id="fjs-1936"></a>FJS-1936 | litestone · cli | **A schema createClient refuses at boot passes litestone advise, migrate create, migrate apply and fli check: a policy naming an undeclared claim under @@auth** | open | 2026-10-07 | Measured 2026-10-07, stressor #5 (fjs-prototypes/chatwoot). The schema has @@auth User (auth's scaffold), so the claim set is active, and Contact carries @@allow('read', check(org) \|\| id == auth().contactId) with no top-level `claim contactId`. buildPolicyMap -> checkExpr (litestone/src/core/policy.js:355) throws 'contactId is not a claim the principal carries', but only inside createClient. Every tool that grades a schema without building a client said nothing: `litestone advise` (13 items, none about the claim), `litestone migrate create` (wrote both migrations), `litestone migrate apply` (applied them), `fli check` (37 rules, 3 unrelated warnings). The first tool to refuse was `fli test:mutate`, which builds the client. So a migration is written and applied for a schema the app cannot boot with, and a scaffold with @@auth makes every new policy claim the case. The fix is for advise (the 'what this schema says wrong' surface) to run the same claim walk the client does; whether migrate should refuse too is the question. |

| <a id="fjs-1879"></a>FJS-1879 | litestone | **@phone refuses letters and keeps every other spelling as typed: '-------', '(((((((' and '+0000000' are phone numbers, '(555) 123-4567' and '+15551234567' are two values, and a @@unique or a lookup keyed by a number is unique per spelling** | open | 2026-10-07 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 5 (Q8). The validator is /^\+?[\d\s\-().]{7,20}$/ (core/validate.js), docs/schema.md describes it as 'E.164 + common formats', and the transforms that run before validation are @trim @lower @upper @slug, none of which normalizes a number. Contact @@unique([orgId, e164]) holds one person twice, as '+15551234567' and '(555) 123-4567'. A teammate's text to the typed spelling opens a second Conversation beside the one the carrier opened, because the carrier only ever sends E.164, and the carrier then refuses the send (40310). The opt-out lookup is string equality, so another spelling of a number that texted STOP is not refused by the app ([FJS-1878](#fjs-1878)). Here the carrier caught it as malformed; a carrier that normalized would apply its own STOP list, and a revocation the carrier never parsed would be enforced by nobody. Wants @phone to normalize to E.164 in the transform tier, with a default region (per field or per app) so a national number can be read, and to refuse what it cannot normalize. As-typed storage can stay as an explicit opt-out. Every rule keyed by a number (thread, contact, opt-out, caller ID) depends on that · [validate.js](packages/litestone/src/core/validate.js) · [docs/schema.md](packages/litestone/docs/schema.md) · [consent.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/consent.test.ts) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1878"></a>FJS-1878 | litestone | **Consent to contact is a statement about the data, and the one word that can reach across a relation to say it, @@deny, belongs to the caller tier: asSystem() drops it, and every carrier send, automation and job writes as the system** | open | 2026-10-07 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 5 (Q7). A number that texted STOP may not be texted. Today that is quo's code: sendText looks for an OptOut row before it writes the outbound Message. Declared on a schema of its own, @@deny('create', direction == 'outbound' && conversation.optedOutAt != null, 'They texted STOP.') parses, reaches one hop through a to-one, refuses a member, and lets an inbound row through. asSystem() writes the same row with no refusal. In this app nobody else ever writes a Message: the browser cannot create one, a carrier webhook is a system write, and a missed-call text-back or a scheduled text is a job with no caller. FJS-D346 and FJS-D347 already name the other tier, rules graded under asSystem() because they are about the DATA (@@check, @@arc, @@exclude, @minItems). Its only open-ended word, @@check, is single-row SQL, and SQLite refuses a subquery in a CHECK. So the rule can be written, and only in the tier that skips the writer that sends. The scaffolded AGENTS.md says the schema guards every caller, including a job, a seed and a migration. For @@allow and @@deny that holds only for a job that runs on a principal's client. IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md covers what an app holds, not whom it may contact. A revocation the carrier never parses (please stop texting me) is one only the app can hold, and the FCC's revocation rule requires honouring it. Wants an expression form of the data tier (an @@check that takes a litestone predicate across a to-one, graded at write like @@exclude), or a ruling that consent stays app code. The rule is also only as good as its key, which is a phone number nobody normalizes ([FJS-1879](#fjs-1879)) · [docs/access-control.md](packages/litestone/docs/access-control.md) · [inbox.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/domain/inbox.ts) · [consent.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/consent.test.ts) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1877"></a>FJS-1877 | conduit | **A target whose idempotency key is a BODY field cannot say so: the caller has to claim replayable instead of the key, conduit's own key goes in a header the target never reads, and a result never says it was a replay, so a collapsed duplicate's refusal reads as the first attempt failing** | open | 2026-10-07 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 5 (Q5). Telnyx Call Control collapses a repeated command_id, which is a field in the JSON body; the docs say a duplicate is ignored and do not say what it answers. TargetDescriptor.idempotency takes { header, auto } and nothing else, so quo's connector sends command_id in the body with replayable: true. That claim is false for a dial: repeating it without the key places a second call. On a sink that places the leg and loses the response (a 500), conduit replays and the carrier collapses the replay, so there is one leg. The app is answered with the replay's 422 90046 (Duplicate command_id) and gets no call_control_id. ResponseMeta has no attempt count, and onRetry is conduit-wide, not per send, so the connector cannot tell that refusal from a first-attempt one. In the ring group this rings a device the app holds no CallLeg for: the caller goes to voicemail, the answer is never bridged or hung up, and its call.answered webhook is refused 500 on every delivery. Sent instead under idempotency_key, the key goes out as an Idempotency-Key header, and two lost responses become three legs. Registering idempotency: { body: 'command_id' } is refused by name. Mutation control: removing the body key fails exactly the one-leg test. Wants the key's location to be a target fact like its header name (e.g. idempotency: { field: 'command_id' }), so the claim made is the true one and auto can mint it, plus a replay count on ResponseMeta. The window is a target fact too (Telnyx: about 60s, unverified): conduit's 45s deadline stays inside it, but a job that retries one layer up can outlive it · [http.ts](packages/conduit/src/transports/http.ts) · [conduit.ts](packages/conduit/src/conduit.ts) · [telnyx/index.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/providers/telnyx/index.ts) · [idempotency.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/idempotency.test.ts) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · Corrected on the real carrier 2026-10-07 (quo Phase 6): Telnyx answers a repeated command_id with the FIRST dial's answer, call_control_id included, and places nothing — a refused dial's 403 replayed as the same 403 and id, a placed ring dial replayed 21s later as a 202 with the leg's original body. So the 422 90046, the lost id and the stranded ring leg were the sink's, not the carrier's: conduit's replay hands the app the id, and both PINNED losses now pass. What stands: the key's location is a target fact conduit cannot state (replayable: true is still the claim made, and is false for a dial without the key), and a replay count on ResponseMeta. Less urgent than filed, since the body key works once sent · [idempotency.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/idempotency.test.ts) · [sink.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/providers/telnyx/sink.ts) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) |

| <a id="fjs-1829"></a>FJS-1829 | junction | **app.ws is the browser socket's door: every route is sent junction's own connected and error frames, shares the browser's frame limits, cannot refuse an upgrade, and has no apiPrefix and no per-socket slot** | open | 2026-10-07 | Found 2026-10-07 in the quo stressor, Phase 4: the one door a vendor-dialled socket has, and the peer speaks its own protocol. After open, wsSend(ws, {type:'connected'}) goes down EVERY app.ws route — pinned in agent.test.ts, the carrier's socket receives it on every stream (Telnyx's client frames are media, mark and clear; the sink answers malformed_frame, its guess at Telnyx). A rate or in-flight refusal sends {type:'error'} and DROPS the frame — on a media stream, a gap in the call's audio. The limits are app-wide (config.http.ws: 100 frames/s with a 2x burst, 256 sockets per IP; a carrier dials every call's stream from a few media-server addresses), with no per-route setting. A route cannot answer the upgrade: a ticket in the path that names nothing is upgraded and must be closed after. app.ws takes the whole path where app.post applies apiPrefix. ws.data has no app slot, so per-socket state is a WeakMap on ctx.$ws, which types.ts says never to touch outside transport code. A throw in a handler is swallowed with no log (try { await handlers.message } catch {}). Wants app.ws(path, handlers, { protocol: 'raw', limits, upgrade(req) }) · [transport/http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) · [transport/types.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/types.ts) · [agent.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/domain/agent.ts) · [agent.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/agent.test.ts) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · blocked by `FJS-D688` |

| <a id="fjs-1810"></a>FJS-1810 | litestone | **A view can declare neither a key nor a relation, so a UNION view — the natural spelling of one ordered read over two models — can be neither keyset-paged nor guarded by its parent's policy** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 2 (PLAN Q11): a thread is Message + Call in one time order, paged by cursor. `view TimelineItem { … @@sql("… FROM message UNION ALL … FROM call") }` parses, but (1) a view field takes no attribute (`id String @unique` is 'Expected IDENT, got @'), so the view has no key, and findManyCursor refuses `orderBy [{at:desc},{id:desc}]` as 'not a total order … declares no unique column'. Offset is the only paging left, and a row arriving between two pages repeats the first page's last row (pinned, then deleted with the design). (2) a view cannot declare `conversation Conversation @relation(…)`, so its read cannot be `check(conversation)` — the rule every row in a thread delegates to. It was gated 8 and the service re-proved the thread on the caller's client before an asSystem() read: the policy restated in code. Both other spellings had a cost the view would not: a header table with @@arc (every write path owes a header row in a transaction, and a missed one is a row missing from the thread with nothing saying so) and two keyset scans merged in the service (~40 lines and a two-part cursor, minted with table.cursorFor, which is not in index.d.ts). With a key and a relation on a view, the view is the answer: SQLite merges, litestone pages, the policy delegates · [PLAN § Phase 2 — Q11](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · [conversations.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/services/conversations.service.ts) · [timeline.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/timeline.test.ts) · [IDEAS/shipped/polymorphic-relations.md](IDEAS/shipped/polymorphic-relations.md) |

| <a id="fjs-1806"></a>FJS-1806 | conduit · caravan | **A webhook naming a subject that does not exist yet can only be parked by the app, and nothing ends a park that will never resolve: a send whose response was lost leaves its receipts parked forever, and the text marked failed while the carrier delivered it** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 1 (PLAN Q3). The same unknown id means WAIT on Telnyx, where the app is the only sender and the id is a send in flight, and CREATE on Quo, where teammates also text from Quo's own apps (both pinned in quo's tests). So the receiver cannot decide to park; it can hold the state (an unapplied record, see FJS-1804's outcomes) and offer a re-drive, which are both app code in quo today (WebhookEvent.appliedAt null, applyParked). The open half: a park is a bet that the subject is coming. When the send's response is lost (the Telnyx sink's loseResponse knob) it never comes. Ending a park needs a clock (an expiry, then a dead letter or a reconcile against the carrier), which is the same unowned, cancellable timer as quo's Q12. Pinned by telnyx.test.ts 'a send whose response is lost leaves its receipts parked forever (the gap)', which flips when a park can expire · [FJS-1804](#fjs-1804) · [PLAN Q3](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · [quo inbox.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/domain/inbox.ts) · [telnyx.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/telnyx.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1805"></a>FJS-1805 | conduit | **A receiver that records a webhook before applying it loses the event when the apply throws: the vendor's retry is answered as a duplicate. The acceptance test inbound-integrations § Order names for the receiver applies nothing, so it cannot see this** | open | 2026-10-07 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 1 (PLAN Q1). Phase 0's hand-built Telnyx route wrote its WebhookEvent row and then applied; an apply that threw left the row behind, and the retry deduped on it, so the event was lost while every delivery was answered. Dedupe has to mean APPLIED, not SEEN: quo now deletes the record when the apply throws and answers 500 so the vendor retries, pinned by quo.test.ts § the receiver and mutation-tested. A second layer the receiver cannot provide at all: one fact can arrive as two DELIVERIES (two Quo subscriptions on one workspace both carry a text, each with its own webhook-id), so exactly-once is the subject's unique key (Message.providerId), not the receiver's dedupe, also mutation-tested. § Order step 5 names verify:pay over example's Stripe route as the acceptance test for B. That route applies nothing past verify, so it grades the four refusals and a redelivery and can catch neither of these · [FJS-1804](#fjs-1804) · [PLAN § Phase 1](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · [quo receiver.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/providers/receiver.ts) · [quo.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/quo.test.ts) · [IDEAS/inbound-integrations.md § Order](IDEAS/inbound-integrations.md) · Seen again 2026-10-07 in the chatwoot stressor, Phase 1 (PLAN Q1), in a shape quo's fix does not survive: the delivery record (InboundMail) is in its own database (`database mail`, 30d retention) and the subject (Message) in `main`, so no transaction spans the record and the apply, and a process that dies between the two files leaves a record that was never applied, with nothing to delete it. Quo's delete-on-throw cannot reach that. What holds: the record is written first and unapplied; a retry that finds it UNAPPLIED applies it again; only an applied record makes a retry a duplicate; and applying twice is safe only because the subject is unique per inbox on (inboxId, mailMessageId), checked inside the apply's own transaction on `main`. Pinned by mail.test.ts 'a delivery recorded but never applied … is applied by the retry' and 'both providers at once: one conversation, one message' · [chatwoot inbound.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/mail/inbound.ts) · [mail.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/mail.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1804"></a>FJS-1804 | conduit | **inbound-integrations § B's receiver record (path · verify · window · dedupe) holds none of three measured webhook dialects whole: it has no field for WHICH key verifies a delivery, none for WHOSE event it is, and its dedupe key is a header in one of the three** | open | 2026-10-07 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 1 (PLAN Q1). Telnyx (Ed25519), Quo legacy (openphone-signature, HMAC) and Quo Standard Webhooks, written as three declarations of one hand-built record and driven over real sockets, 29 tests. (1) Credential: Telnyx holds one PUBLIC key for every tenant, which fits a resolver-held secret. Quo signs each workspace's subscription with that subscription's own shared key, so the key is a ROW the path names, chosen BEFORE verify; conduit's ref resolver has no per-tenant credential (third-party-credentials.md leg three). (2) Owner: neither carrier names our tenant. Telnyx's is found AFTER verify from a body field that moves with the event type (the to of a text, the from of a receipt, either end of a call by direction). Quo's is fixed before verify by the path's subscription, and the body's number is looked up inside it, which is the only thing keeping one tenant's events out of another's inbox (mutation-tested). (3) Dedupe: Telnyx and Quo legacy dedupe on a body id; Standard Webhooks on the webhook-id HEADER, one per delivery, while the envelope id is per event across subscriptions. (4) Parse and dedupe read the subscription too: Quo pins a subscription's payload generation when it is created, so which envelope to expect is the row's fact, not the body's. (5) Window is a check on a SIGNED field in all three, in the dialect's unit: Quo legacy signs milliseconds. (6) Key material comes in three shapes (a 32-byte Ed25519 public key, bare base64, whsec_-prefixed base64), and Standard Webhooks' several v1 entries are the SENDER rotating, which is not the receiver-side secrets[] § What goes in toolbelt asks for. The record the three needed: path · credential(delivery) · verify · window(unit) · parse(delivery, credential) · dedupe(delivery, event, credential) · owner(event, credential) · apply, answering applied, parked or ignored · [PLAN § Phase 1](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · [IDEAS/inbound-integrations.md § B](IDEAS/inbound-integrations.md) · [FJS-D177](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d177) · [quo receiver.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/providers/receiver.ts) · [quo doors.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/domain/doors.ts) · [quo.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/quo.test.ts) · Two mail dialects added 2026-10-07 in the chatwoot stressor, Phase 1 (PLAN Q1). Postmark inbound: no signature at all; the credential is basic auth from the URL the account was given, so there is no signed timestamp and no window, and a captured delivery replays until the password changes; dedupe on its MessageID body field (its delivery id, not the mail's Message-ID, which is a header in Headers[]). Mailgun raw-MIME route: HMAC-SHA256 over timestamp + token and NOT the body, so a captured signature verifies over any body inside the window, which is all that bounds it; and no delivery id, since token is random per request and a retry is a new, re-signed request (modelled from Mailgun's docs in the drive's sink, not measured against live Mailgun). The dedupe key is then the mail's own Message-ID plus the envelope recipient, because one mail to two of our addresses is two deliveries with one Message-ID. Owner: neither names our tenant; the envelope recipient with its plus-tag removed is looked up as an Inbox row, the same after-verify lookup as Telnyx's number. Parse: Postmark hands over parsed JSON (it even splits the plus-tag off, MailboxHash), Mailgun raw MIME, parsed in that connector with postal-mime (inbound-email open question 3, A). So across five dialects: a credential that is a password, an HMAC over the body, an HMAC over two fields, or an Ed25519 key; a dedupe key that is a body field, a header, or the subject's own natural key · [chatwoot postmark.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/mail/postmark.ts) · [mailgun.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/mail/mailgun.ts) · [providers.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/providers.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1801"></a>FJS-1801 | litestone | **A child's read can only delegate the parent's whole read rule, and the narrower one it needs is two hops away: an admin who manages a phone number reads every thread on it, and an admin can make a stranger a member of a number** | open | 2026-10-08 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, membership phase (PLAN Q10). Quo's visibility follows NUMBER membership, not workspace membership. PhoneNumber's read is members.some(userId == auth().id) \|\| check(org, 'update'): admins must see every number to manage it. Conversation is check(phoneNumber), so the admin clause comes along, and an admin who answers no number reads all its threads. 'Members only' on Conversation is phoneNumber.members.some(…), which FJS-D566 refuses at parse (a.b.some). The second rule: a NumberMember's user must be a Member of the number's org, this.userId in phoneNumber.org.members, two hops, refused by FJS-D221. Today an admin can add a stranger to a number and the stranger reads its inbox. Both are marked WANTED in quo's schema, and a test in telnyx.test.ts § Who sees a number pins the first and flips when it is fixed. Two possible shapes, wanting a ruling that amends D221/D566: delegate a NAMED rule of the parent (check(phoneNumber, 'inbox') against a declared @@allow('inbox', …)), or admit one more hop inside some(). FJS-1789 is the emitter's instance of the first: check(parent) carries a public clause the child should not inherit · [FJS-D221](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d221) · [FJS-D566](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d566) · [FJS-1789](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1789) · [quo schema.lite § Who belongs](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/schema.lite) · [telnyx.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/telnyx.test.ts) · Seen again 2026-10-08, chatwoot stressor Phase 2, as a leak to a STRANGER, reached by following FJS-D576. Message's read is `kind != 'note' && check(conversation)` OR `check(inbox)`, the second meaning 'an inbox member'. A widget visitor's message was refused 422 because a key the hook names counts as their read of Inbox (FJS-1953), and D576's stated way out is that the parent's read rule admits the caller. Adding `\|\| id == auth().inboxId` to Inbox's read made `check(inbox)` true for every visitor of that inbox: the agent's note reached the visitor on a list, on a get over the socket and on the live broadcast. Nothing at boot, in advise or in fli check said so. Reverted; pinned by widget.test.ts (re-adding it fails 4 of 43) · [schema.lite Inbox](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/db/schema.lite) · [widget.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/widget.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1798"></a>FJS-1798 | cli | **fli admin:generate writes screens the app's own gate warns about 31 times and a session stub nothing wires: hard-coded light colors that vanish under the dark theme, and a fixed gate level 7 shown to a signed-out visitor while sierra's session.level already holds the server's answer** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in base44 Phase 6, which gives every hosted app admin:generate's screens as its preview (Oracle's linear app, a copy under apps/host/). (1) fli check in the generated app: 31 css-raw-literal warnings, every one in web/src/routes/admin/index.mesa and _module.mesa — '.card { … border: 1px solid #e5e7eb; … color: #111 }', '.card span { color: #6b7280; font-size: 12px }' — so the dashboard's model titles are near-black on the dark theme's near-black (screenshot runs/2026-10-06-screen/preview-admin.png in base44). The generator is the framework's, and its output is what invariant 13 exists to refuse. (2) admin/_session.js is written as { level: 7 } 'permissive on purpose' with setLevel() for the app to wire; the scaffold wires nothing, so a signed-out visitor sees 'gate level 7' in the admin nav and is offered every create/edit/delete, each a 401 on press. @frontierjs/sierra/resource's session.level (0 with no session, the server's grade with one) postdates the stub and is the value it was waiting for; the stub could read it and be deleted. (3) The build of the generated routes prints Mesa's own stale-read warning for every detail page ('page.params.id' read by const id with no $: watch) and for session.level, so a record page opened from another record page keeps the first id. Affordances only — the server grades every request — but this is the first screen a builder shows a person of the app they asked for. · Cost, measured the same day: base44's builder prepares its preview inside the app (one-line services, admin:generate, a web build), and its settle bar is fli check over the whole app. After the first preview, every edit turn's grade carried the generated admin's css-raw-literal warnings, which no answer can change: one ask ('remove the source field from clients') ran its first call and 4 fix calls, $1.03, and stalled with the schema correct on every call. base44 now leaves web/src/routes/admin/ out of the bar (builder/studio.ts gradeAnswerable). Any loop that grades an app with fli check after admin:generate has run meets the same wall. |
| <a id="fjs-1797"></a>FJS-1797 | cli | **fli outbox:install, backfill:install and auth:install apply their table with db push, so in an app that already has a migration history the next fli db:migrate refuses — and the wiring outbox:install prints names a file the scaffold does not have and a context the guide says not to use** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-06 in base44 Phase 6, the builder app itself (fli new --full, two migrations applied). fli outbox:install appended the import to db/schema.lite and ran 'litestone db push': outbox_message was created in app.db and in no migration file; fli db:status still reported '2 applied' and clean. The next fli db:migrate refused — 'this database is not what its migration history builds: ? outbox_message (in db, not in schema) … A migration created now cannot re-apply over it' — so an app's first enqueue costs a recovery (drop the table by hand, or migrate create + baseline) the install never mentions. AGENTS.md's own migration-history rule says db push is local-only and a deploy replays files; the install commands are the framework breaking it on the app's behalf. Same exec in packages/cli/commands/backfill/install.md:182 and auth/install.md:450 (auth runs before any history exists in fli new, so it is the outbox and backfill installs, run later, that bite). The fix is to write the migration file (migrate create, then apply) instead of pushing. Second half, the printed wiring: it says 'Add to api/src/server.ts' (the scaffold's file is api/src/app.ts), passes jobsDir: './src/jobs' (cwd-relative; the scaffold's other wiring uses new URL('./jobs', import.meta.url)), and its service example writes ctx.locals.db / ctx.enqueue where junction's AGENTS.md says $.db / $.enqueue. See FJS-1792 for the print-not-write half. · Seen again 2026-10-09 in sstime Phase 5 (row tenancy, five migrations applied), with two things the row does not say. (1) The push applies the WHOLE pending schema, not the outbox table: the app's own unmigrated edits (a new column on work_offer, two new models) landed in app.db too, so the migrate create run right after wrote a file that then failed on apply with 'duplicate column name: withdrawnAt' — the history and the database now disagree about the app's OWN tables. Recovered by rebuilding the empty dev db from history. (2) Under tenancy { strategy row }, the imported OutboxMessage warns on every litestone command — 'declares no accountId … NOT scoped to a tenant — OutboxMessage. Add the column … or mark each @@tenant(none)' — and the app cannot mark a model it imports; junction/outbox.lite should declare @@tenant(none) itself, since a relay reads every tenant's rows · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/sstime/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1796"></a>FJS-1796 | litestone · junction | **An app starts, and answers health 200, on a database whose tables are not its schema's — a model with no table, a same-named table carrying another model's columns — and nothing at start compares the two, so two apps in one process share one file under two schemas in silence** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in base44 Phase 6 (builder/host.ts mount, runs/2026-10-06-host.json). Control: Oracle's notion app started alone in its own process on a copy of Oracle's linear app's database — health 200, no warning, and its page and membership tables absent; its first read of either is the first anyone hears. The mount case is that plus process-wide config: two apps' own api/src/app.ts imported and started in one bun process both read the one DATABASE_URL (defineEnv reads process.env at import; the default ./db/app.db is cwd-relative, and the banner's app name comes from the cwd too), so both opened one SQLite file — one user and session table between them (a session minted by one app is a session of the other), one workspace table under two different column sets, notion's page/membership never created — and both answered health 200. No migration ran at start (migrations are files, FJS-D123), so the fix is a comparison, not a migrate: at createClient, a declared model with no table, or a table whose columns are not the model's, is a refusal by name, or at least a boot warning the way an ungraded service is. Scope: this is what makes a mount (Q7's third hosting model) unsafe without code, and it is reachable by any deploy pointed at the wrong file. |

| <a id="fjs-1790"></a>FJS-1790 | junction | **A custom method written in junction's documented shape — return $.db.order.transition($.id, 'ship') — answers 200 with null to a caller who cannot see the row, where patch on the same row answers 404** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in base44 Phase 4 (apps/behavior/03-linear-plane @ ck-2): an outsider calling `projects.archive` on another workspace's project got 200 and null; the project and its issues were untouched. | <a id="fjs-1780"></a>FJS-1780 | litestone | **advise's gate-with-nothing-saying-whose-row is silent on a model with any @@allow, whatever op it covers, and grades a model owned through a parent as 'right for a catalog' — so 21 of 24 cross-tenant reads a generated schema declared were not called likely** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-06, fjs-prototypes/base44 (Phase 1 freehand corpus, 21 apps; access drive reading every model as user B in a world of its own). 24 models had a signed-in read gate and no read policy, so B read A's rows. fli advise --json over the same final schemas: 3 likely, 16 possible ('Right for a catalog; worth stating either way'), 5 nothing. The 5: linear's Project, Issue and Comment, jazzhr's Scorecard and Note — each declares @@allow('update'\|'delete', authorId == auth().id) and no read policy, and src/core/opportunities.js counts the model as policied on ANY allow/deny (policied = attributes.some(kind allow\|deny)), so the read is never asked about. Wanted: policied judged per op (a model with no read policy is unanswered for read whatever its write rules say), and ownership read through a required relation to a model that is itself owned. advise exits 0 over all of it, so a builder that reads exit codes never shows the model. · runs/2026-10-06-freehand.json M4 · PLAN.md Q3 · **The delete half, measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor.** Subscription, AppliedCoupon and CreditNote declared read/create(/update) allows under `@@gate("4")` and no delete allow, so delete was unrestricted past the gate: a signed-in user who belongs to NO organization deleted another organization's subscription, applied coupon and credit note (the last against a finalized invoice), 9 of 9 attempts. `fli advise` named none of the three (one unrelated index warning); verifyGateLadder passes, since delete at 4 is what the gate declares; Phase 0's `test:mutate` could not see it, as there was no mutation to make. Fixed in the app by gating delete to 8.
