| <a id="fjs-1779"></a>FJS-1779 | litestone | **Auto-factories cannot seed a quarter of the models a generated schema declares: withParents().createOne() writes @scoped edges as columns, ignores cross-field @@check and @required(where:), leaves @type'd Json empty, and draws one-letter slugs that collide** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06, base44 stressor Phase 1, the access drive (builder/access-drive.ts: createTestEnv autoFactories, factories.<m>.asSystem().withParents({ pins })): 29 of 160 models across 13 of 20 generated apps could not be seeded, so they went ungraded. Four causes. (1) **@scoped/@edge written as a column**: *table page has no column named starred* (notion, `starred Boolean @scoped`), *table cipher has no column named favorite* (vaultwarden), 6 models. (2) **model-level rules unread**: *A meeting must end after it starts* — calendly, connecteam, linear, jazzhr, remnant, eventmark, etsy Payout, each an @@check over two DateTimes; *an issue cannot be related to itself*; *A face's box must have a size*; *A text Send needs its text* (@required(where:)), 13 models. (3) **@type'd Json left empty**: *shippingAddress.name: is required* (etsy, 3 models), *definition.columns: is required* (transit, 5). (4) **a generated unique slug one character long**: *Team: slug "g" is already taken* on the second row, which takes out all 7 models below Team in the CI-runner app. Same family as [FJS-1487](#fjs-1487) — the fixture is built from field types, not from the rules the schema declares on them; verifyGateLadder, verifyRowPolicies and every app's access.test.ts share these factories · [runs/2026-10-06-freehand.json](../fjs-prototypes/base44/runs/2026-10-06-freehand.json) · With corpus prompt 7 (Lago) rerun and the drive holding ONE asSystem() factory per model (so a fresh copy's restarted sequence is ruled out), the count is **37 of 176 models across 13 of 21 apps**, unchanged in kind. Cause (4) narrowed: the one-letter slug is `slug String @unique @trim @lower @regex("^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$")`, and the factory draws the regex's shortest match, *g*, for every row; Lago's `externalId String @unique` collides (*ExternalId 6 is already taken*) between a Customer built inside withParents and one built at the top, so the parent's sequence and the top-level factory's do not share a counter. A fifth cause: a composite relation, `Deployment.project @relation(fields: [projectId, teamId], references: [id, teamId])` — withParents fills each column on its own, *projectId,teamId [2,1] names no Project* · Second sighting 2026-10-06, base44 Phase 2. Emitted by @frontierjs/oracle, which writes none of the shapes listed here (no @scoped, cross-field @@check, @required(where:), @type'd Json or @regex unique), the access drive seeded all 77 models in 21 apps, against 37 of 176 unseeded freehand. One more cause, found while building the emitter: a DateTime @date column parses and passes fli advise, and the auto-factory draws an ISO timestamp for it that @date then refuses ('must be a valid date in YYYY-MM-DD format'), so the model cannot be seeded. The emitter writes String @date, the form db:explain shows. · runs/2026-10-06-oracle.json |
| <a id="fjs-1778"></a>FJS-1778 | cli | **fli check's transition-methods refuses every @@transitions until service code drives it, so a schema written first — what AGENTS.md tells every app to do — is never clean, and the only schema-side reply is deleting the state machine** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06, base44 stressor Phase 1: 20 corpus prompts, claude-opus-5-5 writing db/schema.lite into fresh `fli new` apps, graded by parseFile + `fli advise` + `fli check`, 8 turns. 18 of 20 schemas declared @@transitions on turn 1. The 7 that reached green did it by DELETING every machine (status page, notion, portal, posthog, ksite, immich, eventmark): Incident's identify/monitor/resolve became a free enum and a CHECK. The 11 that kept theirs stayed red to the cap with transition-methods the only rule firing (calendly 2 findings × 8 turns, connecteam 16, etsy 28), and the model said why on every turn: *the fix is a bookings service that calls $.db.booking.transition(id, 'cancel') … deleting the two moves would remove cancelling and no-shows*. 13 of 20 were green-but-for-this-rule on turn 1 and all 20 by turn 2. The rule's own text says *not an error — the machine is still enforced*, yet it is a finding, and `fli check` is the gate `bun run check` and ci.yml run. Cost: $18.98 of the run's $27.60 went to the 81 turns spent after a schema was first green but for this rule. Wanted: the rule silent (or info) where no service exists for the model yet, or keyed to services that exist and do not name the move · [runs/2026-10-06-freehand.json](../fjs-prototypes/base44/runs/2026-10-06-freehand.json) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/base44/PLAN.md) · With corpus prompt 7 (Lago) rerun, the whole corpus: 19 of 21 schemas declared @@transitions on turn 1; the 7 green ones deleted every machine, the 12 that kept one stayed red to the cap (Lago: 10 transition-methods findings × 8 turns, nothing else). Green-but-for-this-rule: 14 of 21 on turn 1, 21 of 21 by turn 2. $20.79 of the run's $30.12 went to the 87 turns after a schema was first green but for this rule · Second sighting 2026-10-06, base44 Phase 2 (the same 21 prompts through @frontierjs/oracle, where an emitter writes the schema from a model's answer): 16 of 21 answers declared a lifecycle, and all 16 were red by the plan's bar on transition-methods alone, never anything else. The 5 plan-green apps are exactly the 5 with no lifecycle. An emitter has no reply to the rule except dropping the machine, so the harness stopped at the first turn where nothing else remained. Under that bar all 21 settled (16 on turn 1). The rule is now the only thing between a generated schema and green in both conditions. · runs/2026-10-06-oracle.json |

From base44/apps/, `fli new probe --source local --full --yes` printed *Creating probe at …/base44/probe* and wrote it there, a sibling of apps/, exit 0 and no warning. From base44/apps/probe/ (empty), `fli new probe --here …` refused with *Current directory is not empty (17 entries)*, counting base44's own root. fli resolves the enclosing project root before a command that has no project yet. `--project <abs>/apps/probe --here` writes to the right place, and that is what builder/run.ts passes. | <a id="fjs-1761"></a>FJS-1761 | basecamp · outpost | **outpost.service runs as root with no systemd sandboxing, so the process that answers a network port can write anywhere on the box** | open | 2026-10-05 | Read 2026-10-05, not run on a machine (FJS-257). The unit `installScript()` writes ([enrollment.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/compute/enrollment.ts)) has `EnvironmentFile`, `ExecStart`, `Restart` and nothing else: no `User=`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `ProtectSystem`, `ProtectHome`, `PrivateTmp`, `ReadWritePaths` or capability bounding. The command port is signed and pinned (FJS-D557), so this is depth rather than an open door, but a flaw in any route — `/exec` by design, `/static/publish` by mistake — lands as root over the whole filesystem. Docker access is root-equivalent anyway, so a non-root `User=` alone buys little; what narrows it is `ProtectSystem=strict` with `ReadWritePaths` for the Outpost's own directories (`/etc/basecamp`, the static root, bun's cache) and `NoNewPrivileges`. Grade it with `systemd-analyze security outpost`, which is the `outpost.sandbox` control in [server-posture.md](IDEAS/server-posture.md) · [outpost CLAUDE.md](packages/outpost/CLAUDE.md) · blocked by `FJS-D602` |
| <a id="fjs-1759"></a>FJS-1759 | sierra · litestone | **Two people editing two keys of one Json column lose one edit, and the spelling that keeps both ($merge) is named nowhere a screen writer reads** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured in the notion stressor, Phase 6c: a database row's cells are keys of one `Page.props Json`. | <a id="fjs-1758"></a>FJS-1758 | litestone | **@@extensible keys a declaration per TENANT only, so a model whose columns are declared per PARENT ROW — a Notion database, an Airtable table, a form builder's questions — has no spelling: a declarer unique on [databaseId, key] is refused at parse, and the per-tenant shape that parses refused 277 of 290 realistic declarations and mirrored one database's values into another's slots.** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05, notion PLAN § Q6 (probes/q6/run.ts over 50 databases, 10k rows; http.ts over the app). **Parse:** `@@unique([model, databaseId, key])` and with the tenant spelled → *'Property' does not declare @@unique([model, key])*; keeping `[model, key]` beside `[databaseId, key]` parses and stays per workspace. **Arm A (per tenant, as shipped):** Bugs.status (number) is a 409 because Tasks.status (select) exists; the 47 small databases got 6 of 282 columns; Bugs' undeclared status is mirrored into Tasks' select slot (s1 = "2"), so a filter naming no database answers Bugs rows; no declared KIND is checked (estimate "abc", due "soon", status "nope", done "yes" all stored; the number slot drops "abc" silently); no sort on a declared key, while orderBy accepts the slot's own name (`n1`) and lists slots as sortable. Right in A: `$declaredFields({ where: { databaseId } })` narrows (4 of 13), and the declarer's own policy grades a guest and a link holder to one database's columns. **Arm C (Property keyed per database, declared to nothing, the app's choice):** 290 of 290 declare; the app writes the kind check (api/src/core/props.ts, ~70 lines, refused beside the field); the wire has no filter or sort on a property (`props[status]=done` → *untyped Json column … Declare @type or use $raw*, and $raw is not on the wire); the JSON schema offers a form nothing; a row MOVED into another database carries its props unchecked — under a per-row scope a move changes which declarations apply, Q1's shape again. The ruling is [FJS-D592](#fjs-d592) · [PLAN.md § Q6](../fjs-prototypes/notion/PLAN.md) · [out.txt](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q6/out.txt) · [http.txt](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q6/http.txt) · [props.ts](../fjs-prototypes/notion/api/src/core/props.ts) · [FJS-D365](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d365) (the same scope: on @@transitions) · [tenant-declared-fields.md](IDEAS/shipped/tenant-declared-fields.md) · blocked by `FJS-D592` · Phase 6c (the screen), notion 2026-10-05: with the declarations as rows, the kit draws none of a database's table. `<Cell>` and `columns()` see `props` as one Json column, so the screen restates: a control per kind (components/PropValue.mesa, 58 lines, select options read off `Property.options`), the per-kind filter match, sort order (a select by option order), URL value typing and orphan detection (lib/props.js, 60 lines of code), and a key generator that skips every key a loaded row still holds, because a re-declared key answers with the deleted column's values in arm C exactly as in A (FJS-1752's shape is the values outliving the declaration, not the slot). Filter and sort run in the browser over one 500-row window (`?status=doing&$orderBy=-estimate`, kept by a reload), and the screen says so. The value check is never reached from the screen: each control is its kind, so core/props.ts guards only non-screen callers. Driven by olivia, gus (read: refused cell and column, live cell and column reach his table) and a link (columns, 0 rows) in Chrome and Firefox on dev and build: notion probes/screens/db.mjs, db.txt. A cell edit must be a $merge (FJS-1759). |
| <a id="fjs-1750"></a>FJS-1750 | sierra · junction · litestone | **A screen cannot know the caller may read a row and not change it: can() grades the gate level only, and an update a row policy refuses answers 404 '<service> with id=… not found' for a row the caller has on screen** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05 in the notion stressor, PLAN § Phase 6, in Chrome and Firefox, dev server and build ([probes/screens/run.mjs](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/screens/run.mjs) step 3). gus is a W1 guest with a Share at `read` on a page; Block update is `check(page, 'update')` and Page update needs an edit or full grant. His screen draws the page's blocks (GET 200) with the edit controls every reader gets, because nothing in the browser can say otherwise: sierra's `resource.can(op, level)` is `canAtLevel(gate, …)` (resource.js), the gate is `0.4.4.4` and gus is level 4, so can('update') is true. He changes a paragraph: the patch answers 404 *blocks with id=01M46RB86XGQV3G864QK0XNF9D not found*. The row is on his screen and readable; *not found* is the policy filtering the UPDATE's target (a refusal must never confirm a row exists, litestone AGENTS.md), which is right for a row he cannot read and wrong-sounding for one he can. The page screen ([pages/[id].mesa](../fjs-prototypes/notion/web/src/routes/pages/[id].mesa) refused()) translates any 404 on a write into *You can read this page but not change it*, which it cannot tell from a block someone else deleted; and the input keeps the refused text. Wanted, either half: a per-row affordance the browser can ask (the row's own update policy evaluated as the caller, e.g. a `$can` on get, or a read-time column a policy derives), or a refused write on a row the caller CAN read answered 403 by name. Related: [FJS-1508](#fjs-1508) (transitions(row, level) does grade the policy, for a state machine only) |
| <a id="fjs-1749"></a>FJS-1749 | junction · litestone | **Who may mint, see or revoke a bearer link has no declaration at the gate junction's guide gives a grant model (@@gate("8")), and a required @guarded digest makes the model uncreatable below 8 either way; the share dialog's link was spelled as a policy-graded create plus a system write of a NULLABLE digest** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05, notion stressor PLAN § Phase 6 (the share dialog shows the link is on and offers Turn off, so a person reads that a link exists). junction AGENTS.md § bearers: *The grant model is @@gate("8")*; litestone: *A required @guarded column makes the model uncreatable below level 8*. Over HTTP: the owner 201, gus (read share on that page) 403 *Create denied by @@allow policy*, gus lists 0 links, the owner 1. | <a id="fjs-1745"></a>FJS-1745 | litestone | **A grant level that sits between two operations has no spelling: Notion's *can comment* (read < comment < edit) is not an operation a parent has, and the child cannot reach the level, so a comment-level guest is refused every comment** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured in the notion stressor, PLAN § Q4 ([probes/q4/run.ts](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q4/run.ts) step 3b, [comment-level.ts](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q4/comment-level.ts)). Share.level is read \| comment \| edit \| full; Comment is `@@allow('create', check(block, 'update'))`, so wren, granted *comment*, is a 403 on her own page. `check(rel, op)` delegates one of four CRUD operations and `@@allow` names no other, so there is nothing on Page for *comment* to be. Said in Comment's own terms, both spellings are refused at parse: `block.page.access.some(…)` (*crosses two relations*) and, with a copied pageId, `page.access.some(…)` (*tests rows two relations away*; its advice, *put the rule on the model page points at*, leads back to Page, which has no fourth operation). What is left is a service hook, the shape Invariant 6 refuses. Related: [FJS-1245](#fjs-1245) (a named move cannot be more permissive than an arbitrary edit), the same *the operations are fixed at four* one model over |

Resolving it as each reader (GET /api/users/:id): the owner can name 4 of the room's 4 people, a member 4 of 4, and gus, a guest, 1 of 4 (himself), because User's read policy does not admit a guest to the roster ([FJS-1732](#fjs-1732)), and *people in the same room* is not a fact any policy can read. The two ways a screen could print names both go around a declaration: put the name in the meta, where the validator (`presenceMeta`, transport/channels.ts) cannot check it against the sender because it is never told the sender (gus sending `{ name: 'Olivia Owner' }` is refused here only because the app's validator refuses every extra key), or have a service read the names as the system for whoever the roster lists. Wanted: the validator receives the connection (or its principal), so a server-stamped identity can ride the meta |
| <a id="fjs-1742"></a>FJS-1742 | junction | **FJS-1425's fix sends '<service> removed <id>' to EVERY refused reader of EVERY update, not to the readers who could read the row before it: a guest of one page receives the id and the moment of every page edited anywhere in the workspace, and every write fans out to every subscriber, refused or not** | open | 2026-10-08 | notion Phase 4 (the real app, gus a W1 guest reading 6 of 60 pages over a junction client socket). Retitling 3 pages he CAN read sends 'pages patched', as it should. Moves of unreadable pages: the same, 39 frames. channels.ts gradeRecipients: `if (!visible) { if (removal) out.push({ conns, frame: removal }); continue }` for every cohort $readAs refuses; its comment accepts 'an id they already held, or one of a row they never saw'. The reader owed a removal is the one who could read the row BEFORE the write; the write path has the old row (an update reads it), so grading the before-image as well would send 'removed' only on a narrowing, which is FJS-1425's case. Found while measuring FJS-1738. · **Amended 2026-10-06, quo stressor Phase 3: it crosses the TENANT line, not only a guest's lens.** Four real accounts on real sockets (api/test/ring.test.ts § who hears a ring): one stranger's call to Acme, rung, answered by ana and hung up, reaches dan, the owner of ANOTHER workspace who reads nothing of Acme's, as 8 frames, every one 'calls removed {id}' or 'callLegs removed {id}' and nothing else. The writes are asSystem() updateMany calls in a webhook, each with announce: 'rows' (the FJS-1811 workaround). A help-desk widget visitor is a bearer minted from a public widget token on somebody else's site (STRANGER, no session), and every visitor joins 'messages'. An agent of ANOTHER org replies to a mail in their own inbox; when the reply is marked sent (an update), the visitor's socket receives 'messages removed {id}' and nothing else. So anyone who opens any customer's widget streams the ids and timing of every reply sent by every org on the host. | <a id="fjs-1741"></a>FJS-1741 | junction | **Junction never calls a FUNCTION channel for a write outside a service call, though it holds the row; under strategy row, where FJS-D191 makes a per-workspace function channel the only shape that grades, no background write on a tenanted model reaches a socket, and the warning's advice (declare a channel NAME) is the shape that refuses every recipient (FJS-749)** | open | 2026-10-05 | notion Phase 4 (probes/q2/run.ts, NOTION_Q2_ANNOUNCE=touch). The app announces Page/Block/Share on workspace:<id> through `channel: (data, ctx) => app.channel('workspace:' + ctx.auth.user.workspaceId)`, basecamp's and linear's shape. Inside shares.remove (transactional), the copy maintainer rewrites PageAccess and writes the 6 affected Page rows with $.db.asSystem(): $tapEvents hears all 6 (measured), announceDataWrites emits pages:updated on the bus, and sendToChannel warns "'pages' declares a function channel: and a write reached it outside any service call … went to the event bus but not to any socket" and sends nothing. gus's stores are identical to the arm that writes no pages at all (6 stale after a revoke, 0 of 6 after a grant). The same holds for a job, a webhook, a seed and a bulk write on any tenanted model: FJS-010 and FJS-307 are fixed for apps with string channels only. The function is HANDED the rows (`(rows, ctx)`); what it lacks is a request, and a tenanted row carries its tenant (Page.workspaceId) or reaches it through @@tenant(via:) (Block). | <a id="fjs-1740"></a>FJS-1740 | junction · sierra | **A record(id) view never re-reads on resync, and a re-read it does make (on 'changed') keeps the row it last held when the server refuses it: after a revoke and a reconnect every live list is right and the open page still shows the row the reader may no longer read** | open | 2026-10-05 | notion Phase 4, PLAN § Q2 (probes/q2/run.ts step 1c, out.txt). gus holds resource('pages').load(), pages.record(D) and resource('blocks').load({pageId: D}) over a real socket. FJS-701's resync re-runs both LISTS: the sidebar goes 6 → 0 and the blocks 3 → 0, matching his re-read. The record still holds D's row while GET /api/pages/D answers 404. Read in client/index.ts record(): it subscribes to the node, to 'removed' and to 'changed' and never to 'resync'; and its fetch() on 'changed' catches a refused get() and returns null WITHOUT emitting or clearing the node ('answers whatever it last knew'), so even a re-read that the server refuses leaves the row on screen. A list removing the row does not reach the record either: a list owns its membership and the node keeps its last value. The two honest answers a record has are the list's: re-read on resync, and treat a refused re-read (404 for a row it held) as gone. | <a id="fjs-1737"></a>FJS-1737 | litestone · junction | **A @createdBy column is listed as REQUIRED in the generated JSON schema, so a service create refuses a payload without it (400 '<field> is required') although the Data boundary stamps it from auth().id and overwrites any value sent; every caller must send a value that is thrown away** | open | 2026-10-05 | notion Phase 4 (fjs-prototypes/notion). | <a id="fjs-1732"></a>FJS-1732 | litestone · junction | **Under tenancy { strategy row } the tenant claim is the only admission and nothing names membership: a guest (a roster row with a lesser role) and a link holder whose bearer names the tenant are both admitted, and every policy that says same-workspace as workspaceId == auth().workspaceId, the documented common case, reads them as members** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05 over HTTP, notion stressor Phase 3 (PLAN Q3). Notion's guest is on the workspace's roster, and a Member row with role 'guest' read through membershipClaim is the only spelling that passes the tenant guard (no row: 403 'You do not belong'; no header: 400). With it, before the app named roles, guest gus read the W1 roster (4 Member rows) and every W1 person (4 Users) through Member read 'workspaceId == auth().workspaceId' and User read 'members.some(workspaceId == auth().workspaceId)'. Writes held: Member.create refused by @@capabilities (neither holds the grant; the row policy alone would admit both), Page.create by its policy. The fix is the app's: memberRole in ['owner','admin','member'] in 5 policies (Page read/create/update, Member read, User read); memberRole != null admits the guest. | <a id="fjs-1718"></a>FJS-1718 | litestone · sierra | **A model whose type column selects its columns (a discriminated union) can say *needed when* to every consumer and *only when* to the boundary alone: @required(where:) crosses as an AST, its mirror is a field @check whose SQL predicate never leaves the server, so the JSON schema, the generated types and <Form> each believe a heading may carry `checked`** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05 in the notion stressor (Q5, Block with 7 types): the boundary refuses all 7 bad shapes with the field's path and message once each variant column carries @required(where: type == …) AND @check("type = 'todo' OR checked IS NULL", "…"). Above it: `db/.json/schema.json` carries x-litestone-required-where as an AST but only x-messages.check for the other half, no predicate; `litestone types` emits one flat Block interface, every variant column `?: T \| null` on every type, so narrowing on `type` buys nothing. Read, not yet measured: sierra's `requiredFor` (field-rules.js:1993) tracks the first half as `type` changes and nothing hides a control the type does not own — measured in notion Phase 6. A `@@check` spelling is worse: no path (`errors[0].path == []`). There is no word for the union (`fli db:explain` refuses @@variant, @@union, @@discriminator, @@oneOf, @requiredIf, @when). Related: IDEAS/overview.md 4.26 (variants, which may only RELAX and add no storage) answers authority per variant, not shape per variant · fjs-prototypes/notion/db/schema.lite (model Block) · Phase 6 (notion, the page screen's add menu): which block types a text box can create is Block.text's `@required(where: type == 'paragraph' \|\| … 'toggle')`, and the resource hands the browser `fields.type.enum` (all seven) and no reading of the condition, so the menu restates the four text types by name ([pages/[id].mesa](../fjs-prototypes/notion/web/src/routes/pages/[id].mesa) TEXT_TYPES), and a heading's size and a to-do's checked are set by hand for the same reason |
| <a id="fjs-1708"></a>FJS-1708 | example | **Example `verify` writes onto the seeded `SUB-3001` and its PRO plan on every run and never clears either. After a few weeks of runs the dev db breaks four checks in two drives, and re-seeding does not fix it.** Each run's `subDetail.changePlan` issues an invoice on `SUB-3001`, and `planDetail.repriced` opens a new PRO `PlanVersion`. Measured 2026-10-04: 208 invoices on `SUB-3001` and 218 versions. Three effects follow. (1) The pages ask for 50 rows newest-first, so `subDetail.changePlan` waits for an invoice count that cannot rise (this is `FJS-1376`'s cause), and `verify:account`'s *and their invoices* never sees `INV-3001`. (2) `planDetail.newWindowEmpty` never sees version 1, the one `SUB-3001` holds. (3) The unpaid invoices lapse `SUB-3001` to `pastDue` through dunning, which reddens `subDetail.stopRenewing`. `db:seed` upserts and deletes nothing, so it heals none of this. `verify:account` also throws before its cleanup runs, which leaves `SUB-ACC*`/`SUB-OTH*`/`INV-A*`/`INV-Q*` behind on every run. Done when both drives mint what they change, or put it back, and both hold on a db that has been driven for a month. | open | 2026-10-04 | Cleared by hand on 2026-10-04: the invoices and the run rows deleted, `SUB-3001` recovered with `recoverIfClear`. Example `verify` then passed `subDetail.changePlan` and `subDetail.stopRenewing`. `planDetail.newWindowEmpty` stays red because the versions were left alone. Unrelated to the billing lift (`FJS-D575`), which passes the same steps. |

| <a id="fjs-1674"></a>FJS-1674 | litestone | **The client schema drops a read-policied column from required, so a create form calls a NOT NULL column optional** | open | 2026-10-04 | Found by Transit Phase 5 (fjs-prototypes/transit, 2026-10-03). packages/litestone/src/jsonschema.js:607: under audience 'client', a field with @allow('read', expr) is rewritten to anyOf [type, null] and left out of 'required', because its value may be absent on a READ for a caller the policy refuses. The browser builds its WRITE form from that same schema, so 'may be absent when read' becomes 'may be omitted when written'. Measured with generateJsonSchema over transit's db/schema.lite: client Template.required = [name, report, kind] and Source.required = [name, kind]; system Template.required = [name, report, kind, source] and Source.required = [name, kind, schemaText]. Both columns are non-optional Strings with @allow('read', auth().isStaff). On screen: <Form> labels Source and Schema text '(Optional)', and a blank one passes the client validator and is refused only by the server's NOT NULL. Transit has two sightings (Source.schemaText, Phase 2; Template.source, Phase 5). The read half and the write half need separate answers: optional for a read is right, required for a create is still true. |

| <a id="fjs-1665"></a>FJS-1665 | email-kit | **A row component (Heading, Text, KeyValue, Image, DataTable) inside a Column renders, and everything after it runs the client's full width** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen by Transit Phase 3 (RevenueReport.email.mesa). Five of the kit's components render a <tr>, so they belong in a Section or a Card; Column renders a <td>. Wrapped as <Row><Column><DataTable/></Column></Row> — the README's own shape for content — the row lands inside a cell, the parser moves it out, and the DataTable lays out 668px wide in a 700px window instead of 568px inside the 600px container. Nothing warns, the README does not say which components are rows, and a string check over the output cannot see it because the server render has already moved the rows. slot-guard.js exists for the sibling mistake (children to a childless component); a row component could say the same when its parent is a Column. |
| <a id="fjs-1657"></a>FJS-1657 | litestone · sierra · ui | **No schema word makes a String multi-line, so a column holding code or an address renders as a one-line input that cannot hold a newline; only @markdown reaches a textarea** | open | 2026-10-04 | Found by the transit stressor's drive (PLAN Phase 2), 2026-10-03. Source.schemaText holds a landing schema as .lite text, many lines, and the generated settings form (ui Form over sierra formFields) renders it as <input type=text>: sierra field-rules.js answers control 'textarea' for a string only when contentMediaType is text/markdown (@markdown), and every other String is 'input'. A one-line input's value has its newlines stripped by the browser's value sanitization, so an admin who edits the schema there gets one line, and in .lite the first // comment then swallows the rest. Measured: saving the form WITHOUT touching the field leaves the row byte for byte (the form sends what changed; the drive asserts it), so nothing is corrupted silently — the field is just unusable for its one purpose. @markdown is the wrong word (the text is not Markdown and would render as it), and an app cannot target one column through registerControl because a resolver is handed the rule alone, with no model or field name. Wanted: a schema word for plain multi-line text (or code, which would reach ui's CodeInput), derived into control 'textarea'/'code' the way @markdown is. Transit leaves schemaText as it is until then; it is written by the seed and by admins through the API. · Second sighting, Transit Phase 5 (2026-10-03): Template.source holds a Mesa template, so the generated one-line box cannot edit it. Template's default form (web/src/resources/Template.mesa) is hand-written for that one column, with CodeInput, and names every field to do it. · **Code half answered 2026-10-03:** `@syntax(lang)` replaces @markdown — `x-syntax` in the JSON Schema, control `code` (CodeInput in that language) for every language but `md`, which stays a textarea. Both sightings are code, and Transit now declares them (`schemaText String @syntax(lite)`, `source String @syntax(mesa)`, and Report's `query`/`transform`/`template`): its hand-written Template form and its `registerControl` claim on `Source.schemaText` are gone, and the generated `code` control is numbered (2026-10-04, Transit `verify` green through both forms). **Still open:** plain multi-line text (an address), which is not a syntax and has no sighting yet. · blocked by `FJS-D673` |
| <a id="fjs-1656"></a>FJS-1656 | litestone | **A view's @@sql reads a @guarded column in plaintext, and an @encrypted one as ciphertext, to any caller who passes the view's gate; nothing at build says so** | open | 2026-10-03 | Found by the transit stressor (PLAN Phase 2, L9) proving sensitive fields in a landing schema held in a row. Probed 2026-10-03 with createClient({ untrusted: true, encryptionKey }) and the app's gate plugin: model Person { id String @id; score Int @guarded; email String @encrypted; @@gate("4.8.8.8"); @@allow('read', auth().isStaff) } and view personScore { id String; score Int; email String; @@sql("SELECT id, score, email FROM person"); @@gate("4"); @@allow('read', auth().isStaff) }. A level-4 staff reader gets { id } from person.findFirst() and { id, score: 7, email: 'v2.…' } from personScore.findFirst(). @guarded is documented as 'absent from reads … outside asSystem()', and FJS-D52 keeps raw SQL behind asSystem(), but a view's @@sql is raw SQL that runs for every reader the view's own gate admits, and its columns take no attributes (FJS-1643), so the protection cannot be restated on it either. Not an escalation inside one schema (whoever writes the view writes the @guarded too, and could drop it), so S3: a silent hole in what the word promises, in a trusted schema as much as a row-held one. Every client already prepares each view at build (FJS-1632); that is where a view whose result columns come from a @guarded, @secret, @hashed or @encrypted column could be refused by name, or the column marked protected on the view. Transit's landing schemas keep sensitive columns out of every view until then. |
| <a id="fjs-1655"></a>FJS-1655 | litestone | **@secret in a schema with no logger database drops its @log half without a word, and an untrusted schema can never declare one** | open | 2026-10-03 | Found by the transit stressor (PLAN Phase 2, L9), probing @encrypted/@guarded/@secret in a landing schema held in a Source row and built with createClient({ untrusted: true }). docs/encryption.md § @secret says it 'expands at parse time to @encrypted + @guarded + @log(audit). Every read and write is logged to the audit logger database.' The parser's own comment (core/parser.js, case 'secret') says '(log only if logger db declared)', and that is what happens: with no logger database the parsed field's attributes are ['secret','encrypted','guarded'] and no read or write of it is ever logged, with no warning at parse, at build or at the first read. The encryption and the guard both hold (probed: ciphertext on disk, absent from a level-4 read, refused in a where, refused on a write outside asSystem()). An untrusted schema refuses every database block by name (FJS-1633), so in a schema held in a row @secret can only ever be @encrypted @guarded spelled shorter, while its docs promise an audit trail. Either refuse @secret where no logger database is declared (naming @encrypted @guarded as the honest spelling), or let the host hand an untrusted client an audit sink it owns; and correct the docs to say which. Transit's landing schemas use @encrypted @guarded spelled out until then. |
| <a id="fjs-1648"></a>FJS-1648 | ui | **A prop a kit component does not declare lands on its root element as an HTML attribute, so <EmptyState sub="…"> renders no text and says nothing** | open | 2026-10-03 | Found 2026-10-03 (Transit). RevenueReport wrote <EmptyState title=… sub="Pick another currency…"> by analogy with SectionHeader and StatCard, which both take sub; EmptyState's body is its default slot, so sub rode {...$attributes} onto the div as sub="…" and the sentence never rendered, through a browser drive that asserted the title. Nothing warned: not the compiler, not fli check, not the browser. A lowercase name that is not a known HTML/ARIA attribute or data-*, passed to a kit component that declares no such prop, is almost always a misremembered prop. Wants a compile-time or dev warning naming the component's props, or a fli check rule over kit components' declared exports. |
| <a id="fjs-1645"></a>FJS-1645 | litestone | **loadRows holds every record of its source in memory before writing, so a 200,000-row CSV of 12.6 MB peaks at 1.4 GB RSS: about 110 times the file** | open | 2026-10-03 | Measured in the transit stressor, 2026-10-03 (PLAN M1), the day loadRows landed: orders.csv (5,000 rows) 206 ms / 125 MB peak RSS into :memory:, 237 ms / 128 MB into a file; orders-wide.csv (200,000 rows) 4.85 s / 1.39 GB into :memory:, 4.97 s / 1.41 GB into a file. The landing target costs nothing (file vs memory within 3%); the memory is loadRows reading the whole CSV into records, then a second array of coerced rows, before the first batch is written. The load needs only the keys it has seen to decide a repeat, so it can take an async iterable of records (or read CSV text incrementally) and write as it goes. Phase 2's REST source arrives page by page and wants the same shape. |
| <a id="fjs-1643"></a>FJS-1643 | litestone | **A view's columns take no field attributes, so a view summing a @money column cannot say its result is money: the parser refuses 'revenue Int @money(field: currency)' inside a view with 'Expected IDENT, got @'** | open | 2026-10-03 | Seen in the transit stressor, 2026-10-03: a landing schema's view customerRevenue sums Order.amount (Int @money(field: currency)) per customer and currency. Declaring the view's revenue column '@money(field: currency)' fails the parse at the '@', so the column is a bare Int of minor units. Everything @money buys a model column is then lost on the view: the client's money formatting, the generated form/table rendering, and fli check's money-rendered-raw, which cannot see that a report printing revenue raw prints cents. A view is the read path T1 reports are built on, so every report over a money aggregate restates the currency by hand. The view's other access attributes (@@gate, @@allow) are accepted; only field-level ones are not. |

| <a id="fjs-1636"></a>FJS-1636 | cli | **fli new <name> --here ignores <name> in silence and names the app after the folder: package name, cli namespace and README all take the directory's name** | open | 2026-10-03 | Measured 2026-10-03, *transit stressor* Phase 0: `mkdir transit-next && cd transit-next && fli new transit --here --source local --full --yes` → `package.json` `"name": "@frontierjs/transit-next"`, `.fli.json` `defaultNamespace: "transit-next"`, `cli/src/routes/transit-next/`, and the closing line *✓ transit-next created*. | <a id="fjs-1624"></a>FJS-1624 | ui | **An editable <Json> always keeps its own undo stack — ⌘Z bound on its root, ↶/↷ buttons in its corner — and takes no way to hand history to the page, so a tree that is one editor among several over the same document is a second stack beside the page's** | open | 2026-10-02 | Found 2026-10-02, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*, Phase 2 (PLAN.md Q2). [Json.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa) under `editable` keeps `past`/`future` (HISTORY = 50), renders the two history buttons whenever `canEdit`, and `onGridKey` calls `onHistoryKey` for every keydown on the tree, with `preventDefault` and no opt-out. Its own comment says the binding is to the TREE so a page with its own history keeps the shortcut, but a page that owns history needs the shortcut INSIDE the tree too: dragonfly opens a <Json editable> in a grid cell beside a JSON text box, and both write one document. ⌘Z in the tree undoes the tree's last edit and announces it through `onchange` as a new write, which the page's stack records as one more step; the page's ⌘Z then lands in the tree as `somebody else's edit`, which the tree's `remember(draft)` pushes onto its own `past` — so each stack can undo the other's undo, and the ↶ in the cell walks a history that does not match the page's. Asked for: `history={false}` (no stack, no buttons, no key binding; every write still announced), so the caller's stack is the only one · [Json.mesa](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa) · **Measured** 2026-10-02 in dragonfly, Chrome 150 over CDP, a <Json editable> open in a grid cell beside the page's own history (which takes ⌘Z in the capture phase, so the tree's binding never sees it). The filed mechanism was a guess, and half of it is wrong; what happens is this. (1) Edit city to `Austin TX` in the tree, press the TREE's ↶: the city goes back, and the page records that as a new write. The page's Undo then puts `Austin TX` back — it undoes the tree's undo — and a second page Undo is needed to take the edit away: two presses for one edit, the first going the wrong way. (2) Edit, then page ⌘Z: the city goes back, and the tree's ↶ stays enabled; pressing it announces the document the page already holds, changes nothing, and disables itself — a dead press. The tree's `$: value` handler drops its draft when the announced document comes back, so a value arriving from outside is not pushed onto `past` here; the page's restore is invisible to the tree's stack, which is why (2) is a dead press and not a redo. Both are the same defect: two stacks over one document, and the caller cannot turn the tree's off. |
| <a id="fjs-1618"></a>FJS-1618 | cli | **fli new has no web-only template: its five are full-stack, api-only, widgets-only, site-only and extension-only, and there is no --no-api, so an app whose only surface is web/ is a full-stack scaffold taken apart by hand in 9 files plus 4 deletions, while app-layout's comment names web-only beside api-only as a whole project** | open | 2026-10-01 | **Measured** 2026-10-01, *dragonfly, the JSON-grid stressor*: `fli new dragonfly --here --source local --no-auth --no-example --no-deploy --yes`, then by hand: delete api/ (7 files), db/, cli/, .env and .env.example; package.json drops dev:api, start, db:migrate, db:backup and db:types, rewrites dev, and drops litestone and junction; tsconfig.json drops api/**; sierra.config.js drops the junction block; vite.config.js drops the API constant and the /api and /ws proxy; _module.mesa drops the sierra/resource status import and the live/offline badge; index.mesa drops fetch('/api/health'); main.js's comment says db/schema.lite exists; README and AGENTS.md point at api/, db/schema.lite and the litestone and junction guides, which are no longer installed. Sierra itself needs nothing: virtual-sierra.js wires no client when junction.url is unset and no registerSchemas without a schema, and the result boots with no console errors or failed requests. useApi is `!surfaceOnly` (new.md:2198), so only a surface-only template turns the API off, and web/ is not a surface-only option. A web-only template that writes no db/ also needs FJS-1617, or fli check stops grading it · [cli commands/project/new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) · [FJS-D107](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d107) · [FJS-1617](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1617) |

| <a id="fjs-1566"></a>FJS-1566 | portal | **Portal, the search stressor: a page that leaves <head> unclosed is captured as ok with 0 words, because the reader counts head as a skipped element and HTMLRewriter never ends it before <body>, so the whole body is skipped** | open | 2026-09-29 | Found seeding the knight persona (2026-09-29). https://lexi-lambda.github.io/blog/2019/11/05/parse-don-t-validate/ opens <head>, then <meta>/<link>/<script>, then <body> with no </head> (valid HTML: the end tag is optional, and <body> implies it). parsePage on the fetched 44 KB answered 0 words; the same page from <body> on, or the <article> alone, answered 4,080. Both readers share the shape: SKIP lists head and counts depth by skip++ / e.onEndTag(skip--), so an implied end never lowers it. Text: [parse.ts:131](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/parse.ts) (SKIP at :64); copy: [article.ts:107](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/article.ts) (SKIP at :28). The Page is written status ok, text and html null, words 0, so Read later opens an empty copy, the library search never finds the page, and nothing on the page says the capture failed. A fixed parser will not reach these rows by itself: capture.ts:71 counts words != null as having a copy, so a link check never recaptures a 0-word page; staleParses (reparse from snapshot) would, if PARSER is bumped. Fix: treat <body> (or the first body-only element) as the end of head, or drop head from SKIP and skip only its children (title, meta, link, script, style are already skipped or empty) |

| <a id="fjs-1517"></a>FJS-1517 | litestone · sierra | **A `@capability` column is charged to its grant whenever the payload NAMES it, `null` and unchanged included, and a generated create form names every column `make()` seeded — so on a model with one, the generated create form is refused for everybody who does not hold that column's grant, for a field the form does not even show.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Driven in Chrome** (jazzhr Phase 4, `.probe/phase4/drive.ts` step `draft-offer`): the recruiter's generated offer form, `<Form resource={offers} only={['title','salary','startOn','terms']}>` over `offers.make({ applicationId, jobId })`, answers *"Offer.signedName" requires the capability "Offer.signedName"* on submit, and no offer is written. `Offer.signedName` is `@capability` so that only the candidate's link may sign (the grid's column tier, the one spelling for *may sign and nothing else*). **Measured on a bare `$setAuth`** (`.probe/phase4/capability-null.ts`), a caller holding `Offer.create` + `Offer.update`: create without the key → ok; create with `signedName: null` → refused; update `{ title, signedName: null }` on a row whose `signedName` is already null → refused. Two halves: litestone's `_checkUpdate` (and the create path) grades a `@capability` key by presence, where *writing* the column is what the grant is about and a null create or an unchanged value writes nothing; and sierra's create posts the whole `make()` record, `only` narrowing what is SHOWN and not what is SENT (its patch sends only changed keys, which is why an edit form on the same model works). Either half fixed clears the form. jazzhr's page strips the column in `onsubmit`, which is the hand-written request the generated form exists to prevent · [capability.js](packages/litestone/src/plugins/capability.js) · [FJS-1516](#fjs-1516) · [FJS-1512](#fjs-1512) · [jazzhr `_Offers.mesa`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/web/src/routes/pipeline/_Offers.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-1516"></a>FJS-1516 | litestone | **On a `@@capabilities` model, a `@system` column the APPLICATION names on a write (`system: ['respondedAt']`) is charged to the CALLER's `Model.update` grant, so a principal who holds only a move cannot have the app stamp anything beside it: the candidate's accept, which the app timestamps, is a 403 asking the candidate for `Offer.update`.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured over HTTP** (jazzhr Phase 4, `.probe/phase4/offer.sh` step 27: the link holder's `accept` → 403 *"Offer.update" requires the capability "Offer.update"*) **and on a bare `$setAuth`** (`.probe/phase4/system-grid.ts`): a bearer holding `Offer.accept`, `Offer.decline` and `Offer.signedName` may `update({ data: { status: 'accepted', signedName } })` and may `update({ data: { status: 'declined' } })`, and the same two writes plus `respondedAt` with `system: ['respondedAt']` are each refused for `Offer.update`. On a model with no grid the same shape passes (`Candidate`: `phone` plus `forgottenAt` with `system: ['forgottenAt']`). Cause, read: `CapabilityPlugin._checkUpdate` (`plugins/capability.js:87`) sorts payload keys into `@capability` columns, move fields and *ungraded*, and charges any ungraded key to `Model.update`; it never reads `args.system`, so a column the app supplies counts as one the caller edits. `@system` means *the application writes it, not the caller* (litestone AGENTS.md § access words), and naming it on the call is how the app says so, which the gate and the column rules honour and the grid does not. The ways around it are each worse: hand the stranger `Offer.update`, or write the stamp as a second `asSystem()` write, which is a second trail line (measured over HTTP: `actorType: 'system'`, still `actorId` = the grant and `subjectId` = the candidate) and splits what should be one line binding the move, the sealed terms, the signature and the time. jazzhr takes the second · [capability.js `_checkUpdate`](packages/litestone/src/plugins/capability.js) · [FJS-1245](#fjs-1245) · [jazzhr offers.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/services/offers.service.ts) |

Available: push* (the string exists only in that advice, `tools/cli.js:901`). jazzhr's dev database was empty, so it was deleted and the history replayed (6 applied, clean). | <a id="fjs-1512"></a>FJS-1512 | litestone | **A sealing model has one freeze instant, the seal, so a column that must be written AT a later move and frozen from then — a signature, written by the accept after the terms were sealed at approve — has no word: `@immutable` freezes it before it can be written, a second `@seals` is refused, and the nearest spelling leaves the signature rewritable after acceptance.** | open | 2026-10-09 | **Measured**, jazzhr Phase 4 on a bare `$setAuth` (`.probe/phase4/sign.ts`, `sign-once.ts`). `Offer` is `approve: draft -> approved @seals` over `@immutable` terms (the seal must be at approve: sealing at accept leaves the terms editable while `sent`, Phase 0), and `signedName String? @capability`, which only the candidate's link holds. (1) **As declared**, signing and accepting are two writes, a signed offer can be declined (`declined`, `signedName: 'Ada Four'`), an accept needs no signature, and after `accepted` the link holder rewrites the signature (`'Ada One'` → `'Somebody Else'`, 200). (2) **`signedName @immutable`**: refused at `sent` with `SealedDocumentError` — the column froze at approve, before anybody could sign — and the one-write sign+accept is refused as *"Offer.accept" was refused by an @@allow/@@deny policy on update*, which names a POLICY for what is the seal's refusal. `fli db:explain @immutable` says *written once, at create, and never again by anybody*, which is not what it does on a sealing model. (3) **`accept: sent -> accepted @seals` beside approve's**: refused at parse (*already sealed by 'approve' … seals nothing*), correctly for what `@seals` means. (4) **What jazzhr ships**: one write carrying `status: 'accepted'` + `signedName` (graded as the `accept` move plus the `Offer.signedName` grant; the trail line has `transition: 'accept'`, the sealed terms and the name in one `after`), `@@check("(status = 'accepted') = (signedName IS NOT NULL)")` (refuses sign-alone, unsigned accept, sign+decline), and `@allow('write', status == 'sent')` on the field, which turns a later rewrite by the link holder into a **silent 200 no-op** — and `asSystem()`, a job or `fli tinker` still rewrites it (`'Mallory'`, measured). A candidate spelling: a move-scoped freeze, e.g. `signedName String? @immutable(after: accept)` or `@sealedBy(accept)`, frozen from the named move's target the way `@immutable` is from the seal · [jazzhr schema.lite `Offer`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite) · [seal.js](packages/litestone/src/core/seal.js) · [FJS-1482](#fjs-1482) · [PLAN.md § Q4](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · blocked by `FJS-D568` · A second domain, 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 3 Q5: an issued invoice's PDF is rendered after the seal, since Chrome has no place in the write transaction. pdf Bytes? @system @immutable on the sealing Invoice refused the first write (SealedDocumentError); without @immutable any later write replaced what was sent. Shipped instead: a child row, InvoiceDocument, whose columns are @immutable on a model that does not seal (frozen at create) with @unique invoiceId, so the table refuses a second render from any writer — a candidate answer for the signature as well · [pdf.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/pdf.test.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-1501"></a>FJS-1501 | mesa | **A prop whose default reads a plain `const` declared above it compiles clean and throws at render: `const a = 'x'` then `export let b = a` is *Cannot access 'a' before initialization*. Reduced through `renderComponent`: `const a = 'x'; export let b = a` **throws**. `const a = m.sep` (a read of an import) then `export let b = a` **throws**. `let n = 2; const a = n * 2; export let b = a` (a derived const) renders `4`, and `let a = 'x'; export let b = a` renders `x`. So a constant Mesa treats as plain is placed after the props, and one it treats as derived is not. | <a id="fjs-1494"></a>FJS-1494 | sierra | **`autoImport` has no precedence: a name found twice is a build error. A site cut from a template needs the client's block to win over the template's, and ksite's tag resolver is exactly that order — page-local `_X.md`, then `content/blocks/X.md`, then `content/blocks/X.svelte`, then the engine's `src/blocks/X.svelte`.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *ksite stressor* Phase 1, by reading ksite's `~/code/KOBAMI/SITES/ksite/site/src/scripts/resolve-component.js` against [auto-import-plugin.js `claim`](packages/sierra/src/build/auto-import-plugin.js#L100), which throws *Auto-import naming conflict*. This row is about TAGS. Layout names are their own namespace since [FJS-1493](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1493), with an ordered `markdownLayouts` list where a later directory wins, so `Trust.md` saying `layout: Trust` no longer collides with anything. The cost to the port is that ksite's single `src/blocks/` has to split into `src/blocks/` (tags) and `src/layouts/` (wrappers). Two tag cases have no spelling yet. **(a)** A client block overriding the template's, which is the seam the ksite stressor's Q5 turns on. **(b)** The page-local tier: `_Hero.md` beside a page, meant for that page alone. A co-located `_x.mesa` is never routed, and it is never auto-imported either. The ordered list `markdownLayouts` uses is one candidate for (a). |
| <a id="fjs-1490"></a>FJS-1490 | litestone · cli | **A fresh app's first `fli db:migrate` is refused if `fli dev` ran first: the dev server's `createClient` builds the tables by auto-DDL and records no migration, so the history is empty over a full database — and the refusal blames a `db push` nobody ran.** | open | 2026-10-06 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 1, on the app `fli new --full` made in Phase 0 and then booted with `fli dev`. `db/app.db`: `_litestone_migrations` has **0 rows**; `_litestone_meta` holds `autoMigrate.ddlHash = de910a6492dfff71`; the auth tables exist with 0 users. `db/migrations/main/20260929023442_initial.sql` (written by `fli new`, after its commit: [FJS-1479](#fjs-1479)) was never applied or recorded. The first `fli db:migrate` after a schema change: *✗ this database is not what its migration history builds: ? credential (in db, not in schema) … `db push` writes tables and no file, so a pushed change lives here and nowhere else*. No `db push` was run; the scaffold's own schema header says *Litestone generates DDL automatically on first start*, and `AGENTS.md` says *a schema change ships as a migration file, `fli db:migrate`* — the two instructions meet on day one and the second refuses what the first did. **The way out is right but unnamed**: the history file exists and matches, so `fli db:baseline` is the truthful repair, and the message offers `migrate create` + `baseline` (which writes a SECOND file for what `initial.sql` already builds) or `db reset`. Which is the bug is a ruling: auto-DDL in a dev process that has a migrations directory, or the scaffold not applying its own initial migration, or the refusal not recognizing auto-DDL's own hash · [../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/migrations/main/20260929023442_initial.sql](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/migrations/main/20260929023442_initial.sql) · [FJS-1479](#fjs-1479) · **Amended 2026-10-03, transit stressor Phase 0: `fli dev` is not needed — `fli new` alone leaves this state.** A throwaway `fli new probe-new --source local --full --yes --no-git --no-ci --no-deploy` (1.9 s) ends with `db/app.db` already built by auto-DDL (`_litestone_meta` only, no `_litestone_migrations`) and `litestone migrate status` → *1 pending* `…_initial.sql`. So every `--full` app starts with a full database and an empty history; one of the sub-commands it composes (`auth:install`, `notifications:install` or `make:scaffold User`) opens a client before step 12b writes the migration. Also on the real app: `fli db:migrate` (`migrate dev`) prints *the migration history already builds the schema — nothing to apply* and then *✓ Migration created and applied*, while `db:status` still says 1 pending — the success line is false. `fli db:baseline` repaired it (0 users, so nothing was lost) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/transit/PLAN.md) · Also on --create-only (Transit 2026-10-03, adding two virtual @from fields): litestone says 'the migration history already builds the schema — no migration needed' and the cli prints '✓ Migration file created in db/migrations/' under it, with no file written. · Recurred 2026-10-06 in the base44 stressor with no `fli dev`: `fli new` left `app.db` built and `20261006092046_initial.sql` pending; `fli db:baseline` repaired it. · Re-confirmed by the Fable audit 2026-10-05, run 4.1 (build from docs alone): `fli new --auth` alone leaves the initial migration pending over a built database, so the first `fli db:migrate` fails |

| <a id="fjs-1486"></a>FJS-1486 | litestone | **`@@capabilities` covers `create`, and a stranger holds no list — so a drop box cannot opt into the grid. On a model anybody may create, the grid refuses the stranger by name, and the grid is the only spelling for *the stranger may make this one move and no other*.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 1, on the discovery draft: `Application` is `@@gate("0")` + `@@capabilities`, with `@@allow('read'\|'update', id == auth().applicationId \|\| auth().id in job.teamIds)`. `$setAuth(null).application.create({…})` → `AccessDeniedError "Application.create" requires the capability "Application.create" and this caller holds no such grant`. **Why the model is on the grid at all**: a candidate (a bearer, level 0) must be able to WITHDRAW their own application and make no other move. A move's `@gate` only raises the update level ([FJS-1245](#fjs-1245)) and a policy cannot name a move, so the grid is the one spelling — and it works: a bearer holding `['Application.withdraw']` withdraws, the same bearer is refused `reject` by name, a bearer holding nothing is refused both. So *anybody may apply* and *the applicant may only withdraw* are both the client's rules, on one model, and cannot be declared together. **The ruling assumed the other case**: [`FJS-D146`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d146) keeps the gate as *you must be a member at all, which is the one thing a capability cannot say, because a stranger holds no list* — a stranger kept out by the gate. A drop box ([FJS-1244](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1244)) is the model a stranger is let INTO. **The workaround is invisible in the seed**: the app's principal resolver hands every anonymous caller a baseline `['Candidate.create', 'Application.create', …]`, so which strangers may create what lives in TypeScript. Candidate directions: `@@capabilities` that leaves `create` to the gate and policies, or a declared grant for the anonymous caller · [schema.lite § Application](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L389) · [FJS-D146](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d146) · [FJS-1244](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1244) · [FJS-1245](#fjs-1245) |

| <a id="fjs-1479"></a>FJS-1479 | cli | **`fli new` makes its `init` commit BEFORE it writes the initial migration, so a fresh app's first commit has no `db/migrations/` and `git status` is dirty on day one. The order is in the source: step 12 runs `git init`, `git add .`, `git commit -m "init"`, and step 12b runs `migrate create initial` after it. `fli check`'s `migration-history` and `deploy:doctor` both read the working tree, so neither sees it locally; it shows only in a clone (CI, a teammate, the deploy host). Fix: swap 12 and 12b, or `git add db/migrations && git commit --amend` after 12b · [project/new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) `12. git init` / `12b. The initial migration` · **Seen again 2026-10-03, transit stressor Phase 0:** `fli new transit --here --force --source local --full --yes` → one commit `init` (50 files), then `git status --short` → `?? db/migrations/` · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/transit/PLAN.md) · **Seen again 2026-10-04, notion stressor Phase 0:** `fli new notion --source local --full --yes` → `git status --porcelain` is `?? db/migrations/` straight after the scaffold; committed by hand, as linear (624893c) and jazzhr did. Fourth run in a row to hit it. · Recurred 2026-10-06 in the base44 stressor: `?? db/migrations/` straight after `init`. |
| <a id="fjs-1477"></a>FJS-1477 | junction | **`FJS-D505` lets a custom method call itself a READ (`{ method, read: true }`) and says nothing about a read that writes. `read` turns off both the idempotency cache and the announcement, so if it is put on a method that writes rows, a signed-in retry spends twice and the write is never announced. Nothing refuses it** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Reasoned, not measured.** `read: true` is not built yet: `READ_METHODS` in [idempotency.ts:31](packages/junction/src/core/idempotency.ts) is still `find`/`get` by name. The case is *Portal, the search stressor*: its pass search starts by spending a `Pass` (a create the schema grades, [FJS-1437](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1437)), then fans out to providers. That looks like a read, and it is not one. A custom method announces once per method, and a read is left out by name ([service.ts](packages/junction/src/core/service.ts), the announcement block), so marking it `read` silences the only announcement the spend gets. Idempotency does not matter for Portal, because a guest's `Idempotency-Key` is ignored (`principalOf` is null), but it does for any signed-in caller. **Fix:** the refusal half of `FJS-D505`, with no new word or option. In a `read: true` call, the first row write throws a 500 that names the method and the model, and the transaction rolls back. This cannot be checked at boot, since a method body cannot be read. The candidate seam is `$tapEvents(fn)`, decided where the announcement is decided, so there is one owner. Portal's search stays a write method, and `FJS-D514`'s `claims:` replaces its `gate: 0`. **Same area:** `PrincipalResolver`'s comment says it *Runs only for an AUTHENTICATED caller* ([litestone.ts:2857](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts)), but `withLitestoneDb` runs it for a guest on purpose. · `FJS-D505` · `FJS-D514` · after the `FJS-D514` build that is in the working tree · blocked by `FJS-D581` |

| <a id="fjs-1466"></a>FJS-1466 | litestone · junction | **`@@fts` has no way to say *match without the marks*. None of its tokenizers folds Greek accents or Hebrew points, and `tokenize:` takes a name and nothing else. So the fold is app code on both sides, a loader that writes a shadow column and a service that folds the query, and the declared `$search` directive, which does neither, answers `βίβλος` as typed with 0 rows.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *remnant, the legacy-port stressor*, Q3, on the real corpus (31,100 verses, 443,808 words). **Every existing route, one at a time:** (1) the four tokenizers: 0 of 6 probe words (`γενεσεως βιβλος Ιησου בראשית ברא אלהים`) against the text as written. Under the default `unicode61`, Hebrew points are separators, so `בְּרֵאשִׁ֖ית` indexes as the fragments `ב`, `אש`, `ית`. (2) A tokenizer argument: `tokenize: "unicode61 remove_diacritics 2"` is refused at parse (`Expected IDENT`), and it would not help: over NFD text in plain FTS5 it folds 2 of 6, because FTS5's diacritic table stops at U+0331, so the perispomeni (U+0342), the iota subscript (U+0345) and every Hebrew point and accent (U+0591–U+05C7) survive. (3) **The nearest declaration that exists**: `function fold(s: String): String { @@expr("replace(…)") }` plus `textFolded String @fold(text)`. It takes 199 nested `replace()`s (the 147 precomposed letters and 52 marks this corpus holds, 3.7 KB of SQL), and it agrees with the JS fold on all 443,808 words and hits 6 of 6. But it writes in 24.1 s against 8.8 s for a JS-folded column (9.0 s for `@@fts` over the raw text), it is keyed to this dataset's characters (a data release bringing one more is silently unfolded), and the query side still needs a JS twin: a schema function is inlined into DDL, not a callable SQL function, and bun:sqlite registers none. **What remnant shipped** ([fold.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/core/fold.ts): NFD, strip `\p{M}`, drop transliteration's syllable dots): five shadow columns written by the loader, and the query folded in [search.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/services/search.service.ts). Over HTTP every probe and all four of Q3's queries (`G0976`, `βίβλος`, `biblos`, `record`) find Matthew 1:1, median 1–8 ms. **What it costs:** nothing ties the shadow to its source, so a write of `text` that forgets `textFolded` leaves the index answering the old text. Adding it to a loaded corpus took a 46 s refold through the client, because SQL cannot compute the fold, while the same fold at load time is free. And **every other entrance misses**: `GET /api/verses?$search=βίβλος` → 0 rows, `biblos` → 0, and only a pre-folded `βιβλος` → Matthew 1:1. **And it is not only accents**: the corpus writes ί as U+1F77 in all 2,138 lemmas that carry it, while a Greek keyboard types U+03AF, which is canonically the same letter in different bytes. So an unnormalized index misses even a correctly accented query. **Candidate**: a word on the index, `@@fts([text], fold: marks)` (NFD, strip marks), with litestone keeping the shadow on every client write, as it already stamps `@default(ulid())` in JS, and folding the query inside `search()`. Then `$search`, a Resource's search and a console all agree. Measured for Greek and Hebrew only; by FTS5's own table the same holds for any mark above U+0331 (read, not measured) · [remnant PLAN.md Q3](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/PLAN.md) · [remnant search.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/test/search.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1460"></a>FJS-1460 | litestone | **The parse refusal *a relation cannot cross databases* is skipped when the target model is `@@external`: the same cross-database relation parses clean, emits a foreign key onto a table in another file, and every create then throws `no such table: main.Verse`** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *remnant, the legacy-port stressor* (`fjs-prototypes/remnant/db/corpus/homes.ts`, Q1's homes B and C). `StudyNote` in `main` with `verse Verse @relation(fields: [verseId], references: [verseId])`. **Home B**, `Verse` in `database corpus` + `@@db(corpus)`: refused at parse, *a relation cannot cross databases — 'StudyNote' is in database 'main' and 'Verse' is in 'corpus' … every create throws 'no such table'*, naming the fix. Exactly right. **Home C**, the same plus `@@external` on `Verse` (the corpus file built outside the app): no error, no warning; the DDL is `FOREIGN KEY ("verseId") REFERENCES "Verse" ("verseId")` inside `main`; `studyNote.create` → `SQLiteError: no such table: main.Verse`, which is the failure B's message predicts; `studyNote.findFirst({ include: { verse: true } })` answers the note with **no `verse` key and no error**; `verse.findFirst({ include: { notes: true } })` → `no such table: study_note`. `@@external` says Litestone does not create the table; it does not change which file the table is in, so the check applies unchanged. Fix: run the cross-database check on every relation whatever the target's DDL ownership, and make an `include` that cannot be served refuse rather than drop the key · `packages/litestone` parser, relation validation |
| <a id="fjs-1450"></a>FJS-1450 | junction | **`createApp({ principal })` takes one resolver and junction ships no way to compose two, so an app with a second claim source writes a plain function. The principal snapshot then reports it as *a hand-written resolver, which describes nothing*, with *Claims: None described*, which hides even a `bearerClaim` that describes itself when it is alone** | open | 2026-10-09 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *Portal, the search stressor* (PLAN.md Q8). The one slot takes `async (ctx, u) => ({ ...await passClaim(ctx, u), ...await shareClaim(ctx, u) })`. `junction principal --app api/src/app.ts --stdout` prints *`principal` — a hand-written resolver, which describes nothing about itself … a change to which model proves membership is invisible in this diff*, and *Claims: None described.* Both work (pinned, `shared.test.ts`, `passes.test.ts`). The review surface sees neither. The `describe()` shape is one `{ kind, model, subject, … }`, so even a hand-written `describe()` could state only one kind, and there is no kind for a claim proved by a signature rather than a row. Candidate, not coined: `principal: [passClaim, shareClaim]`, merged in order with a refusal on a claim name two resolvers both emit, each one described. · [junction tools/principal-snapshot.ts](packages/junction/tools/principal-snapshot.ts) · [portal api/src/core/principal.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/core/principal.ts) · [IDEAS/bearer-access.md](IDEAS/bearer-access.md) · `FJS-D113` · blocked by `FJS-D522` · Seen again 2026-10-05, notion stressor Phase 3 (PLAN Q3), and it is a case the FJS-D522 array refuses as ruled: membershipClaim and bearerClaim (a share-to-web link) must BOTH emit workspaceId under strategy row, the bearer because without the tenant claim the deny refuses every row (FJS-1731). Notion's rule is a precedence, not a merge: no session -> the link; a session on the named roster -> the membership plus the link's page when the link is for that workspace; a session on no named roster -> the link. D522's 'a claim name emitted by two refused' leaves no array that states it, so the plain function stays, and it describes nothing · fjs-prototypes/notion/api/src/core/principal.ts · fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q3/out.txt (runs 2-4) · Met concretely in the chatwoot stressor Q8 (2026-10-08): a second bearerClaim (a rating link, CsatLink) beside the widget's visitorClaim. Composed by hand in core/visitor.ts principal: one of session/visitor/link or a 400. describe() feeds the CORS allow-list and the socket-frame allow-list (declaredCallHeaders), so the composite has to merge both resolvers' headers and claims by hand or the second header is CORS-refused; and a description names ONE model, so junction principal cannot say the link's claims come from CsatLink · [chatwoot core/visitor.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/core/visitor.ts) · Met again 2026-10-08, ela stressor Phase 6 (Q11): a survey link (SurveyGrant, x-survey-token) beside Phase 5's widget grant (ResponseGrant, x-response-token), merged by hand in ela api/src/core/principal.ts · Hit again in sstime Phase 6: two bearers (the offer link and the ELA reader's Authorization: Bearer ela_…) composed by hand in core/principal.ts as ELA's is, with the ELA row copied out of the one BEARER slot. | <a id="fjs-1438"></a>FJS-1438 | litestone | **`@@allow('update', …)` grades the row BEFORE the write, so a move of a foreign key into somebody else's parent is admitted unless the model also declares `'post-update'` — an operation the catalog does not list. *Portal, the search stressor* had the hole twice: its new Bookmark filed into a friend's collection, and its Phase 1 DomainRule moved into a friend's SHARED lens, where his grantees' searches read it** | open | 2026-10-09 | **Measured** 2026-09-28 in *Portal, the search stressor* (PLAN.md § Bookmarks), at the litestone layer and again over HTTP. `@@allow('create', collectionId == null \|\| collection.ownerId == auth().id)` refuses a create into Joe's collection; the same predicate in `@@allow('update', ownerId == auth().id && (…))` admitted `PATCH collectionId → Joe's` (200, stored), because `update` compiles to a WHERE over the row as it IS. The move is graded only by `'post-update'` (`checkPostUpdatePolicy`, `client.js`), which the parser accepts (`VALID_POLICY_OPS`) and `catalog.snapshot.md` omits: `@@allow`'s arity reads `'read'\|'create'\|'update'\|'delete'\|'all'`, and litestone's AGENTS.md sends an agent to that table for every word. **Worse, the app's older model had it since Phase 1**: DomainRule's `@@allow('update', ownerId == auth().id)` let me move my own rule into Joe's golf lens (`lensId`), and a lens is read by everyone Joe shares it with, so my rule re-ranked their searches. Pinned by removing the one line from a schema copy: red without it, green with it. [FJS-378](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-378) fixed exactly this for the GENERATED tenancy denies and said why (`'all'` covers `post-update`, a bare `'update'` does not); a hand-written update policy over a relation path is still the unguarded shape, and nothing says so: `advise`, `fli check` and `test:access` all pass it. Candidates: list `post-update` in the catalog; an `advise` rule for an `update` policy (or a `create` policy) naming `x.col` through a foreign key the caller may write, with no `post-update` beside it; or grade `update` against both images · [portal api/test/bookmarks.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/test/bookmarks.test.ts) · [portal db/schema.lite](../fjs-prototypes/portal/db/schema.lite) · [FJS-378](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-378) · **Third app, 2026-10-09, lago stressor.** A child's `@@allow('update', check(org, 'update') && plan.orgId == orgId)` admitted an admin of two orgs moving B's Charge onto A's plan (and its metric, a ChargeTier onto A's charge, a Subscription onto A's plan): the policy read the row before the write. `@@allow('post-update', …)` refuses all four, pinned by lago-access.test.ts § a child's organization is its parent's, and the mutation control kills each post-update drop. `fli db:explain @@allow` still prints the signature `('read'\|'create'\|'update'\|'delete'\|'all', …)` with no post-update, and says nothing of which row update reads · [lago-access.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/lago-access.test.ts) · [mutate-access.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/mutate-access.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-1440"></a>FJS-1440 | junction · litestone | **`$search` reaches FTS5 `MATCH` as the caller wrote it, so an ordinary word is query syntax: `mini-redis` answers 500 *no such column: redis*, `c++` 500 *fts5: syntax error near "+"*, `rust:` 500 *no such column: rust* — and the 500's message repeats a word of the search** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28 in *Portal, the search stressor* (PLAN.md § Bookmarks) over HTTP, `GET /api/bookmarks?$search=…` on a model with `@@fts([title, note, url, tags], tokenize: trigram)`. `mini` and `redis` alone match. A hyphen is FTS5's column filter, `+` and `:` its operators, and `table.search()` passes the string to `MATCH` unchanged, so every search box built on the documented `$search` directive 500s on a hyphenated word, a language name or a trailing colon. It is a 500 and not a 400, so it is logged as a server fault, and the message names part of what the person typed. In an app whose promise is that a query is never kept (Portal's Q3), that is a search term in the error log. **Cleared, measured**: a phrase in quotes does survive the URL — the encoder doubles the quotes (`""mini-redis""`) and the parser takes one pair off — so the literal form is reachable, only never the default. Portal's `bookmarks` service quotes each word in a `before: find` hook. Fix: the compiler owns this setter, so for a member target it can emit the write followed by `$$set_pick($$runtime.get($$sig_pick))`, which is the `obj = obj` the docs ask for. | <a id="fjs-1420"></a>FJS-1420 | junction · sierra | **`FJS-D13` sends a stream of records to a channel, *where the pipeline produced each frame*, and a channel cannot carry ONE call's progressive answer. `announce()` runs once per call. And a live store refuses a frame that is not a row. So the only spelling that paints a result as it arrives, into a live store and through the pipeline, is to write the result as rows, which for *Portal, the search stressor* is keeping the query.** **Measured** 2026-09-27 (PLAN.md Q2), five fake providers (40 ms, 40 ms, 600 ms, flaky, dead), the page painting each as it lands. No browser package reads `text/event-stream`, and `EventSource` cannot carry a bearer token, so the browser half is fetch, a reader and the frame format by hand (≈ 40 lines). It is the one spelling that reaches only the connection that asked, and the one that hears a hang-up (`onDisconnect`). **B**, each merged hit a `SearchHit` row in a live `list()`: first hit ≈ 60 ms, 46 announcements and 20 rows per search, each row holding the query in its title and URL, on disk until swept (`@@expires` schedules nothing). Every frame reached the person's other tab, query included (41 of 41 at the API, and a second Chrome tab refused tab one's frames with *ignored an update with no id*). Every live `Usage` store refuses every frame with that warning, so a non-row payload can land in no store. **The fork, written down:** stream outside the pipeline (A), or write to stream (B). C is neither: its frames leave the pipeline after the first. The same ask from another side as `FJS-1403`, `FJS-1410` and `FJS-1412`: a method's answer has no declaration, so it cannot say it arrives in parts or that it must not be kept. Candidate shapes, not coined: a method that answers in frames over the calling connection, each through `protect()`, with the client exposing them to a store as a sequence of one result; or a connection on the service context, so an after-return frame can at least be addressed | open | 2026-10-05 | [portal PLAN.md Q2](../fjs-prototypes/portal/PLAN.md) · [portal fakes/progressive.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/progressive.ts) · [portal fakes/drive-web.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/fakes/drive-web.ts) · [DECISIONS.md FJS-D13](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d13) · `FJS-1416` · `FJS-1419` · `FJS-1403` · `FJS-1410` · `FJS-706` · The reader half of spelling A is closed by FJS-1581: readEvents in @frontierjs/sierra/fetch, over toolbelt's /sse, which ctx.sse() writes with; portal's streamA now reads through it. The other half of *a service context names no connection*: a page room has to be entered by a service call (a client cannot join a channel), and that call can name only the caller's SESSION, so the app joins every live connection signed in with it. gus has the page open in one tab of two; `pages.enter` joined 2 connections, the other tab received `presence:sync` for a page it never opened, and the room's roster shows him twice on every screen.
