| <a id="fjs-1333"></a>FJS-1333 | mesa | **A 1,000-row keyed table holds about 2.4 MB of heap in Mesa against 0.24 MB for the same table written by hand — roughly 2.5 KB per row, ten times the floor.** | open | 2026-09-24 | **Measured** by `bun run bench`: `Runtime.getHeapUsage` after a forced GC, idle and then after `#run`, +2,460 KB for Mesa and +243 KB for the floor, the same on a 1-run and a 10-run pass. Each row is one `makeBlock` with its own row signal and four effects (`data-id`, `class:danger`, the id text, the label text), and the delegated handlers are closures per row. Not a leak: five create/clear cycles return within ~70 KB of idle (floor ~40 KB). Unexplained and worth reading on the same pass: the Mesa page idles ~2.3 MB above the floor before any row exists, with 25 KB of JavaScript against 5 KB. A heap snapshot per row is the next measurement; nothing gates it, since heap moves with the engine · by hand |
| <a id="fjs-1318"></a>FJS-1318 | ui | **`CommandPalette` cannot show a server's search results: it takes no query callback, and it ranks whatever `items` it is given against the typed words AGAIN, so a hit made on text the row does not show is dropped and the person reads *No results*.** | open | 2026-09-23 | **Driven in a real browser**, linear Phase 6 (Chrome, `vite dev`). linear's ⌘K is fed by `GET /api/search` ([search.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/linear/api/src/services/search.service.ts): FTS5 over issues, comments and projects, graded per model). The palette declares `items`, `placeholder`, `groupOrder`, `emptyText` and `onclose`, and has nothing that reports what was typed. So [linear `SearchPalette.mesa`](../fjs-prototypes/linear/web/src/components/SearchPalette.mesa) finds the palette's input in the DOM by its private class (`.fjs-cp-input`), listens for `input`, asks the server and replaces `items`. The palette then passes those items through `rank(items, query, { keys: ['label', 'group', 'sub'] })` and draws only what that keeps. **Measured:** *throttling*, a word in ENG-3's description: the server answered **1** hit (`Rate-limit the search endpoint`), and the palette drew **0 rows**, *No results for "throttling"*. *flamingo* (inside a comment's first 80 characters) and *load-test* (in a title) drew 2 and 1, because the word happens to be in the label. So the palette works for exactly the hits that did not need a server. The same filter also hides every static command (*Go to …*) while a server query is open, and it re-orders the server's bm25 ranking by the device's fuzzy score. Worth: an async source, `search={(q, signal) => Promise<items>}`, whose answer is drawn as given, ranked by whoever produced it. Or at minimum an `onquery` callback and a `filter={false}`. **`onquery` landed 2026-09-30** (ui `CHANGES.md`) and both apps take it instead of the class; what is still open is the re-ranking · [CommandPalette.mesa](packages/ui/components/overlay/CommandPalette.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-1271"></a>FJS-1271 | litestone | **A `File` ref stores the origin it was uploaded against, so every stored file points at the old host the moment an app moves — and the key that would survive the move is sitting in the same JSON.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Measured** while moving `example`'s drives off the dev ports. The ref in `product_image.file` is `{"key":"storage/ProductImage/2e6246fc4edd.png","provider":"local","endpoint":null,"publicBase":"http://localhost:8110",…}` — the key is relative and portable, and `publicBase` beside it is not. Start the same app on another port and every photograph 404s or refuses the connection while the bytes sit untouched under `storage/`; the drive that found it read `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:8110` from a browser whose API was answering on 7110. **A port is the cheap version of the real case, which is a DOMAIN**: an app that moves from `shop.example.com` to its own domain, or gets a CDN in front of storage, orphans every uploaded file in every row, and no migration can be written for it without parsing JSON in SQL. **Nothing reports it** — the rows read fine, the API answers, and only a browser fetching the url finds out. **What it costs today**: `example`'s drives cannot move the API port, which is why they move only the dev server. **The likely answer is that `publicBase` is DERIVED at read time** from the provider's current config plus `key`, which is § V.5 exactly — a stored copy of something the config already knows. Against it: an object genuinely served from a URL the current config no longer describes, which is an argument for keeping `endpoint` per row and not for keeping the app's own public base. **The derived path already exists and the stored value shadows it**: `urlFor` is *if a publicBase was stored in the ref, use it* ([storage/index.js:44](packages/litestone/src/storage/index.js)) and falls back to `endpoint` + `key` only when the ref carries none, so the smallest change is which of the two wins. It is written at upload in [file.js:234](packages/litestone/src/storage/file-storage.js). Needs a ruling before a change · found by `verify:cart` and `verify:catalog` |
| <a id="fjs-1269"></a>FJS-1269 | notifications | **Nothing in the framework can reach a phone, and the stated reason for building it here — *a subscription is a row* — is the one thing an app already gets for free. The reason to build it is the 350 lines of RFC that every app otherwise writes identically and gets silently wrong.** | open | 2026-09-22 | Q5 of the connectteam stressor, answered ([PLAN.md § Q5](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/PLAN.md)). The brief refuses both shipped transports in one sentence — *if the reminder is an email nobody opens, or a badge inside an app that is not running, the person is late and we have paid for a system that did nothing* — so this product is the first on the list that is WRONG rather than lesser without push. **Ground state re-probed 2026-09-22:** `ls packages/notifications/drivers/` is `email.ts`, `inapp.ts`; `grep -rn 'vapid\|web-push' packages/ --include=*.ts --include=*.js` is **0 hits across the whole framework**. **What was free, and it is worth saying first.** The `transports:` seam cost nothing: anything with `send(recipient, message, app)` registers, a registered driver beats a built-in name, a transport with no driver is refused BEFORE any delivery rather than at the moment one is needed, and a `push:` formatter beside `inApp:` on a `defineNotification` needs no adapter. Nothing in the app is a workaround for this package. **What 2.17 and `ecosystem-gaps.md` § 16 both get wrong.** Both argue that making the subscription a Model *is the whole argument for building it here rather than leaving it to an app*, because it then inherits `@@gate`, `@encrypted` on the keys and Invariant 7's redaction, where *every hand-rolled version is a plain table with none of that*. **Measured: a hand-rolled version in a FrontierJS app is not a plain table.** `model PushSubscription` was declared in the app's own `db/schema.lite` and got all three — `@encrypted` hides `p256dh`/`authSecret` from every caller read and returns PLAINTEXT to `asSystem()`, which is what a sender needs and is the one of the five visibility words that fits; the audit trail carries `[redacted]` (see [FJS-1266](#fjs-1266) for the half of that which is a defect); and the gate is the app's to state. That argument is an argument for litestone, which the app already has. **What actually costs, measured.** `api/src/core/push.ts` is **~350 lines** implementing RFC 8291 (aes128gcm over ECDH-ES), RFC 8188 (the body framing) and RFC 8292 (VAPID): an ephemeral P-256 keypair per send, **four HKDF derivations** whose `info` strings must be byte-exact including their NULs, a `salt‖rs‖idlen‖keyid` header, the `0x02` last-record delimiter, and a JWT whose signature must be raw `r‖s` where node emits ASN.1 DER by default. **Every one of those fails the same way: the push service does not hold the key, cannot tell a bad derivation from a good one, and answers `201 Created`.** A wrong build is indistinguishable from a phone in a tunnel, which is why the app's own test decrypts what it encrypts with a device private key rather than asserting a length — every cheaper check passes on garbage. **Three more things an app must build that the driver interface does not suggest.** (1) **A driver that WRITES**: a subscription expires with no notice and the only signal is a 404/410 on a send, so the transport that delivers is the one that must prune; `transport`/`send`/`shutdown` hints at none of it. (2) **A fan-out inside the fan-out**: `inApp` writes one row per recipient, but a person is N devices with N key pairs and N independent 410s, and `Recipient` — whose design note is *a Recipient is not a User* — has nowhere to put them. (3) **`asSystem()` on every send**, because the keys are `@encrypted` and the sender reads everybody's rows, which is [FJS-1216](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1216)'s cost once more: every delivery is filed against the application rather than against anybody. **The boundary § 16 says to settle first, with a measurement.** *A push payload is a read that leaves the building via a vendor, so `@guarded` columns must not be in it.* It cannot be enforced at the driver: the driver necessarily holds `asSystem()` to decrypt the subscription keys at all, so it has full visibility by construction. The check belongs where the payload is BUILT — the notification's `push:` formatter — or nowhere. **Scope note:** measured to the edge, no vendor send, per that run's scope call. The request is built completely — URL, headers, VAPID JWT, encrypted body — and handed to an injected `deliver`; the default is a plain `fetch`, since a push service is ordinary HTTPS with no SDK and no account, which is what makes this buildable by an app at all. · `IDEAS/overview.md` 2.17 · `IDEAS/ecosystem-gaps.md` § 16 · [FJS-1270](#fjs-1270) · [FJS-1266](#fjs-1266) · [FJS-1234](#fjs-1234) · driven in `api/test/reminders.test.ts` |
| <a id="fjs-1266"></a>FJS-1266 | litestone | **An `@encrypted` column reaches the audit trail two different ways depending on WHO wrote the row — `[redacted]` for a system write and ABSENT for everybody else's — so a trail reader cannot tell *the column was not supplied* from *it was supplied and you may not see it*.** | open | 2026-09-22 | Found on connectteam Phase 6 ([PLAN.md § Q5](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/PLAN.md)) and driven as a pair: two creates of one model with identical data, one through `asSystem()` and one as the ordinary USER(4) caller who owns the row. `model PushSubscription` declares `p256dh String @encrypted`, `authSecret String @encrypted` and `@@log(audit)`. The system write's `after` carries `"p256dh":"[redacted]"` and `"authSecret":"[redacted]"` — **Invariant 7, exactly as promised**. The owner's write has neither key in `after` at all. The trail is built from the row as the WRITER could read it back, and the visibility table's `@encrypted` row is *caller writes, caller does not read* — so for every principal below the system client the column is not redacted, it is missing. **Not a nulls-are-dropped artefact:** `userAgent`, a plain nullable column the same caller left unset, is in the same snapshot as `null`. The consequence is that the one question a trail exists to answer about a credential column — *was a device key written, or rotated, here* — has no answer. Absent means *not supplied* on the system path and *supplied, hidden* on every other, with nothing in the row to separate them, and a reader has no way to know which path wrote a given row. Invariant 7's promise is that such a column *logs as `[redacted]`*, which is a claim about presence as much as about the value. **What would close it:** build the snapshot from the row as WRITTEN and then redact, rather than from the row as read back. The redaction already exists and is already applied on the system path — this is which value it is applied to. · driven in `api/test/reminders.test.ts` § 3 · [FJS-D349](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d349) is the other direction of the same trail (a log that reveals too much) |
| <a id="fjs-1254"></a>FJS-1254 | sierra | **A `type` declared in `db/schema.lite` reaches the browser's own schema registry and the UI realm cannot address it: a Resource naming one silently renders the form for the SERVICE'S model instead — a different field list, and nothing warns.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Driven in a browser**, connectteam Phase 5. Every write in this app that matters is a custom method whose payload is not a row of any model: `shifts.assign` takes `type AssignShift` (the trio a manager types, who, where and an availability override) because the model's two instants are `@system` and three overlap rules run on the way in. The shape IS in the browser — `fli db:jsonschema` emits every `type` block into `$defs` beside the models, and `virtual:sierra` registers the whole table, confirmed by reading the dev server's own payload: `AssignShift` and `GeoFix` are in it. `createResource('shifts', { model: 'AssignShift' })` + `<Form>` then rendered **`Shift`'s** form: `siteId`, `employeeId`, `ruleId`, `onDate`, `startLocal`, `endLocal`, `status` — `ruleId` exists only on the model, `ignoreAvailability` only on the type. No warning from the resource, none from `<Form>`, and `fli check` green — `resource-model-miss` fires on a name that resolves to nothing, not on one that resolves to something else. **So the declaration a custom method is validated against on the server is invisible to the screen that fills it in**, and the labels, required flags and option lists get written out by hand in the page — the exact second copy the framework's one rule exists to prevent. Junction already takes `methods: [{ method: 'assign', input: 'AssignShift' }]` and validates the body against it (measured: a bad `onDate` comes back `400 [{ field, message }]`, rendered under the control), so the two ends of the same declaration disagree only about the browser. Either a Resource should be able to name a `type` and generate from it, or naming one should be refused by name · [schedule/index.mesa](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/web/src/routes/schedule/index.mesa) · [schema.lite § What a screen sends that is not a row](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/db/schema.lite) |
| <a id="fjs-1258"></a>FJS-1258 | litestone | **`createClient()` answers the hand-written `LitestoneClient`, whose every model is `unknown`, and nothing ties an app's generated `db/schema.d.ts` to it — so `db.employee` and `db.asSystem().employee` alike leave TypeScript, and the app's own `fli typecheck` gate fails on the lines the docs tell you to write.** | open | 2026-10-09 | **Corrected 2026-09-27** by re-probe: the hand-written client's `asSystem()` already answers `LitestoneClient` ([index.d.ts:773](packages/litestone/src/index.d.ts)), not `AnyLitestoneClient`; a probe in connectteam gets `TS18046: 'db.employee' is of type 'unknown'` on the plain client too, so `asSystem()` is not the cause. connectteam's `core/db.ts` calls `createClient({ … })` untyped; `createClient<TClient>` exists but no doc or scaffold passes the generated type, and the generated `schema.d.ts` declares its own `createClient` that nothing imports. The fix is to make the generated types reach `db` (a scaffolded generic, or module augmentation), not to retype `asSystem()`. Original measurement, connectteam Phase 5: `db.asSystem().employee.findFirst({ … })` is `TS2571: Object is of type 'unknown'`, and spreading a row read that way is `TS2698: Spread types may only be created from object types` — two errors in the gate `bun run check` runs, from two ordinary uses of the one escape hatch the framework documents by name. The app's domain modules already carry `type Db = any` at every such boundary and the workaround here is another cast, which is the point: the bypass is not rare or discouraged — a seed uses it, a claim resolver uses it, every conflict check in this app uses it because a check must see rows the caller may not. `AnyLitestoneClient`'s comment says why it is shaped this way — a generated client declares its own `$schema` and naming a shape here would make the generated flavour unassignable ([FJS-018](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-018)) — so the fix is not to widen that interface but for `asSystem()` to answer the SAME type as its receiver, which is what it does at runtime. `db/schema.d.ts` is generated and holds exactly that type **Amended 2026-09-28, remnant (second caller, the service side)**: junction's `$.db` is the same hand-written `LitestoneClient`, so in a service `$.db.verse.findUnique({ … include })` answers `{}` and `fli typecheck` refuses every field read off it (14 errors in one service). The app's generated `db/schema.d.ts` reaches neither. remnant states the shapes it reads and casts, in [verses.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/services/verses.service.ts) and [search.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/services/search.service.ts) · [index.d.ts:525](packages/litestone/src/index.d.ts) · [core/auth.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/core/auth.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D495` · base44 Q6 (2026-10-09): a generated nightly job on the module client's `db.asSystem()` failed typecheck with `system.todo` of type unknown (apps/studio/q6-todo-copy, close-stale-todos.job.ts), and the writer's fix call cast it: `(db.asSystem() as unknown as { todo: TodoAccessor }).todo`. A writer pays a fix call per job for this, and the cast hides the accessor's real shape from the type checker. |
| <a id="fjs-1260"></a>FJS-1260 | cli | **`fli make:service <Model>` writes a service that contradicts junction's own documented shape on four counts — and its plural disagrees with `fli make:resource`'s, so the two generators for one model produce a page that 404s.** | open | 2026-10-06 | **Driven**, connectteam Phase 5, nine generated services. The template is `const plural = lower + 's'` and emits `export default createBaseService({ name: plural, model: plural, hooks: {} })`, against a package whose AGENTS.md says the file is `<plural>.service.ts` exporting `create<Plural>Service()` over a PascalCase singular `model:`, and against the scaffold's own `users.service.ts`, which is written that way. Four disagreements: `export default` where the autoloader documents a factory (*a `*.service.ts` exporting no `create…Service` factory is an authoring error that refuses `start()`* — it does not, measured: the app booted with all nine); `model: 'sites'` where the word is `Site`; a file named for the singular; and printed next steps naming `api/src/server.ts` and `app.services.register(…)`, neither of which exists in a `fli new --full` app, where services are autoloaded from `junction.config.js`. **The plural is the one with a cost.** `fli make:service ClockEntry` serves `/api/clockEntrys`; `fli make:resource ClockEntry`, run five minutes later, writes `createResource('clockEntries')` and tells you the service file is `clockEntries.service.ts`. Measured: `GET /api/clockEntries` → `404 Service 'clockEntries' not found`, `GET /api/clockEntrys` → `200`. Two generators, one model, one letter apart, and the failure lands in the browser rather than at either command **Re-measured 2026-09-28, remnant**: given the service's own name, as junction's file naming reads it (`fli make:service verses`), it writes `name: 'versess', model: 'versess'`, and still prints the `api/src/server.ts` next step. Not re-filed · [make/service.md:38](packages/cli/commands/make/service.md) · [junction AGENTS.md](packages/junction/AGENTS.md) **linear, 2026-09-22 (Phase 2), a third app:** `fli make:service Issue` wrote `issue.service.ts` holding `export default createBaseService({ name: 'issues', model: 'issues' })` and printed *Register in api/src/server.ts* — a file this scaffold does not have, since services are autoloaded; `fli make:resource Issue` then named `issues.service.ts` for the same model. Rewritten by hand as `issues.service.ts` in the scaffold's `createIssuesService()` shape, likewise `comments` and `teamMembers`. · Sighted again 2026-10-03 (Transit Phase 1, C2): fli make:service SyncRun wrote syncRun.service.ts exporting default createBaseService({ name: 'syncRuns', model: 'syncRuns' }) and printed the api/src/server.ts register step; fli make:resource SyncRun then said the file must be syncRuns.service.ts. Same for Reject. Both rewritten by hand to the users.service.ts shape. · Seen again 2026-10-05, notion stressor Phase 3: five services, unchanged (page.service.ts, model: 'pages', register in api/src/server.ts). Deleted and written in the scaffold's users.service.ts shape · fjs-prototypes/notion/api/src/services/ · Sighting 2026-10-06, base44 Phase 4: `fli make:service --auto --yes` over an Oracle app wrote singular files (`order.service.ts`) with `export default createBaseService({ name: 'orders', model: 'orders' })` — no create…Service factory, which junction refuses at start — and wrote `user.service.ts` and `notification.service.ts` beside the scaffold's `users.service.ts`. base44's builder writes the one-line services in AGENTS.md's shape itself instead |
| <a id="fjs-1242"></a>FJS-1242 | cli | **`fli check` runs its rules over a schema litestone itself refuses to parse and exits 0 — so `bun run check` is green on a schema that cannot migrate and kills the app at boot.** | open | 2026-10-09 | **Measured** in the connectteam stressor on one line — `kioskPin String @hashed @guarded` on a model, which litestone refuses with *@hashed conflicts with @guarded*. Against that file: `litestone validate` errors, `litestone migrate create` refuses, and the app's own boot throws (`_buildEnv`, `testing.js:141`). `fli check` prints `⚠ 31 rule(s) checked · 9 warning(s)` and **exits 0**, saying nothing — it reads the schema well enough to run `transition-methods` over every move in it. `fli check --list` holds 61 rules and none asserts that the schema parses: `schema-in-memory` grades whether the committed artefacts describe the running models, which is a different question and passes here. **The only thing that failed `bun run check` was the scaffold's generated `api/test/access.test.ts`**, which calls `parse()` for its own reasons; with that one file replaced by a trivial passing test, the whole gate exits 0 — 31 rules, lint clean, typecheck clean, 1 pass. Every scaffold's `AGENTS.md` names `fli check` as the Data-realm gate and `bun run check` as *the same gate CI runs*, and the generated `ci.yml` runs only that. Same family as [FJS-1228](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1228) — the gate does not read the UI realm either — one realm over, and this is the realm `fli check` exists for. **A second instance, a different class of error and a worse ending.** `@@gate("4.6.5.6")` violates the ladder's non-decreasing rule. Parse does not catch it — the gate is validated at plugin init — so `litestone migrate create` wrote a migration and `fli db:migrate` **applied it**, creating 23 tables for a schema `createClient` then refuses with *levels must be non-decreasing in R.C.U.D order*. `litestone validate` catches it and exits 1, because it is the one tool that runs both passes; `fli check` exits 0 again. So the honest statement of this row is not *`fli check` misses one rule* but **there is no single command in the gate that answers "will this schema run"** — and the one that does (`litestone validate`) is not in `bun run check`, not in the generated `ci.yml`, and is named in no scaffold's `AGENTS.md`. Adding it to the `check` script is a one-line fix. Found by the connectteam stressor, `fjs-prototypes/connectteam/PLAN.md` Phase 1. **Third instance, 2026-09-27, *Portal, the search stressor* Phase 1** — a third class: `@@unique([ownerId, lensId, domain])` over an optional `lensId`, which litestone refuses as *cannot be enforced* (and names the fix). `fli check` exits 0 (33 rules, 2 warnings); `fli advise` and `createClient` refuse; `bun run check` went red only through the scaffold's `access.test.ts` calling `makeTestClient` — the same single thread. **And the one-line fix this row proposes is the wrong line, measured:** `litestone validate` is *which STORED rows this schema would now refuse* — it opens `db/app.db`, so on a schema ahead of the database (every change before its migration) it exits 1 with *no such table* per new model, and with no `app.db` (CI; it is gitignored) it creates an empty one and exits 0. The command that answers *will this schema run* without a database is `fli advise`: exit 1 on the refusal above, exit 0 on the fixed schema with warnings. Portal's `check` script now opens with `fli advise &&` ([package.json](../fjs-prototypes/portal/package.json)). · **Seen again 2026-10-09, lago stressor, twice in one session.** `fli check` reported 38 rules, 4 warnings, exit 0 on a schema `litestone migrate` refused at line 692 (`@unit(%)`, Unexpected character '%'), and again on a schema refused at line 14 (`driver logger`, after FJS-D661 renamed it). The second is every app scaffolded before 2026-10-08 16:23: `bun run check` stays green until the migrate · [lago PLAN.md § Phase 1](../fjs-prototypes/lago/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1237"></a>FJS-1237 | litestone | **A validator whose type the column cannot hold parses clean, so `@gte(1)` on a `Boolean` reaches the read schema as `{"type":"boolean","minimum":1}`.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found beside [`FJS-1236`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1236) and kept apart from it, because that row is about a rule that can never be REACHED and this is about one that can never be MEANT. **Measured**, each parsing `valid: true` with no error and no warning: `flag Boolean @gte(1)`, `name String @gte(1)`, `n Int @length(2, 5)`. The first reaches a generated form as a numeric minimum on a checkbox. `@@fts` over an encrypted column and a fractional `@default` on `@scale` are both already ruled — the first in `advise.js`, the second in the parser — so the question is which side of [`FJS-721`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md)'s boundary this falls: nothing SQLite refuses, so `advise.js`, unless the pairing is decidable enough to be a parse error. The catalog already states each validator's argument shape (`core/catalog.js`), so the type it applies to is derivable rather than a second hand-written table. **One neighbour of this is now answered and it narrows what is left**: a value rule on a RELATION is `value-rule-on-a-relation` in `advise.js` ([`FJS-1239`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1239)), which leaves the scalar-against-scalar mismatch as the whole of this row. |
| <a id="fjs-1230"></a>FJS-1230 | ui | **`slot="name"` is honoured by exactly one component in the kit and silently dropped by every other — so a `<Card>` given a header and a footer renders neither, and the screen looks finished rather than broken.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found when a user of the calendly stressor reported a dead end on the Availability screen. The real damage was one layer past it: **that page had lost both of its headings AND both of its forms**, and nothing anywhere said so — no warning, no console message, no build error. The add-window form and the add-exception form simply were not in the DOM, so the screen read as an empty state that had been designed. [Card.mesa](packages/ui/components/layout/Card.mesa) declares `header` and `footer` as snippet props and renders `{@render header?.()}`; **it references `$slots` zero times**, so `<h2 slot="header">` and `<div slot="footer">` are dropped. [Form.mesa:928-932](packages/ui/components/forms/Form.mesa) does both — `{@render actions?.()}` `{:else if $slots.actions}<slot:actions />` — and is **the only component in `packages/ui/components` that does**; measured across every component declaring a snippet prop, `Form` is 1 and all the rest are 0. **The kit already knows this is a real trap, and fixed it in one direction only.** Form's own comment: *Two spellings because two protocols reach a component and the rest of this kit uses the other one: every other container here (Card, Table, EmptyState, SectionHeader) takes its trailing content as a snippet prop, so a caller who wrote `{#snippet actions()}` over a form got no buttons and no complaint.* That is this bug with the operands swapped, it was worth a code change when it pointed at Form, and the general case was left open. **Apps are actively taught the dropped spelling.** Every Resource `fli scaffold` generates ends with `<Button slot="actions" …>` inside a `<Form>` — correct, because Form is the exception — so a developer reads `slot=` as the kit's idiom and writes `<h2 slot="header">` on the next Card they reach for. That is exactly the path this app took. **This is [FJS-1208](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1208)'s class met a second time**: content handed to a component and discarded with no diagnostic. That one was `<EmptyState>` dropping children across 28 call sites in basecamp; this one is every container in the kit dropping named slots. The narrow fix is the `$slots` fallback Form already has, copied to Card, Table, EmptyState and SectionHeader. **The better fix is the one neither has: warn.** A component that receives `$slots.<name>` it will not render knows, at render time, that it is throwing content away — and a `console.warn` naming the component and the slot would have turned a silent blank screen into a one-line answer. Nothing else catches it: `fli check` (34 rules) and `fli typecheck` are both green over the broken page, and Biome cannot read `.mesa` at all ([FJS-1228](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1228)) · [Card.mesa](packages/ui/components/layout/Card.mesa) · [Form.mesa:928](packages/ui/components/forms/Form.mesa) · [FJS-1208](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1208) · the stressor's `web/src/routes/availability/index.mesa` · blocked by `FJS-D668` |
| <a id="fjs-1223"></a>FJS-1223 | notifications | **The paved road for telling a person something cannot carry an attachment, while the transport underneath it can — so a confirmation with an `.ics` has to leave the notifications package entirely.** | open | 2026-09-20 | Three layers and the middle one is the wall. Junction's `MailMessage` declares `attachments?: MailAttachment[]` and `smtp.ts` builds correct multipart MIME for it, `Content-Disposition: attachment` and all ([mail/index.ts:27](packages/junction/src/mail/index.ts), [smtp.ts:565](packages/junction/src/mail/smtp.ts)). Notifications declares its OWN `MailMessage` — `{ subject, lines, to?, html?, text? }`, no `attachments` — its builder offers `subject/greeting/line/action/html/text` and no `attach()`, and `sendEmail` calls `app.mail.send({ to, subject, text, html })`, so an attachment would be dropped even if the authoring type grew a slot for it. **The two types are hand-kept in sync and the file says so**: *Keep in sync with junction's `MailMessage` in `src/mail/index.ts`* ([types.ts:121](packages/notifications/types.ts)). That is the same duplication whose last divergence was [FJS-904](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md) — a subject with no body, every `.greeting()` dropped, and the send reporting success — and the note beside it records exactly why: *notifications declares its own structural `App.mail` type rather than importing junction's, so the two can disagree.* They disagree again, now on `attachments`. **For a scheduling product this is the artifact the whole thing is for**: the calendly stressor sends its confirmation through `app.mail.send()` directly, which works and which means the notification kind, its preferences and its in-app sibling are all bypassed to attach one file. The fix is either an `attach()` on the builder plus a passthrough in the driver, or notifications importing junction's type instead of restating it · [drivers/email.ts](packages/notifications/drivers/email.ts) · [builders.ts](packages/notifications/builders.ts) · [types.ts](packages/notifications/types.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1213"></a>FJS-1213 | litestone · cli | **`verifyGateLadder` returns *this gate is wrong* and *this gate could not be asked* in one array, and the documented assertion — the one `fli new` generates into every app — is that the array is empty. Declaring what `AGENTS.md` tells you to declare on line one turns it red.** | open | 2026-10-07 | `docs/testing.md` shows `expect(await env.verifyGateLadder()).toEqual([])` and the scaffold ships exactly that in `api/test/access.test.ts`. **Measured on a five-model scheduling schema: 43 rows, 0 gate defects.** 16 are `skipped` — a create policy the synthetic principal cannot satisfy, which the same doc's own table describes as correct behaviour; 19 are `error` from one cross-column `@@check` (`startTime < endTime`) the auto-factory cannot satisfy, reported honestly as *no fixture could be built, so the gate was never asked* and still counted; 8 are [FJS-1212](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1212). The app's only ways out are to stop declaring row policies, to delete the `@@check`, or to write an exclusion list against a tool whose job is to be trusted — the third is what this one did, with the ids in the comment. **This is [FJS-1006](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1006)'s principle with new causes** — *a checker that emits 27 unaskable rows is one people stop reading* — and that fix was per-cause (views filtered to `read`), so each new cause re-opens it. The general shape is two channels: the mismatches, which must be empty, and the non-gradings, which an app should be able to see, count and hold steady. A `{ mismatches, ungraded }` return, or an `ungraded: 'throw' \| 'return'` option, would let the generated assertion stay honest and stay green · [testing.js](packages/litestone/src/testing.js) · [docs/testing.md](packages/litestone/docs/testing.md) · [FJS-1006](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1006) **Second app, 2026-09-22 — connectteam, and it gives the row a number.** A 14-model workforce schema, none of it exotic, turns the generated assertion red with **346 entries: 342 say the gate was never asked, 4 are labelled mismatches, and 0 are access holes** — the auto-factory cannot build a fixture for a model carrying `@point`, `@money` or a `String @date`, which is most of them. The 4 labelled as mismatches are miscategorised rather than real: each carries `"thrown": "Validation failed — this record is not valid"` beside `"expect": "allow", "got": "error"`, so the classification INSIDE the array is wrong too, not only the assertion over it. An app author's only reading of `bun test` on day one is *my schema has 346 access problems*. **Third app, 2026-09-22: linear, the first under `strategy row`, and a new cause.** 62 entries, 0 access holes. 19 are one cross-column `@@check` (`Cycle`, `startsAt < endsAt`), the same cause as the first measurement, so `Cycle`'s gate is never asked at all. 43 are `skipped`, and under row tenancy that number is structural: the tenancy block desugars a `@@deny('create', …)` onto EVERY scoped model, so each scoped model's allowed-create cells are unaskable whether or not the app wrote a policy. linear's [access.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/linear/api/test/access.test.ts) filters on why an entry is present, as connectteam's does **Amended 2026-09-27, *Portal, the search stressor* Phase 1: 29 rows, 0 gate defects, and two causes this row did not have.** 24 are `skipped` (create/update policies, as above). **4 are `error` on `Bang.create`, *FOREIGN KEY constraint failed***: `ownerId String? @system @default(auth().id)` stamps the SYNTHETIC principal's id into a foreign key to `User`, and no such user exists — [FJS-1200](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1200) fixed exactly this for `verifyTenantIsolation` (a sentinel) and the ladder was not given the same fix. **1 is `error` on `Grant.update` at SYSTEM, *granterId is @immutable***: the ladder's update fixture writes every column, `testing.js` has no mention of `@immutable`, so any model with an immutable column fails its update rung at the one level no policy skips. Portal's `access.test.ts` now filters `skipped` and these two by model and op, with the ids in the comment — the third way out, again. [portal access.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/test/access.test.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D572` · Sighting 2026-10-06, base44 Phase 4: the scaffold's api/test/access.test.ts `expect(await env.verifyGateLadder()).toEqual([])` fails on every Oracle-emitted app, 194 lines of 'skipped — the model declares a row policy for create', so `bun run check` is red on a correct app and base44's builder grades fli check, lint, typecheck and boot without the scaffold test · Sighting 2026-10-06, base44 Phase 6, on the builder's OWN schema (not a generated one): verifyGateLadder reports 73 rows 'no fixture could be built' — every App, Turn and Checkpoint op — all for one cross-field rule, @@check("ownerId IS NOT NULL OR runId IS NOT NULL", "an app belongs to a person or to a run"). The ladder's fixtures come from its own registry (testing.js _chains: factoryFrom + withParents({ fresh: true })), which skips optional parents and cannot see a @@check (FJS-1779), and it ignores the explicit factories createTestEnv was given — so an app with a correct either/or ownership rule has no way to make the scaffold's own test green short of deleting the rule. bun run check stays red on base44 for this reason alone. · Sighting 2026-10-06, quo stressor membership phase: the ordinary Organization + Member shape with members.some(…) policies turns the scaffold's toEqual([]) red on every create a membership rule covers (Member, NumberMember, PhoneNumber, Contact, Message), all 'skipped'. quo filters got !== 'skipped' with a comment and grades those creates on real members in telnyx.test.ts § Who sees a number · [access.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/access.test.ts) · A third cause, 2026-10-06, quo stressor Phase 2: autoFactories does not read @@arc, so a model declaring it — the catalog's own answer to a closed polymorphic set (FJS-D189) — gets a fixture with both or neither foreign key set, the table CHECK refuses it ('exactly one of messageId, callId must be set'), and every op at every level comes back got: error, 'no fixture could be built, so the gate was never asked'. Not skipped, so the scaffolded toEqual([]) fails on it, and filtering it out leaves the model's gate untested. A hand @@check tying a discriminator to the arc fails the same way · [PLAN § Phase 2 — Q11](../fjs-prototypes/quo/PLAN.md) · Seen again 2026-10-06, quo Phase 3: OpenWindow's @@check("opens < closes") makes every op at every level 'error', exactly the scheduling case above; excluded by name in the app's access.test.ts with a pin that it is the only exception, and its gate driven by hand · [access.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/access.test.ts) · A new cause, 2026-10-07, ELA stressor (Q4): under tenancy { strategy row }, a model whose create gate is below 8 is skipped at every level from its gate up — CrewDaily and UserDaily @@gate("4.4.4.9"), 8 rows — and the refusing 'row policy for create' is not the app's: it is tenancy's own desugar (accountId == auth().accountId), which the synthetic principal fails because it carries no claim. Unlike an app-written policy this one is gradable — the ladder knows the tenancy claim and could stamp one on its synthetic principal — so these rows are not merely mis-channelled, they are askable and not asked. ela pins the 8 by model and op in api/test/access.test.ts · [ela PLAN Q4](../fjs-prototypes/ela/PLAN.md) · Seen again 2026-10-07 in the chatwoot stressor: the scaffold's generated access test was red from Phase 0's commit, 566 rows of which every one was skipped (a create policy), with no gate defect among them; Phase 0 did not run `bun run check` far enough to see it. Adding a create rule takes the create gate out of the ladder's sight · [chatwoot PLAN Q1](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1245"></a>FJS-1245 | litestone | **A per-move `@gate` on `@@transitions` is a FLOOR on the model's `update` level and can only raise it, so a named move can never be more permissive than an arbitrary edit — and *anybody submits their own document, only a manager approves it* is not expressible.** | open | 2026-10-06 | **Measured** in the connectteam stressor: `Timesheet @@gate("4.5.5.7")` with `@@transitions(status, submit: open -> submitted, approve: submitted -> approved @gate(5) @seals, …)`. The employee submitting their own timesheet is refused with `"Timesheet.update" requires level 5, user has level 4` — although `submit` declares **no gate at all**, which at a call site reads as *no extra authority required*. It is not: `gradeMove` is reached only after the update gate has passed, and the comment there states the intent — *a transition gate is a floor on top of `@@gate`'s update level* ([client.js:3695](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js)) — with a rationale (*shipping an order and refunding one are not the same authority*) that argues only for RAISING. **The direction that works was measured beside it**: `approve @gate(5)` correctly refuses USER(4) and is taken at ADMINISTRATOR(5). **The shape that has no spelling is a move-grain drop box**, and it is common rather than exotic: submit a timesheet, acknowledge a policy, hand in a form, request a review, mark a task done. Each is *many people may make THIS ONE move, and almost nobody may edit the document*. The only two settings available both lose: leave `update` at 5 and the person cannot submit their own work, which is what this app shipped; or drop `update` to 4 and any signed-in caller may PATCH any field of anybody's timesheet, narrowed only by a row policy that would then have to be written for every operation. **`@@capabilities` is not the escape** — a named move is a capability automatically, but the grid is ANDed with the gate rather than substituted for it, so the ladder still refuses first. **This is [FJS-1244](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1244)'s finding on a second mechanism.** That row is the CRUD ladder refusing *many write, few read*; this is the transition ladder refusing *many make one move, few edit*. Two different words, one missing idea: the framework can express *more authority than an update* and cannot express *this specific act, by anyone who may read the row*. The fix is a design question rather than a patch — whether a move's `@gate` should REPLACE the update level rather than floor it, and what `fli check` should say about a move that lowers **Amended 2026-09-28 by the jazzhr stressor (Phase 1), measured, and it moves the row.** *"`@@capabilities` is not the escape"* holds only for a steep gate: on a FLAT one ([`FJS-D146`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d146)'s own advice) the grid IS the move-grain drop box. `Application @@gate("0") @@capabilities`: a bearer holding `['Application.withdraw']` withdraws, and is refused `reject` by name; `Offer @@gate("0") @@capabilities` with `signedName @capability`: the candidate signs and accepts, is refused a salary edit (`Offer.update`), and staff holding no `Offer.accept` cannot accept for them. Three costs remain. **(a)** Without the grid the ladder forces the whole model down: `"0.4.0.5"` (a stranger's move, staff-only create) is refused as *update below create*, so every staff-only verb becomes a silent policy. **(b)** A grid model a stranger must also CREATE cannot exist: [FJS-1486](#fjs-1486). **(c)** The grid is per person, so *only THIS job's hiring manager may approve* has no spelling: a move takes a level or a grant, never a row predicate · [jazzhr schema.lite § Offer](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L594) · [client.js § gradeMove](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [schema.lite § Timesheet](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/db/schema.lite) · [hours.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/test/hours.test.ts) · [FJS-1244](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1244) · blocked by `FJS-D511` **Amended 2026-09-29, jazzhr Phase 4: *only THIS job's hiring manager may approve* is spellable today, measured over HTTP.** The offer carries its job (`jobId Int @immutable`, held equal to `application.jobId` by the create policy `jobId == application.jobId`, so a one-hop column and never a stale copy), and `@@deny('post-update', status == 'approved' && job.hiringManagerId != auth().id)` grades the row the write would leave. A recruiter holding `Offer.approve` is refused by name (403 *Update denied by @@deny post-update policy on "Offer"*), the job's hiring manager approves, `send` and `withdraw` out of `approved` are unaffected, and litestone's own `transitions()` reports `approve: false` for the recruiter. Two costs, both stated: it is a rule about a STATE, not a move (exact only because `approve` is the only way into `approved`), and it must be a DENY: a second post-update ALLOW, added for a different rule, ORs with the first and re-admitted the recruiter (caught in review, not by a test). The direct spellings are each refused at parse with a good message: `application.job.hiringManagerId` *crosses two relations … carry the value on this model*. What stays open here is the ladder dragging create to 0 · [jazzhr `.probe/phase4/approver.ts`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/.probe/phase4/approver.ts) · **Second sighting, the narrowing direction (base44 stressor, Phase 7, 2026-10-06):** Oracle's Vaultwarden `Membership` declares `accept: invited -> accepted` with `@@allow('update', check(organization, 'update'))`, so the organization's admin takes `accept` on an invitation addressed to someone else — the derived screen offered *Accept* to the inviter and the move held after a reload (runs/2026-10-06-derived.json, 19-vaultwarden). *Only the invitee accepts* needs a per-move row predicate (`memberId == auth().id`), and a per-move `@gate` is a level only. |
| <a id="fjs-1246"></a>FJS-1246 | example | **`example`'s payroll has no input shape for a MEASURED hour: `annualGross` is `rate * hoursPerWeek * 52` and `hoursPerWeek` is an `Int` contracted constant, so a workforce product's whole output can reach a payslip only by being averaged back into the contract it was measured to replace.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Graded, not ported** — PLAN.md § Q6 in the connectteam stressor, against the shapes `example/api/src/domain/payroll/` demands and with nothing copied into that tree. An approved timesheet (`@@transitions`, `@seals`, lines carrying `onDate`, `kind`, `minutes` and a `rateSnapshot` frozen at the rate in force on the day) was converted into `PayWindowRow` and run through transcribed copies of `annualGross` and `periodShare`. **It goes, and everything that makes it a measurement is lost on the way.** Spring fortnight: 420 measured minutes, **€84.00 actually earned**, → `{ basis: 'hourly', rate: 1200, hoursPerWeek: 4 }` → `annualGross` €2496.00 → `periodShare(·, 26)` **€96.00 — out by €12.00**. Autumn fortnight: 540 minutes, €108.00 earned → `hoursPerWeek: 5` → €3120.00 → **€120.00, out by €12.00**. **Four refusals, and only the fourth is one a type checker catches.** (1) `hoursPerWeek` is an `Int @gte(0) @lte(168)`, so a fortnight holding one 7-hour night is **3.5 h/week and is stored as 4** — the rounding alone is the whole €12.00. (2) `PayWindow` holds ONE `rate` for a window while a timesheet holds a `rateSnapshot` per LINE, so a raise mid-period is a fact about a day at one end and a fact about a window at the other, and neither shape can express the other without losing the split. (3) `PayComponentKind.overtime` is in `EARNING_KINDS` and `draftPayslip` emits exactly one earning line, always `basicPay`; `overtime` is reachable only through `arrears.ts`, which is a CORRECTION to a previous period — so a timesheet that HAD overtime lines would have nowhere to put them. The record's own observation (*an enum value nothing in the application produces*) measured from the other side, and the timesheet builder cannot produce one either, because overtime is a rule about a WEEK and no row in either schema holds one. (4) `employeeId: number` against a uuid. **The loop is therefore not closable at this joint**, and the reason is a modelling one rather than a bug: `example` prices a CONTRACT and a workforce product establishes a MEASUREMENT, and the only bridge available re-derives the contract from the measurement and then pays the contract. Whether the fix is a third basis (`measured`, whose lines ARE the gross), or a `Payslip` that can be drafted from lines rather than from a window, is the open question. Nothing here is a defect in what `example` ships — it is the input shape being the one thing that was taken on faith · [employment.ts § annualGross](example/api/src/domain/payroll/employment.ts) · [payslip.ts § draftPayslip](example/api/src/domain/payroll/payslip.ts) · [handoff.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/domain/payroll/handoff.ts) · [hours.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/test/hours.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1214"></a>FJS-1214 | litestone | **[`FJS-D143`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d143) ruled on 2026-08-25 that a column declares what KIND of time it holds, and nothing implements it — so the first product that needs a recurring wall-clock time cannot spell its central object.** | open | 2026-10-08 | **Measured**: `grep -rn "timeZone\|timezone" packages/litestone/src --include=*.js` → **0 hits**, no `zoned`, no `wallClock`, and no import of `@frontierjs/toolbelt/datetime`. The catalog's only time words are validators — `@date`, `@datetime`, `@time`, all in the *Refuse a bad value* group — so *Tuesdays 09:00–17:00 in Europe/Lisbon* is stored as `String @time` with the zone in a sibling column on another model and the relationship between them held by a comment. **Corrected 2026-09-20 after measuring the browser payload — the gap is narrower and sharper than first filed.** The FORMAT half already works end to end: `@time` crosses as a `pattern` plus `x-time`, and sierra's control resolver renders `<input type="time">` for it, in a branch whose own comment reads *a wall clock has no zone, so `<input type="time">` round-trips it and the plain input is right* ([field-rules.js:619](packages/sierra/src/resource/field-rules.js)). So the framework already holds the wall-clock CONCEPT in three places — that resolver, toolbelt's `fromWall`/`resolveWall`, and `FJS-D143` itself — and the schema language is the one place that cannot say it. What is missing is the ZONE BINDING: nothing declares that this column resolves against `schedule.timeZone`, so the pairing is convention held by a service that remembers, no query can ask *is this instant inside this window* (the zone resolution SQLite cannot do is the same one `FJS-D143` measured as a silent NULL), and the comparison lives in application JS — where this app has already written it wrong once, applying its time check to one branch of a two-branch function. **The arithmetic is not what is missing** — `@frontierjs/toolbelt/datetime` already exports `fromWall`, `resolveWall`, `partsIn` and `plainDateIn` with Temporal's `disambiguation` vocabulary, which is the twenty lines the ruling scoped. What is missing is the declaration that binds a column to a zone, and a comparison the framework computes and binds. A plain DATE has the same gap wearing the other half of the vocabulary (`AvailabilityOverride.date`, *not the 24th*), so one word plausibly carries both. Found building the calendly stressor, where it is the reason the exercise was chosen · [`IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md`](IDEAS/time-and-recurrence.md) · [catalog.snapshot.md](packages/litestone/catalog.snapshot.md) · [datetime.js](packages/toolbelt/src/datetime/datetime.js) · [schema.lite § AvailabilityRule](../fjs-prototypes/calendly/db/schema.lite) **Priced in the connectteam stressor, 2026-09-22 — the answer is money, in both directions, and the two errors cancel.** *Tuesdays 22:00–06:00, Europe/Lisbon* at €12.00/h, driven across both of Lisbon's 2026 boundaries. The wall clock the manager typed is **480 minutes on every night of the year**, because it cannot see a boundary. The night starting Sat 28 March is **420 minutes** (`2026-03-28T22:00Z` → `2026-03-29T05:00Z`, offset 0 → +60); the night starting Sat 24 October is **540** (`2026-10-24T21:00Z` → `2026-10-25T06:00Z`, offset +60 → 0). So the same rota line pays **€96.00 both nights against €84.00 and €108.00 actually worked — overpaid €12.00 in spring, underpaid €12.00 in autumn**. **The two errors cancel over a year, which is what makes this worse than a one-directional bug**: an annual total reconciles, a year-on-year comparison reconciles, and nothing audits to anything. One person, one weekly night shift, on a rota of sixty. **The layer that should have owned the difference is the COLUMN.** `startLocal String @time` says it holds a time and not which zone reads it, so the app resolves the instant pair itself and stores it in two `@system` columns — and **nothing in the language knows those two columns were derived**. Measured: a depot entered as `Europe/Lisbon` that is really in the Azores, corrected afterwards to `Atlantic/Azores`, leaves every stored `startsAt` **one hour out, permanently, with nothing anywhere disagreeing** — and because both zones observe DST the error is CONSTANT rather than seasonal, so even the comparison that would have caught the DST version cannot see it. **And the validator half is missing too, which is how the wrong zone got in.** `Site.timeZone String @default("UTC")` accepted `"banana"` without complaint and threw at the first shift, from `@frontierjs/toolbelt/datetime` — one layer below the one that should have refused the write. The IANA set is knowable at parse time (`Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')`), so `@zone` is a validator that could exist beside `@date`, `@datetime` and `@time` and does not. Calendly's answer to the same gap was *a slot cannot be spelled*; this one is *a person was paid the wrong amount, twice, and the two amounts hide each other* · [hours.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/domain/timesheet/hours.ts) · [wall.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/domain/scheduling/wall.ts) · [hours.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/test/hours.test.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D512` · **Seen again 2026-10-06, quo stressor Phase 3 (a team phone's business hours), with a failure mode the calendar did not have: the zone is read inside a WEBHOOK.** PhoneNumber.timeZone is a String @length(1, 64) and OpenWindow.opens/closes are String @time on another model, bound by a comment. Nothing refuses a zone that is not one: 'America/Chicgo' is stored. toolbelt's partsIn throws on it, and the place it is read is a Telnyx call.initiated webhook, so an unguarded read answers 500 and the carrier retries while the caller hears silence. The app catches it and answers the call as open (domain/hours.ts; ring.test.ts § a zone that is not one). The same gap for ClosedDay.date (String @date, a day in the number's zone). A zone word with a validator would have refused the value at write time, which is the only place it can be answered politely · [hours.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/domain/hours.ts) · [schema.lite § OpenWindow](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/schema.lite) · Third stressor, 2026-10-08, chatwoot Q4: an SLA counted in business hours. Inbox.timeZone is a bare String, and OpenWindow.opens/closes are String @time whose zone is on the parent Inbox, held together by a comment. Business-minute arithmetic (add N, count between, both across DST) is about 80 lines of app code over toolbelt's fromWall/partsIn. It measured right: Fri 16:55 AEST + 4 business hours = Mon 12:55 AEDT, 67 real hours and not 68 · [chatwoot sla/hours.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/sla/hours.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1211"></a>FJS-1211 | repo · litestone | **Five of `PHILOSOPHY.md` §V's nine questions have a generated artefact to answer them from, and the two about VOCABULARY are not among them — so *is this a new concept* is answered from memory on every proposal that asks it.** | open | 2026-09-20 | Measured over the 28 committed snapshots the `snapshots` CI phase gates. **Five questions have one and it is direct**: *derived* has the derivations themselves (`ddl`, `jsonschema`, `access`, `release`, `routes`, `principal`, `jobs`, `notifications`), *owner* has [seams.snapshot.md](seams.snapshot.md), *boundary* has [exports.snapshot.md](exports.snapshot.md) plus `surface` and `principal`, *failure* has [errors.snapshot.md](packages/junction/errors.snapshot.md) and `access`, *silence* has [invariants.snapshot.md](invariants.snapshot.md). **Two are partial and they are the front half.** *Origin* has nothing that lists where each truth lives — `seams` lists handoffs, which is not the same claim. *Concept* has [catalog.snapshot.md](packages/litestone/catalog.snapshot.md), every word a `.lite` file can hold with its completeness asserted against the parser — and **no other realm has an equivalent**; there is no catalog command outside litestone, so Mesa's blocks, Sierra's targets and the UI kit's terms are in nobody's index. *Complexity* has none and never will. **What is owed is one place holding every term the framework defines — an FJS dictionary — so the mental model can be READ rather than recalled.** The reason to want it is the audit: [`ARCHITECT.md`](ARCHITECT.md) §2 is 22 Use/Not rows, 12 clarifications and 5 terms under *Not yet named*, every one of them a hand-written claim, and **nothing grades whether the code still uses the left column** — a word three packages enforce and a word the docs alone use read identically from §2, which is [FJS-D190](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d190)'s argument about invariants arriving one realm over. **The design turns on one question and it should be ruled before anything is written: is the dictionary DERIVED or AUTHORED?** §2 is authored, so generating a second list beside it fails *another origin of truth* by construction; the honest shapes are either that §2 becomes generated and the authored claim moves down to the places that enforce it, or that the dictionary derives only what has an enforcer and cites §2 for the rest, marking a term with no enforcer the way `invariants.snapshot.md` marks a `none` row. Three sources can already be read: litestone's catalog, `bridge-index` through `seams`, and `DECISIONS.md`, where most of §2's clarifications already cite the `FJS-D##` that coined or refused the noun. **The name `lexicon` is taken** — [`IDEAS/lexicon.md`](IDEAS/lexicon.md) is the i18n half `FJS-D12` did not reach, a different thing entirely. · [PHILOSOPHY.md](PHILOSOPHY.md) §V · [decision-rules](.claude/skills/decision-rules/SKILL.md) § What each one catches |
| <a id="fjs-1205"></a>FJS-1205 | litestone | **The browser host writes a named refusal for `@encrypted`/`@hashed` on a device and it can never be the error a caller sees — `Buffer.concat(…)` is evaluated as the ARGUMENT to the refusing function, so the throw is `ReferenceError: Buffer is not defined`.** | open | 2026-09-20 | `src/host/browser.js` exports `createHmac` as `no('createHmac', KEYS)`, whose message is the whole explanation a device developer gets: *an @encrypted or @hashed column is not readable on a device: WebCrypto is asynchronous, and holding the key here would defeat the attribute.* Every call site in `core/encryption.js` builds its arguments first — `createHmac(HMAC_ALG, Buffer.concat([Buffer.from(key), salt('kid')]))` — and `Buffer` is node's, absent in a browser, so the argument throws before the refusal is reached. **The refusal is the design and it is right** (`FJS-D305`); what is broken is that the sentence explaining it is unreachable, which is the *advice that fails when taken* class `proof-target` names. **`deviceSchema` is the likelier layer for the fix**: it already grades what it drops `changed`/`lost`/`noted`, and it says nothing about `@encrypted` today — a device handed a column it cannot read is a column that should have been filtered, with a note, rather than one that throws at the first write. | <a id="fjs-585"></a>FJS-585 | cli | **A Release records a configuration set that nothing applies, so `configurationHash` identifies the DECLARED configuration and not the one the process is running on.** `configurationSet(deployConf, target)` resolves `deploy.configuration` and `deploy.secrets`, hashes them into the Release id and writes the values and secret references into the journal — and no step ever puts them on the target. The container is started with `--env-file ${envFile}`, which is `.env.production` on the machine; `fli` READS that file (`01b-env-check` compares its keys against `.env.example`) and MOUNTS it, and writes it nowhere. So the two descriptions of one Release's configuration are independent, and a `configuration:` block edited in `frontier.config.js` changes the Release id, the generation and the recorded values while the running process is unchanged — a redeploy that mints a visibly different Release and alters nothing. Half of it is already known and stated: `fli deploy:revert` refuses to restore the pair once the generation has moved, saying it writes no `.env` on a target and therefore genuinely cannot. What is not stated is the other direction — that the hash is a fact about a file in the repository rather than about the deployment — and invariant 2 of `IDEAS/release-transitions.md` is written as though it were the latter (*serving state is the pair (Release, Generation)*). Found 2026-08-29 while wiring attached services ([`FJS-D158`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d158)), which is why that check grades `process.env` rather than the recorded set. **Half-addressed 2026-08-30**: the Release now says the hash covers a DECLARATION (`formatRelease` prints `(declared)`, `release:mint`'s table and `core/release.js`'s header say so), and `01b-env-check` grades the target against the declared KEYS — so the block asserts *this environment carries these* instead of being vacuous, and a declared block turns the check on by itself. What remains is the values half. Two honest ways out and they are different features: `fli` writes the env file from the configuration set (which makes the pair real and puts secret RESOLUTION on the critical path), or the Release records that its configuration is unmanaged and the hash covers only what it can see. | open | 2026-08-29 | [release-transitions.md](IDEAS/release-transitions.md) · `packages/cli/core/release.js` |

| <a id="fjs-555"></a>FJS-555 | cli · basecamp | **`example/site` and `example/widgets` build from a context holding a real `.env` with no `.dockerignore` at all.** Retitled 2026-09-13 — basecamp's half is fixed and what follows is how the class was found. Filed as: a `.dockerignore` pattern without `**` protects the context ROOT and nothing under it, so basecamp shipped a stray SQLite database into its image. Docker matches an ignore pattern with Go's `filepath.Match`, where a plain `*` does not cross a separator — measured: with `.env.*`, a root `.env.production` is excluded and `api/.env.production` is **copied**; `**/.env.*` reaches both. basecamp's file said `db/*.db`, which excluded `db/basecamp.db` and admitted `db/db/basecamp.db` — and `db/db/` is precisely what a relative `database { path }` resolved against the wrong working directory creates ([FJS-449](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-449)), git-ignored, so it appears in no diff. The Dockerfile does `COPY db ./db` and then `COPY --from=build /app /app`, so it reached the final image; `fli deploy:local` on a developer's machine therefore built an image carrying that machine's database, which is the exact hazard the file's own comment says it is guarding against. **The pattern is fixed and the class is now checked** — `fli deploy`'s `02b-build-check` step grades this over the server's real build context and refuses. **That claim was half true and is corrected 2026-08-29**: the check's classifier (`isStateFile`) graded `.db-wal` and `.db-shm` as state, and its finder (`CONTEXT_FIND`) looked for `*.db` and not the sidecars — two lists for one fact, written twice, disagreeing, with the finder's own comment claiming it was *the same list spelled for `find`*. So SQLite's sidecars were invisible to the rule that exists to catch them, and the SCAFFOLD's own `.dockerignore` still said `db/*.db`. The consequence was measured on a real deploy: the container writes `db/app.db-wal` into the mounted volume, which is inside the build context, so **every deploy after the first copied the running app's write-ahead log into the image** — one deployment's live data in the artefact, and a moving image digest, which under `04c-journal` made an unchanged redeploy mint a new Release. The finder is now built from the classifier's list and a test asserts they agree; the scaffold writes the `**/` forms basecamp already had. What is left is the other apps: `example/site` and `example/widgets` build from an `example/` context holding a real `.env`, with no `.dockerignore` at all, and both are reported as a warning because their runtime stages copy subtrees rather than the whole — the value stops in an intermediate layer on the build host, readable with `docker build --target build`, rather than shipping | open | 2026-08-26 | [build-check.js](packages/cli/core/build-check.js) · [.dockerignore](packages/basecamp/.dockerignore) · `IDEAS/release-transitions.md` § Phase 1c |
| <a id="fjs-503"></a>FJS-503 | litestone · junction | **`input:` validates a custom method's payload and types nothing, so the method body opens with a hand cast that can assert anything.** `{ method: 'recordTracking', input: 'TrackingUpdate' }` names a `type` in the seed and the payload really is checked against it — and then the body writes `const { trackingCode } = $.data as { trackingCode: string }`, under a comment in `example` defending the cast on the grounds that the validator already ran. The defense is true and TypeScript cannot see any of it: `as { trackingCode: number }` compiles identically, and the next line reads a field that is not there. **A generated surface that omits the one thing the declaration was for is the shape of `FJS-250`.** **Both halves already exist and nothing joins them.** `typegen.js` emits every seed `type` as a real interface (`export interface TrackingUpdate { trackingCode: string }`, line 123), and `ServiceTypes` beside it already proves the pattern — a generated registry, module-augmented, so `client.service('posts')` infers with no import and no type argument. The missing piece is one more registry: emit `SeedTypes { TrackingUpdate: TrackingUpdate, … }`, make `input:` `keyof SeedTypes` so a typo is a compile error rather than a boot throw, and have junction index `SeedTypes[T]` to type `$.data` inside that method. The service file does not change and the snapshot does not change. **Considered and rejected: importing the type instead** — `input: types.TrackingUpdate`. It makes a hand-written declaration depend on a generated file having been built, gives a JS app an import that does nothing, and duplicates a name that has to keep existing anyway because `surface.snapshot.md` records inputs by name and the validator resolves by name. Passing the schema OBJECT is worse still: the seed stops being the runtime owner and a stale artefact validates against last week's rules. **The rule that decides it, and it explains the three existing choices rather than contradicting them**: an early-bound handle where the thing pointed at is hand-written in the same language (caravan's `defineJob`), a name plus a registry where it is generated from another one (`input:`) or where the boundary cannot import (`registerControl`). What is given up is go-to-definition on a string, and the mitigation is the editor rather than the API — the vscode extension already jumps from `model:` into the seed, and jumping to the seed is the better destination than jumping to the artefact | open | 2026-08-25 | [typegen.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/typegen.js) · [junction litestone.ts `validateInput`](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [example orders.service.ts](example/api/src/services/orders.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-268"></a>FJS-268 | repo | **A consuming app's `tsc` reported the framework's type errors, not its own — and all of them came from junction.** Every `@frontierjs` package points its `exports` at a `.ts` and none emits `.d.ts`, so tsc follows those imports and checks the framework as part of the app's program; `skipLibCheck` covers `.d.ts` only and there is no option for *check my files, not the ones they import*. **Measured rather than assumed, and the measurement moved the fix**: of the 61 diagnostics a freshly scaffolded app saw, 61 were junction's — auth, caravan, conduit, notifications and testing ship `.ts` too and were already clean. So this was not the structural problem it was filed as. **Fixed 2026-08-15 by making junction's consumer surface type-clean** (`index.ts` + `src/**`, 0 errors from 67), which found seven real defects on the way — see the closed rows for `FJS-272`. Junction carries no typecheck baseline now, which is 0. **What stays open is the structural half**, and it is a cost question rather than a correctness one: an app still compiles the whole framework on every `tsc` run and in its editor, and `fli typecheck` filters the output rather than preventing the work. Shipping `.d.ts` beside the source would stop it and give an editor types it does not have to compile — against it, two artefacts to keep in step, and a `.d.ts` that drifts from its `.ts` is worse than none. Nothing forces the decision now that the output is clean | open | 2026-08-15 | [typecheck.js](packages/cli/core/typecheck.js) · [junction/tsconfig.json](packages/junction/tsconfig.json) |

It was not turned on because the number was measured first: Biome's `recommended` gives **7,249** findings on this tree, and the shipped curated set gives **~600**, of which 123 are `noUnusedImports` and 25 are `noGlobalIsNan`. | <a id="fjs-620"></a>FJS-620 | litestone | **Findings from the performance audit that are in no other register, so they depend on somebody reading a July document.** [`FJS-112`](#fjs-112) exists and is about the METHOD — no owner, no gate, no committed baselines — and the still-open list it names in passing covers H8, H9, JSONL cold-load streaming, S3 streaming/multipart and a low-impact tail. Five medium findings are named in neither it nor either idea document: **M4** nested writes — the atomicity half is closed by [`FJS-638`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-638), which put nested writes inside the transaction machinery, and what stands is that they run one statement per child; **M5** variable-length `IN (?,?,…)` lists compile a distinct statement per value-count and churn the 500-entry LRU, **and there is no chunking guard for SQLite's bind-variable limit**, which is a correctness cliff rather than a slow one; **M11** transform `getTableStats` recomputes all-table COUNTs per table, O(T²); **M12** the retention pass, whose first-line early exit and O(n²) `shift()` loop both landed but whose un-indexed `DELETE … WHERE createdAt < ?` still full-scans unless the app happened to index the column — the audit's fix was to auto-create it when a `database` block declares `retention`. **M7 was the fifth and is closed** ([`FJS-619`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-619)). Not one issue per finding, because the audit is the detail and duplicating it here is how the two disagree; this is the pointer that makes them open | open | 2026-08-31 | [PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md](packages/litestone/bench/PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md) § Medium-impact findings · [FJS-112](#fjs-112) |

Worse, the only harness had not been run in three weeks and had a broken case (`gate-getlevel` used a plural accessor and died, silently skipping the measurement that proves the GatePlugin cache still works). Re-verified and repaired 2026-08-06; `bun run bench` now exists. What is still missing is a **gate**: CI exists and has no bench phase ([FJS-009](#fjs-009)), there are no committed baseline numbers, and absolute µs are not comparable across machines — the only method that showed anything was interleaving the same bench against the pre-change tree on one box. `packages/mesa/bench/` (`bun run bench`) gates DOM mutations and bytes against a committed baseline and is run by hand; nothing calls it. `IDEAS/performance-regression-watch.md` is the design for the gate | open | 2026-08-06 | [litestone bench/PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md](packages/litestone/bench/PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md) § Re-verified |
| <a id="fjs-1124"></a>FJS-1124 | litestone | **Every `factories.<model>.asSystem()` starts its sequence at 1, and the unique-retry loop hides it until the seventh row.** A chain method clones the factory and the clone copies `_seq` from the ORIGINAL, which never builds and so never advances — every clone generates `user1@test.com` first. `createOne` retries a unique violation up to `UNIQUE_RETRIES` (5), advancing the clone's own seq each time, so the Nth fresh call silently makes N−1 failed inserts and the seventh throws `UniqueConflictError: User: email "user6@test.com" is already taken`. Measured 2026-09-13 on a `fli new --auth` app: seven calls of `env.factories.user.asSystem().createOne()` answer user1…user6 then throw; one held `asSystem()` handle is clean. The failure looks like a fixture bug in whichever test crosses the seventh row, and `docs/testing.md`'s own first sample (`env.factories.doc.withParents().createOne()`) and `tutor:test`'s lessons use the fresh-chain-per-call shape. `testing.js` already knows the hazard in its own callers (one clone per model, § "every chain method clones from the ORIGINAL, whose seq never moves") — the fix is for the chain to share the sequence rather than for each caller to hold a handle. The scaffold's `api/test/access.test.ts` holds one handle and says why. | open | 2026-09-13 | [seeder.js `_clone`](packages/litestone/src/seeder.js) · [`buildOne`/`createOne`](packages/litestone/src/seeder.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1125"></a>FJS-1125 | litestone · cli | **`createTestEnv` types every table as `unknown`, so a test in a scaffolded app casts `env.system` and `actingAs()` to `any` to call a verb.** `TestEnv.system`, `.db` and `actingAs()` are the untyped `LitestoneClient` from `index.d.ts`, whose table accessors are an `unknown` index signature; the typed client an app has is `db/schema.d.ts`, written by `bun run db:types`, and `createTestEnv` is not generic over it. Measured 2026-09-13: `fli new --auth`'s `api/test/access.test.ts` fails `fli typecheck` with 13 errors (`TS18046 'env.system.user' is of type 'unknown'`) until it casts, which is what basecamp's `api/test/*.test.ts` already do (`env.system as any`, eleven times in `rollback.test.ts`). The scaffold cannot import the generated type instead: `db/schema.d.ts` does not exist until `db:types` runs, and a CI checkout does not run it. A type parameter on `createTestEnv` (and on `@frontierjs/testing`'s re-export) would let a test name the app's client and drop the cast; the first test every new app gets currently teaches `as any`. | open | 2026-09-13 | [testing.d.ts `TestEnv`](packages/litestone/src/testing.d.ts) · `makeApiAccessTest` in [project/new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) |

| <a id="fjs-1939"></a>FJS-1939 | litestone | **fli db:advise reports each column of a composite foreign key as an unindexed foreign key of its own — UserDaily.day 'is the foreign key for crewDay and nothing indexes it' while @@index([crewId, day]) covers crewDay's (crewId, day) exactly** | open | 2026-10-07 | Found by the ELA stressor (Q4). UserDaily.crewDay is @relation(fields: [crewId, day], references: [crewId, day]). With @@index([crewId, day]) declared, advise still warns on day, asking for @@index([day]) — an index no query through the relation uses. It reads a composite FK column by column and accepts only an index LED by that column; the child side of a composite FK wants an index whose leading columns are the FK's, in order. A warning a correct schema cannot silence except by adding a useless index. · [advise.js](packages/litestone/src/core/advise.js) · [ela PLAN Q4](../fjs-prototypes/ela/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1938"></a>FJS-1938 | litestone | **A write refused by a composite foreign key names the wrong relation: UserDaily's crewDay (fields: [crewId, day] → CrewDaily) is reported as 'crewId,day [1,"2026-10-08"] names no Crew (relation `crew`)' while Crew 1 exists and the missing row is the CrewDaily** | open | 2026-10-09 | Found by the ELA stressor (Q4). UserDaily declares crew Crew? @relation(fields: [crewId]) and crewDay CrewDaily? @relation(fields: [crewId, day], references: [crewId, day]); both share crewId. Creating a UserDaily whose (crewId, day) has no CrewDaily is refused — right — but the message attributes the composite's columns to the single-column relation that shares its first field, so it sends the reader to a Crew that is there. The DDL is correct (FOREIGN KEY (crewId, day) REFERENCES crew_daily (crewId, day)); include works both ways. Wants: the refusal names the relation whose fields match, crewDay → CrewDaily · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [ela PLAN Q4](../fjs-prototypes/ela/PLAN.md) · Seen again in the data move: `sys.userDaily.create({ data: { crewId: 1, day } })` with no CrewDaily → "crewId,day … names no Crew (relation crew)" · Found by the ela data move (Phase 3) · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/ela/PLAN.md) · Seen again 2026-10-08, ela Phase 6: a missing CrewDaily reported as 'names no Crew' while seeding survey scores |
