| <a id="fjs-1814"></a>FJS-1814 | litestone | **A view removed from the schema is never dropped: migrate create answers 'the migration history already builds the schema', and every later rebuild of a table it reads restores it** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in a scratch schema after the quo stressor's Phase 2 dropped its TimelineItem view. A removed TABLE gets a commented DROP and '(in db, not in schema)'; a removed view gets no line at all. The rebuild's 'views over it, dropped for the rebuild and restored after' reads the views from the live database, not the schema, so the orphan is re-created on every later rebuild — and a rebuild that drops a column the orphan reads would fail its `SELECT 1 FROM view` probe. quo dropped it by hand · [20261006222844_q11_keep_two_tables.sql](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/migrations/main/20261006222844_q11_keep_two_tables.sql) |
| <a id="fjs-1813"></a>FJS-1813 | litestone | **findManyCursor answers { items, nextCursor, hasMore } while index.d.ts CursorResult and docs/querying.md say rows, and cursorFor — the one way to mint an edge for a row you took — is not typed at all** | open | 2026-10-06 | Found 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 2, merging two keyset scans into one thread (PLAN Q11). client.js:7506 and :7670 return items; junction's findWindow reads page.items correctly; a caller following the type or the doc reads page.rows and gets undefined. Merging two tables' windows needs the cursor of the last row TAKEN from each, which is table.cursorFor(row, orderBy) — present on the table, absent from index.d.ts, so the merge is written against `as any` · [querying.md](packages/litestone/docs/querying.md) · [index.d.ts](packages/litestone/src/index.d.ts) · [conversations.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/src/services/conversations.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1807"></a>FJS-1807 | litestone | **fli db:explain @secret shows apiKey String @secret and never says that a required @secret column leaves the model creatable by asSystem() alone, so a key an admin pastes in gets the word that refuses the admin** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 1. QuoWebhook.signingKey, a key an admin copies from Quo's dashboard, was reached for as @secret from the explain entry (its example is apiKey, and it says 'an API key is both'). @secret expands to @encrypted @guarded. litestone AGENTS.md § Choosing a column's access word says a required @guarded column makes a model uncreatable below level 8; the @secret entry does not. Swapping the one word in quo fails exactly one test, the admin's create (quo.test.ts § a subscription's key). The word for a key the caller writes and nobody reads back is @encrypted, and the @encrypted entry does say so. Its own example is ssn, so a reader looking for 'an API key' lands on @secret · [quo schema.lite QuoWebhook](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/schema.lite) · [quo.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/quo/api/test/quo.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1802"></a>FJS-1802 | auth | **auth's User fragment ships accountId as the app's account, and the Organization reference says the membership row is the answer, so an app that adopts the reference holds two answers to which org a person is in** | open | 2026-10-06 | Seen 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, membership phase. The scaffold's User carries accountId String? @allow('write', auth().isAdmin), commented 'every session carries it, so an app scoping by account reads it from there'. references/Organization.lite says a person is in an org by a Member row, and nothing either side says which wins or that accountId is for a one-org app. quo leaves accountId unused and null. Either the fragment says it is for one-account-per-person apps and is dropped when Member is adopted, or SessionContext.accountId is derived from the membership (FJS-D361's relator under strategy row) · [quo schema.lite § User](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/schema.lite) · [Organization.lite](packages/litestone/references/Organization.lite) · [FJS-1591](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1591) |
| <a id="fjs-1800"></a>FJS-1800 | litestone | **references/Organization.lite writes its policies on auth().orgId and auth().memberRole, which only an app where each person belongs to one org, or one under strategy row, can resolve, and it does not say so** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, membership phase. Deriving either claim with claim … from Member(userId) needs userId unique (FJS-D359 refuses a non-unique subject), which rules out @@relator([orgId, userId], once), the line the file tells a host to swap in, and means one org per person. FJS-D361 admits the relator only under strategy row. quo has several orgs per person and no tenancy block, so it took oracle's members.some(userId == auth().id) spelling (FJS-D566) and wrote the role guard as members.some(userId == auth().id && role == 'admin') on Organization's update. The file's 'No tenancy attributes' note says the host decides tenancy; it should also say its policies assume a claim, and give the members.some spelling for a host without one. The Member.role guard is the one this matters most for, because it is the line FJS-1799 says the emitter dropped · [Organization.lite](packages/litestone/references/Organization.lite) · [FJS-D359](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d359) · [FJS-D361](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d361) · [FJS-1799](#fjs-1799) · [quo schema.lite § Who belongs](../fjs-prototypes/quo/db/schema.lite) |
| <a id="fjs-1794"></a>FJS-1794 | junction | **junction surface ranks a custom method by its declared floor and never reads whether the body elevates, so over a generated app it lists two caller-scoped moves as ungraded and leaves out the public method that writes through asSystem()** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 on base44 Phase 4's calendly (apps/behavior/01-calendly @ ck-3): § Custom methods whose caller's standing is not graded lists `bookings.cancel` and `bookings.markNoShow`, both of which transition on `$.db` and refuse another host (drive-confirmed), and omits `bookings.book`, which is `gate: 0` and creates the invitee and booking through `$.db.asSystem()`. A declared gate 0 is a decision and reads as one; the gap is that the file the FJS-1087 triage was built on cannot tell an elevated body from a scoped one, and a body that names `asSystem` is a source fact a reader could record beside the floor · [surface](packages/junction/tools/surface.ts) · [FJS-1087](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1087) |
| <a id="fjs-1774"></a>FJS-1774 | cli | **The scaffold's test script is `bun test api/test`, a name filter rather than a path, so an app holding another app anywhere below it runs that app's tests as its own** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the base44 stressor, Phase 1: with generated apps under base44/apps/ (git-ignored), base44's `bun run check` ran 24 tests across 4 files, 18 of them from apps/*/api/test/access.test.ts, against schemas the builder was rewriting mid-run. | <a id="fjs-1757"></a>FJS-1757 | litestone | **extensible-columns.md says a declared key with no slot *stores, displays and edits like any other, and only a query naming it falls back to a scan*; the client refuses that query instead (*declared but holds no slot … no filter that would be honest*).** | open | 2026-10-05 | Measured 2026-10-05, notion PLAN § Q6 arm A: 277 of 290 realistic declarations could not exist and 2 of 13 that did held no slot; `props: { flag: true }` is a ValidationError, not a scan. | <a id="fjs-1746"></a>FJS-1746 | junction | **The warning for a custom method that answers a projection points at the wrong fix: it says answer the whole row, when the method writes nothing and wanted read: true** | open | 2026-10-05 | Hit in the notion stressor, PLAN § Q4. `pages.enter` (join the page room; writes no row) returned `{ pageId, joined }`. Junction warned *answered 2 of 7 columns … The announcement carries the row instead, re-read by id … Answer the whole row, or state the payload with ctx.dispatch*. Meanwhile every enter went out to the whole workspace as `pages enter <id>`: six enters, 4 to 5 frames on every socket ([probes/q4/run.ts](../fjs-prototypes/notion/probes/q4/run.ts) step 1d, before the fix). The fix was `{ method: 'enter', read: true }` ([FJS-D505](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d505), AGENTS.md § Silent failures), which the warning does not name. A custom method whose answer is not a row of its model is the likeliest read-only method, so the warning should offer read: true first |

| <a id="fjs-1721"></a>FJS-1721 | basecamp · junction | **A custom method that answers rows reaches the client in one of two shapes, chosen by how its author spelled the return, and `invoke()` is typed `unknown`. So every caller guesses with `Array.isArray(raw) ? raw : raw?.data ?? []`, and the first branch can never be taken** | open | 2026-10-05 | Probed 2026-10-05 against a real app over HTTP and WS, and both transports agree. A method returning `[...]` arrives as a list envelope `{ kind: 'list', data, errors }` with no `total`. A method returning `{ total, data }` is a `single` (per `FJS-140`) and arrives as that object. No client ever sees a bare array. Basecamp builds `{ total, data }` by hand in five methods and hedges in six places (`rowsOf` in `admin/index.mesa:59` and `servers/[id]/index.mesa:178`, inline in `activity`, `alerts`, `networks`, `channels`, `api-keys`). Two of its comments are wrong: `servers/[id]/index.mesa:169` says `action()` hands back exactly what the server sent, which is false for an array, and `activity/index.mesa:70` cites `FJS-020`, the broadcast-payload ruling. Not an Invariant 4 breach: the envelope owner is consistent. The gap is that nothing declares a method's answer shape. `IDEAS/declared-method-contract.md` covers input and gate, not output · packages/junction/src/core/envelope.ts (`wrapResult`) · packages/junction/src/client/index.ts (`invoke`) |

| <a id="fjs-1567"></a>FJS-1567 | portal | **Portal, the search stressor: page capture does not follow a <meta http-equiv=refresh> redirect, and a capture that reads 0 words is still status ok, so a redirect stub or a client-rendered page is kept as an empty copy with nothing to say so** | open | 2026-09-29 | Found seeding the knight persona (2026-09-29). https://www.conventionalcommits.org/ answers 200 with a 208-byte page whose only content is a meta refresh to /en/; https://www.inkandswitch.com/local-first/ answers 200 with 152 bytes refreshing to /essay/local-first. [fetch.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/fetch.ts) follows HTTP redirects only (redirect: 'manual', each hop re-checked by the SSRF guard), and nothing in api/src/domain/pages reads a refresh. Both were kept as status ok, words 0, finalUrl the stub. A client-rendered page does the same: https://chromedevtools.github.io/devtools-protocol/ (content in a <template>, filled by script) is ok with 0 words; not a parser defect, but indistinguishable from one on the page. [capture.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/capture.ts) writes status ok for any parse, and capture.ts:71 (hasCopy = words != null) then treats the empty copy as kept, so a link check never retries it. Fix: follow a same-document meta refresh (delay 0, http(s) only) as one more guarded hop; and write a 0-word parse as failed (for example 'the page had no readable text'), keeping link live, so the page shows the capture failed and offers Capture again. Seen with FJS-1566, whose empty copies are the same symptom from a different cause |
| <a id="fjs-1564"></a>FJS-1564 | repo · cli · css · sierra · vscode | **No surface or realm has a color of its own, so nothing a developer sees says at a glance which part of the app it came from** | open | 2026-09-29 | The goal: pick one primary color per realm (Data, API, UI, plus Deployment and Testing) and per surface (api, web, site, widgets, extension, desktop, cli), and carry it everywhere a dev sees that part of the app until the color alone says where they are. Today the nearest thing is accidental: `fli dev` prefixes come from bun run --parallel ([new.md:265](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md#L265)), which colors by script ORDER, so dev:web is yellow in one app and cyan in the next. **Where it would help:** (1) fli dev log prefixes and its banner (slot · web 8003 · API 8103), each surface and port in its color; (2) every realm-tagged log line -- litestone, caravan, junction $.log -- so a scrolling terminal sorts itself; (3) fli ci phases, fli proves / prove, fli check findings, fli next and fli ws:atlas, tagged by realm or surface; (4) fli new and fli make:* output naming what they created; (5) a FrameworkError's terminal print naming the realm it was thrown in; (6) the browser tab in dev -- a tinted favicon per surface so web and site tabs differ, the Sierra error overlay border, the dev banner; (7) Litestone Studio chrome in the Data color, Junction devtools in API, each registerDevService entry in its own; (8) the VS Code extension -- file icons for .lite (Data) and .mesa (UI), folder colors for api/ web/ site/ db/, the status bar; (9) docs -- ARCHITECT.md's realm diagram, README § Project Structure, VOCABULARY entries, the docs site, the data-/api-/ui-hazards skill headers, audit artifacts; (10) Basecamp and Outpost -- an app's surfaces in their colors across servers, deploys and releases; (11) test output -- the Suite realm, and which drive (API tier or browser) a failure came from; (12) the ISSUES.md Pkg column as fli next and register views render it. **Constraints:** one owner for the table (surface → realm, hue, ANSI and hex), most likely toolbelt since cli, css, sierra and the vscode extension all read it -- or beside CAT in [ports.js](packages/cli/core/ports.js), already one row per surface -- never a copy per package (Invariant 4); in @frontierjs/css it is a tone for DEV chrome only and never reaches a shipped app's styling (Invariant 13); color is never the only signal -- a label or glyph goes with it, NO_COLOR and 16-color terminals degrade cleanly, and the palette is checked for color-blind separation; "surface" already means a background layer in @frontierjs/css (surface.css), so the naming must not add a third sense. **Open questions (decision-rules):** realm as hue family and surface as a shade inside it (web, site, widgets, extension and desktop are all UI) or a distinct hue per surface; what color cli gets; whether Deployment and Testing get colors or stay neutral |

| <a id="fjs-1498"></a>FJS-1498 | litestone | **A counter keyed by a unique pair has no one-statement spelling: `upsert` refuses `{ increment }` in its update half (FJS-D27 put the operators on `update`/`updateMany` only), so `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET n = n + 1`, the write every aggregate table is made of, is three statements and a caught conflict in app code.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured** 2026-09-29, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 2: spelling A of Q1 is an `IntakeCount { source, quarter, count }` bumped on every application. `upsert({ where: { source, quarter }, create: { …, count: 1 }, update: { count: { increment: 1 } } })` is refused: *"increment" changes a value that is already there, so it belongs on update, not upsert*. The refusal is deliberate (the method's fast path reads `excluded`, the slow path calls `update()`, and only one could apply an operator), and the message names no way out. What the app wrote instead ([pipeline.ts `bumpIntake`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/lib/pipeline.ts)): `updateMany` with the operator; if the count is 0, `create`; if that throws on the unique pair, `updateMany` again. Correct under concurrency only because of the retry, and three round trips for one fact. Also met on the way, and a GOOD refusal: an operator on a column with `@gte` is refused with *state the bound as `@check`*. Candidate: the fast path emits `SET "count" = "count" + excluded…` for an operator, and the slow path is refused for an operator instead of the whole method, or the message names the three-statement shape · [FJS-D27](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d27) · [pipeline.ts](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/lib/pipeline.ts) |

The template in [new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) (`'the api on 8100'`, `'the web app on 8000'`, and the fixed formula `env*1000 + category*100 + project*10 + service`) was never updated when [FJS-1148](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1148) moved slots into `fli dev`. **Measured on one machine, with another app's vite on 8000:** `fli dev --dry` answers *slot 2, web 8002 · API 8102*, and `fli dev` binds exactly those (`ss -ltnp`). The README's `bun run dev` starts the API on 8100, and then vite dies with *Port 8000 is already in use* (`strictPort`), which takes the API down with it. It fails loudly, so it costs minutes, not data. The fix is the README naming `fli dev` and saying the slot is per root (`fli ps` shows it). |
| <a id="fjs-1286"></a>FJS-1286 | junction | **The API's boot banner prints `mode=production` for every server that is not in debug, including a dev server running `NODE_ENV=development`.** | open | 2026-09-28 | Re-measured 2026-09-28 in remnant, same line. Found in linear's Phase 0. [app.ts](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) computes `mode: config.debug ? 'debug' : 'production'`, and `config.debug` is set only by `DEBUG=1` ([config/index.ts](packages/junction/src/config/index.ts)), while the environment that `loadConfig` layers on is `NODE_ENV`. **Measured:** a fresh `fli new --full` app with `.env` `NODE_ENV=development`, started by `fli dev` (`bun --watch`), logs `devtools=disabled mode=production`. The field answers *is debug on*, but its label claims the environment, so a developer looking at why a dev server behaves like a production one is told that it is one. Either name the field `debug=` or report `NODE_ENV`. |
| <a id="fjs-1282"></a>FJS-1282 | cli | **`fli` cites an Invariant BY NUMBER in output an app developer reads, and the numbered list lives in this repo's root `CLAUDE.md`, which no installed app has — so the reason for a refusal is a pointer to nothing.** | open | 2026-09-22 | `fli check` prints `(invariant 18)` beside a failed rule ([checks.js](packages/cli/core/checks.js) `rule.invariant`, and the `--list` column in [check.md](packages/cli/commands/fli/check.md)); `fli make:widget` refuses a name with *(Invariant 19)* ([widget.md](packages/cli/commands/make/widget.md)); the adopt lesson says *(Invariant 2)* ([03-introspect.md](packages/cli/commands/tutor/_steps-adopt/03-introspect.md)); and the help prose of `make:resource`, `web:resource` and `make:site` does the same. The word is contributor vocabulary ([Invariant](VOCABULARY.md)) and the number is only meaningful beside the list. The fix is to state the RULE in the message — *model names are PascalCase singular* — and keep the number where the reader has the list: comments, registers, `invariants.snapshot.md`. `rule.invariant` stays on the rule table, since `ws:invariants` reads it · [VOCABULARY.md](VOCABULARY.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1268"></a>FJS-1268 | cli · notifications | **`fli check`'s `surface-src` grades FILES at a surface root and never directories, so a whole directory of application source sitting beside `src/` is unreported — and it is the location `@frontierjs/notifications` probes FIRST.** | open | 2026-09-22 | Found wiring reminders on connectteam Phase 6. Invariant 3 is *a surface keeps its source in `src/`, and only its entry beside it*, and the rule enforces it: a stray `api/probe.ts` written during this session was reported on the next run, by name, with the reason. `resolveNotificationsDir` probes `[resolve(base, 'notifications'), resolve(base, 'src', 'notifications')]` and takes the first that exists ([loader.ts:56](packages/notifications/loader.ts)), where `base` is the directory of the entry — so for an app whose entry is `api/index.ts` the preferred location is `api/notifications/`, a directory of app source beside `api/src/`. **Measured:** `api/notifications/ShiftReminder.notification.ts` in place, `fli check --only surface-src` → *nothing to report*. The same file one directory further in is where `example/` puts it and is what this app declares. Two small things, either of which closes it: have the rule walk directories at a surface root as well as files, and/or reverse the package's probe order so the layout it prefers is the one the invariant wants. The probe order matters on its own — an app that lets itself be probed rather than declaring `notifications:` gets whichever it happens to have created. · [loader.ts](packages/notifications/loader.ts) · Invariant 3 |
| <a id="fjs-1252"></a>FJS-1252 | cli | **`fli check`'s `service-model` fires on the spelling junction's own AGENTS.md teaches, and the message asserts a security consequence the running app contradicts.** | open | 2026-09-22 | The rule says a hyphenated service file *singularizes to 'clock-entry', while `db.<accessor>` is the model name with a lower first letter, so a hyphenated name never meets it. The miss is silent and fails open: the `@@gate` is not found and the model is served to anyone.* Junction's AGENTS.md says the opposite in as many words — *`order-lines.service.ts` becomes the service `orderLines` over `OrderLine`* — and `example/` ships ten hyphenated services. **Measured**, connectteam Phase 5, with `model:` deliberately absent: `kiosk-devices.service.ts` served `/api/kioskDevices`, and `KioskDevice @@gate("6.6.6.6")` answered **403 to an ADMINISTRATOR(5)** and 401 to a stranger — the gate was found and enforced, so *fails open* is not what happens. The advice is still right (`example` states `model:` on every one, and this app now does too), but a rule whose message describes a hole that is not there costs the reader a measurement to find out, and this is the rule most likely to be met on the first day of a new surface · [checks.js § service-model](packages/cli/core/checks.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1255"></a>FJS-1255 | junction · sierra | **A declared MOVE and a service METHOD are separate namespaces, so a screen that derives its buttons from `@@transitions` — which is what sierra offers it — can only call a method that happens to share the name, and gets a 405 when it does not.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Driven in a browser**, connectteam Phase 5. `resource.transitions(row, level)` reads `@@transitions` off the registered schema and answers `{ name, from, to, gate, allowed }`, which is exactly the right thing for a queue screen: the button set comes from the schema, a published shift stops offering *publish*, and a per-move gate disables what the caller cannot do. The screen then calls `resource.service.invoke(name, id)` — there is nothing else to call — and the move `export: approved -> exported @gate(8)` met a service method named `exportToPayroll`, so the button answered `Service 'timesheets' does not offer 'export' (allowed: find, get, patch, submit, approve, reopen, build, exportToPayroll)`. Renaming the method fixed it, which is the whole finding: the two names are joined by nothing but an author remembering. `fli check`'s `transition-methods` grades the other direction only — *a declared move and the code that makes it still name each other* — and was **green** on this app while the button was 405ing, because a method named `exportToPayroll` does name the move in its body. Either the rule should also ask whether every declared move is reachable BY ITS OWN NAME, or a service should be able to declare `{ method: 'exportToPayroll', transition: 'export' }` and have the client resolve it **Re-measured 2026-09-22, linear Phase 3, against states that are ROWS:** there is no name at all. `issue.transitions(row)` answers `[]`, `issue.transition(id, …)` refuses, the JSON Schema has no `x-transitions`, and `transition-methods` reports *no @@transitions in db/schema.lite*, so the rule is green because it has nothing to read. The moves ARE readable, as `WorkflowTransition` rows under the team's own policy (hidden with a private team, measured), so a screen can draw its buttons from data and move with a plain `patch({ stateId })`. [`FJS-D365`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-d365) B would give each move a name (its target's `@@label`) and bring `transitions()` back · [workflows.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/linear/api/test/workflows.test.ts) · [timesheets.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/services/timesheets.service.ts) · [approvals/index.mesa](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/web/src/routes/approvals/index.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-1256"></a>FJS-1256 | junction | **A custom method has ONE answer, and it is both the caller's result and the payload broadcast to every other tab — so a write whose useful answer is a report makes every connected client log *ignored an update with no id* and drop it.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Driven in a browser**, connectteam Phase 5. `timesheets.build` writes a `Timesheet` and one `TimesheetLine` per measured clock entry, and what the caller needs back is the report — how many lines, which clocks were still running, which days had no pay rate — so it answers `{ sheet, lines, stillRunning, noRate }`. That answer is announced on the service's channel under the method's own name, and junction's client logged `[Junction] ignored an update with no id — it cannot be matched to a row. An announcement carries the record it is about.` The documented instrument is `$.dispatch = false`, which is a boolean: the app can announce a shape no client can use, or announce nothing and leave every other tab unaware that a timesheet now exists. There is no third spelling — `$.dispatch` cannot name the row to announce, and returning the row instead loses the report the caller came for. The row's own guidance names the read-shaped case (*a count, a report, a credential* → `$.dispatch = false`); this is the write-shaped one, where silence has a cost. A `$.announce(row)` beside `$.dispatch`, or a method-level `announces:` naming which part of the answer is the record, would close it · [timesheets.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/services/timesheets.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1262"></a>FJS-1262 | junction | **A custom method whose payload is legitimately empty cannot be called over HTTP: `{}` is read as *no body* and answered 400, while the same call over the WebSocket works — so the transport the browser prefers hides it.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Measured**, connectteam Phase 5. `POST /api/clockEntries` with `X-Service-Method: stop` and body `{}` → `400 {"name":"BadRequest","message":"Request body is required"}`; the same request with `{"shiftId":null}` → `200` and the row; no body at all → the same 400. The browser never sees it, because the client prefers the socket once connected and the WS path carries `{}` happily — driven: the *Clock out* button on the employee screen works in a tab and the identical call from `curl` does not. Every non-browser caller is on the HTTP path: a script, a webhook, a native shell, a server-to-server call, and `fli`-driven smoke tests. *Clock me out*, *revoke my other sessions*, *mark all read* are ordinary methods whose payload is nothing, and the workaround is to invent a key · [body.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/body.ts) · [clock-entries.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/services/clock-entries.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1234"></a>FJS-1234 | litestone · caravan | **A predicate that depends on a ZONE held in a column cannot be a `where`, so deferred work over per-row wall-clock times is a full scan of the window: every candidate row is read and all but a few are discarded in application code.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found building reminders on the calendly stressor (PLAN.md § Q9). The product rule is *08:00 on the morning of, in the INVITEE's zone* — the one people actually want, as against *24 hours before*, which is easy precisely because a duration needs no zone. **The query that should exist cannot be written.** `booking.inviteeTimeZone` is a column, so *has 08:00 passed for this row* is a calendar calculation per row against a different zone per row. `where` has no zone-aware operator and no index can serve one, so the sweep bounds what it can — `startsAt` between now and now+48h, an ordinary instant comparison — and asks the real question in JavaScript. **Measured on a small database: `considered 60, sent 2`.** The ratio is set by the horizon and the spread of zones, not by how many reminders are due, so it does not improve with scale — it is 2 sends from 60 reads today and 2 from 60,000 on a real calendar. **Re-measured at workforce scale on connectteam 2026-09-22, and the claim holds with one correction.** 4,000 published shifts over a fortnight, 600 people, 8 sites, a 48-hour horizon, a five-minute cron. ONE fire at 07:00Z — 08:00 where half the workforce is, the most productive instant of that day — **considered 566, sent 96**, discarding **264 pending + 206 tooLate** on the predicate that cannot be lowered and **0 past** on the one that can, in **773 ms**. Over a whole day the same window is re-read 288 times for the same 173 reminders the employer owes: **165,744 rows read, 173 sent — 1 send per 958 rows**, and **~180 seconds of CPU a day** to do it (24 fires measured at 621 ms each across the block holding both zones' 08:00; the read total is arithmetic on the measured per-fire figure, the sends are measured). **The correction is which of the two terms matters.** Moving the same 600 people from 2 zones (the brief's: Lisbon and the Azores, ONE hour apart) to 12 spread around the world left `considered` **identical at 566** and moved the sends only from 96 to 81 — spreading people over more zones spreads their 08:00s over the clock rather than changing how many there are. **So the cost is set by the horizon alone, and the spread is second order**: an employer with one hour of spread pays a global calendar's price. That removes the one escape a reader might infer from this row — that a geographically tight product is cheap. **And the cheap fix this row floats does not reach this case.** A `@generated` column holding the reminder instant fails here for a stronger reason than *cannot do arithmetic*: the zone is `User.timeZone`, which is **two relations** from `Shift` (`Shift → Employee → User`), and *which* morning is itself undecided between `Shift.onDate` (the day the manager typed, in the SITE's zone) and `startsAt` read in the person's — two columns the schema does not relate. Measured: the same shift is `tooLate` for a Lisbon employee and `pending` for an Azorean one, with reminder instants a DAY apart. **`tooLate` is a second consequence worth naming**, at 206 of 566 rows in one fire: a rule stated as a wall clock has shifts it can never remind at all — every shift starting before 08:00 local, which in a field workforce is the early turn. A rule stated as a duration has no such case, which is precisely why building that one would have measured nothing. · driven in connectteam `api/test/reminders.test.ts` § 1, § 6 **This is [FJS-1214](#fjs-1214)'s gap in a second place and the consequence is different enough to be its own row.** 1214 is about DECLARATION — a recurring wall-clock window cannot be spelled in `.lite`. This is about QUERY: the same absence of zone awareness means a zoned predicate cannot be pushed down, and the cost lands on background work rather than on a form. The arithmetic exists and is correct — `fromWall`/`partsIn` in toolbelt answered every DST case in `api/test/reminders.test.ts` first time, including a Tokyo booking whose reminder falls on the previous UTC date — so what is missing is not the maths but anywhere to put it that the database can see. **The cron half is not a defect and is worth stating so the row is not read as one.** `handle(…, { cron, timeZone })` binds ONE zone per schedule (measured: `registrations()` returns `timeZone: null` for an unzoned expression), which is right — a schedule is a statement about the app, not about a row. It is *because* that is right that the work has to become a sweep, and the sweep is where the missing predicate bites. **What would close it:** a zone-aware comparison litestone can lower — the same word [FJS-1214](#fjs-1214) asks for, reused in `where` — or, far cheaper and worth considering first, a `@generated` column holding the reminder instant, which fails today for the reason `Booking.endsAt` already documents in this app's schema: a generated column cannot reach across a relation or do arithmetic on one. **What is NOT a finding, measured and cleared:** caravan's dispatch door. `delay` is a duration and the `jobs` table stores `run_at`, an instant, which looked like `FJS-D143`'s category error one layer down — it is not. The conversion is exact (measured: `run_at` landed on the target instant, off by 0 ms), and a `runAt` option does not exist rather than being silently ignored, so TypeScript refuses it at the call site; reaching it needs a deliberate `as any`. The queue contributed durability, one-fire-per-cluster and retries, and none of Q9's difficulty came from it · [caravan/src/cron.ts](packages/caravan/src/cron.ts) · [FJS-1214](#fjs-1214) · [FJS-D143](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d143) |
| <a id="fjs-1235"></a>FJS-1235 | cli | **The scaffolded `dev:api` watcher does not restart when `db/schema.lite` changes, because the schema is read from disk at runtime and is not in the module graph — so editing the schema silently does nothing and the API keeps serving the old one, answering confidently wrong.** | open | 2026-10-08 | Found on the calendly stressor; cost two stalls before the cause was understood, each spent debugging an app that was correct. **Measured, with a control, on one running process:** `PORT=8102 bun --watch run api/index.ts`, then — (1) a real content change to `api/src/core/db.ts` → the app **restarted** (a second boot banner in the log); (2) a real content change to `db/schema.lite`, including adding an actual column to a model → **no restart**, no log line, no warning. The control is the point: the watcher works, and it is not watching the file that defines the data. **Both halves are scaffolded by `fli new` and they disagree.** `api/src/core/db.ts` builds a schema PATH — `fileURLToPath(new URL('../../../db/schema.lite', import.meta.url))` — and hands it to `createClient`, so the schema is opened with `fs` at runtime and never imported. `bun --watch` watches the module graph. A path string is not an import, so the most-edited file in a schema-first framework is the one file the dev loop ignores. **The symptom is the bad kind: silence plus a plausible wrong answer.** A column added to the schema is absent from the running API, so a request writing it fails validation for a field the developer is looking straight at, and a policy edited to open a door stays shut. Nothing says *restart me*; `fli check` is green because the source is fine; and the natural conclusion is that the schema change is wrong. **Same shape as [FJS-1232](#fjs-1232)** — two scaffolded pieces that do not agree, with nothing announcing it — and this app has now hit that shape three times in two days ([FJS-1233](#fjs-1233) is the third). **Fix:** make the dev script watch the schema — `bun --watch --watch-extensions .lite` if it supports one, otherwise `bunx --bun concurrently` or a small chokidar wrapper in the scaffolded script — or, better, have litestone `createClient` register the schema path with the runtime's watcher when one is present, so every app gets it without a script change. Cheapest interim: one line in the scaffolded `AGENTS.md` under *What a generic habit gets wrong here*, since that file already exists to catch exactly this class · [FJS-1232](#fjs-1232) · [FJS-1233](#fjs-1233) · Seen again in stressor ela: a migrated @generated column was refused as an unknown orderBy until the dev API was restarted. Measured: an `import '../db/schema.lite' with { type: 'text' }` makes bun --watch restart, but api/src/core/db.ts is also imported by vite/site builds where the attribute breaks — the fix is fli's or litestone's |
| <a id="fjs-1232"></a>FJS-1232 | cli · sierra | **`fli new` writes `NODE_ENV=development` into the app's `.env`, and a production Vite build reads it — so `import.meta.env.DEV` is TRUE and `PROD` is FALSE inside the shipped bundle, and every dev-only branch in the app ships to production.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found on the calendly stressor while giving the `site/` surface an API origin. The fallback was written the ordinary way — `` VITE_API_URL ?? (import.meta.env.DEV ? 'http://localhost:8100' : '') `` — and `bun run build:site` baked **`http://localhost:8100/api`** into the prerendered island. **Measured three times on one file, changing one line:** with `NODE_ENV=development` present the build emits `` Q=`http://localhost:8100/api` ``; with that single line removed from `.env` and nothing else changed, the same build emits `` Q=`/api` ``; switching the branch to `import.meta.env.MODE === 'development'` emits `/api` with the line back in place. **The chain is all scaffolded, and no part of it is the app author's mistake.** `fli new` writes `NODE_ENV=development` into `.env` ([the scaffolded file](packages/cli/)); the scaffolded `build:site` script runs `bun --bun --env-file=../.env vite build`, which puts it in `process.env`; Vite computes `isProduction` from `process.env.NODE_ENV` ahead of the mode, so `vite build` — whose mode IS `production`, and which minifies, tree-shakes and prerenders as production throughout — defines `import.meta.env.DEV` as `true`. **Why this is worse than a wrong constant.** `DEV`/`PROD` is the documented way to write a branch that must not ship, so the failure lands precisely on the code chosen for being safe: dev URLs, verbose logging, mock payment paths, seeded credentials, disabled auth checks. It is silent at every stage — the build succeeds, `fli check` is green, the output looks right, and the bundle is minified so nobody reads it. On this app it would have shipped a public booking page pointing at `localhost:8100`, and the symptom in production is *no times available*, not an error. **Vite's own docs say NODE_ENV and mode are independent and that setting NODE_ENV in `.env` affects the build**, so the behaviour is theirs and correct; the bug is the scaffold pairing that line with a build script that loads it. **Fix, cheapest first:** stop shipping `NODE_ENV=development` in the scaffolded `.env` (it is what `bun run dev` already implies, and the API reads `NODE_ENV` from the real environment); or drop `--env-file` from the build scripts; or have `createSierraViteConfig` set `define: { 'import.meta.env.DEV': mode !== 'production' }` so the flag follows the mode it claims to describe. A `fli check` rule cannot reach this — it is invisible in source and only appears in the emitted bundle — so a build-time assertion in sierra's build (`DEV` true while mode is `production` → warn by name) is the only grader that would have caught it. Pairs with [FJS-1226](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1226): both are a declared value that boots clean and means something other than it says · [site/config/vite.config.js](packages/sierra/) · [FJS-1226](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1226) |
| <a id="fjs-1233"></a>FJS-1233 | sierra | **An island on the prerendered `site/` surface cannot reach the API by the relative path every other surface uses, and nothing says so — not the dev server, not the build, not `fli check`. The page renders "no times available" over an API it never called.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Found by opening the app's own public booking page on the calendly stressor. The island fetched `` `/api/event-types/${id}` ``, which is correct on `web/` and correct from the server, and resolves against the SITE's origin here — a different deployment by design. **Measured:** `GET`/`POST` to `/api/...` on the site dev server returns the SPA-fallback shell, the island reports `Slots unavailable (404)`, and the page then renders *There are no open times in the next two weeks* — a plausible business answer to a question never asked. **The asymmetry is the finding.** `web/config/vite.config.js` is scaffolded WITH an `/api` proxy and a comment explaining that without it requests hit the SPA fallback; `site/config/vite.config.js` is scaffolded with no proxy, no comment and no alternative, and `site/deploy/serve.js` — the `--serve` host for the built output — has no `/api` handling either. So the same line of code works on one surface and fails on the other, in dev AND in production, and the surface that fails is the one whose whole point is being a separate origin. The `widgets/` surface, by contrast, makes this unmissable: it takes `data-api` as an ORIGIN and cannot be written the relative way. Three surfaces, three different contracts, one of them silent. **Adding a proxy to `site/`'s dev config would be the wrong fix** and is worth saying, because it is the obvious one: it would make dev pass and leave the served build broken, which is strictly worse than failing in both. **The right shape is the widget's** — the site is told the API's origin — so what is missing is the scaffolded `VITE_API_URL` (documented in `.env.example`, defaulting to the relative path for the same-origin-behind-one-proxy deployment) and, ideally, a prerender-time check: sierra already walks every island chunk to publish it, so a relative `/api` literal in an island is statically findable and could be reported the way `publishes:` is. **Also missing: `envDir`.** Both surfaces' vite configs leave it at the default, which is the surface folder, while the app has exactly ONE `.env` at its root — so `VITE_SITE_URL` written where every other variable in the app lives reaches nothing and the client silently falls back to a localhost default. Same failure mode as the above, one layer up · [site/config/vite.config.js](packages/sierra/) · [FJS-1232](#fjs-1232) |
| <a id="fjs-1231"></a>FJS-1231 | ui | **`<Select>` given a `value` that matches none of its options renders blank and says nothing — which is indistinguishable from *nothing is chosen*, and is how a wrong handler presents itself.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Reported on the calendly stressor as *selecting a day makes the selection disappear*. **The cause was the app's**, and this row is not a complaint about the contract: `<Select>` and `<Input>` wire `on:change={onchange}` / `oninput?.(e)` straight to the DOM and their headers say *Native event callbacks*, so a handler written as `(v) => Number(v)` receives an Event, `Number(Event)` is `NaN`, and the control then holds a value no `<option>` has. The browser renders that as an empty box. **What is missing is the sentence.** The component knows, at render time, that it was handed a non-null `value` matching none of its normalized options — it has already built `flatOptions` — and it renders blank instead of saying so. Nothing else catches it either: the page compiles, `fli check` is green, and there is no console output, so the only signal is a control that silently discards what you pick. **The kit already holds this exact principle** and applies it one case over. `optionsNote` exists because *an empty list and a list nobody could fetch render identically, and a person [cannot tell]* ([utils.js:260](packages/ui/utils.js)), and `truncationNote` exists for the same reason. A value that matches no option is the third member of that family and the only one with no note. A `console.warn` naming the component, the value and the option count would have turned a blank box into a one-line answer; a visible note is probably wrong here, since the state is usually a bug rather than something to tell an end user about. Worth pairing with [FJS-1230](#fjs-1230), which recommends the same remedy for a different silent discard, and with [FJS-1208](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1208). Three components in one kit now drop what they were given without a word, which is a house style rather than three bugs · [Select.mesa](packages/ui/components/forms/Select.mesa) · [utils.js:260](packages/ui/utils.js) · [FJS-1230](#fjs-1230) |
| <a id="fjs-1210"></a>FJS-1210 | sierra · litestone | **A model joining the device schema turns a previously-inert foreign key into a real constraint, and hydration has no answer for it: the warm fills tables in DECLARATION order, so one batch can fail whole on a row the next declaration would have written.** | open | 2026-09-20 | Found by declaring `@@sync(field)` on `example`'s `ProductVariant`. `deviceSchema()` keeps the `@@sync` models plus what they reference, so the variant table appeared on the device — and `InventoryMovement`, which the stockroom already hydrates, has a relation to it. Before the policy that column was an inert number with no table behind it; after it the FK is emitted, and hydrating 40 movements against an empty variants table failed the whole batch: `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY`, reported as a `warning:` the screen never shows. **`example` answers it the way an app can** — declare the variants too, and import them FIRST, since `warmOffline()` walks `_declared` in registration order. What is owed is the framework half: the order is currently an app's to get right, with nothing saying so and nothing checking it, and the failure is a silent console warning plus a device that is emptier than it looks. Two shapes to weigh — sort the warm by FK dependency over the device schema (derivable, and it makes the import order stop mattering), or defer foreign keys for the length of a hydration batch (cheaper, and it lets a batch land whose parents arrive later). An `fli check` rule is the third: a device model referenced by another device model and declared by no `offlineQuery` · [offline.js](packages/sierra/src/resource/offline.js) · [local-db.js](packages/sierra/src/resource/local-db.js) · [device-schema.js](packages/litestone/src/device-schema.js) |

| <a id="fjs-1185"></a>FJS-1185 | litestone | **A `seeAlso` in the catalog is matched on the word alone, and eight words exist at two levels — so 36 of the cross-references in the generated reference point at the wrong entry, silently.** | open | 2026-09-19 | `CATALOG.find(r => r.word === w)` takes the first row carrying the name, so `@@log`'s pointer at `log` resolves to the FIELD `@log`, `@@id`'s at `unique` to `@unique`, `@@capabilities`'s at `allow` to `@allow`. Some of the 36 are right by luck and some are not, and which is which is a per-row judgment about what the author meant — which is why this is filed rather than swept. The MECHANISM is fixed: a `<level>:<word>` entry resolves to that level, used by `@@index`→`@@unique` (litestone CHANGES 2026-09-19), rendered as the bare word by the assistant and as a link by the reference. What is owed is reading the 36 and qualifying the ones that mean the other level. The sharper fix underneath is to make a bare ambiguous name REFUSE at render, which cannot land until the 36 are resolved — that ordering is the whole of this row. Found fixing `@@index`'s under-reported signature · [catalog-reference.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/catalog-reference.js) · [catalog.js](packages/litestone/src/core/catalog.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1178"></a>FJS-1178 | sierra | **A resource's `offlineQuery` is only registered once its MODULE is evaluated, and a route's modules are code-split — so the screen nobody has opened, which is the one the declaration exists for, declares nothing until somebody opens it.** | open | 2026-09-16 | `createResource` runs at import (Invariant 18) and `declareOffline` runs with it, so a boot that imports no resource warms nothing. `example` answers it with one line in `web/src/main.js` — importing the resource after `virtual:sierra`, which is what builds the client it is created against — and that is a real answer rather than a workaround, since WHICH resources are worth the entry chunk is a bundling decision the app owns. What is owed is the framework half being decidable at all: a glob over `src/resources/` is derivable (Invariant 19 already fixes what that directory holds) and `offline:` already exists in `sierra.config.js` for the shell, so `offline: { resources: true }` is the shape. **Two things make it more than a glob**, and they are why it is filed rather than built: `createResource` needs a client, so an eager import must be ordered AFTER `initJunction` or every resource module throws at boot; and every resource so imported lands in the entry chunk, which is exactly the number `FJS-D302` grades · [offline.js](packages/sierra/src/resource/offline.js) · [main.js](example/web/src/main.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1177"></a>FJS-1177 | litestone | **`@@index([<a relation field>])` parses, migrates and builds an index on a string CONSTANT** — SQLite reads a double-quoted identifier that is not a column as a string literal, so `CREATE INDEX "idx_c_other" ON "c" ("other")` succeeds and indexes the same value for every row. | open | 2026-09-16 | Measured 2026-09-16: `parse()` answers valid, `generateDDL` emits the line, `db.exec` applies it clean, and `sqlite_master` holds the index — which is the whole failure, since a declared index that does not exist is the one defect nothing about the app looks different for. The author almost certainly meant the relation's foreign key, so there are two candidate fixes and they are not the same ruling: refuse it at parse the way `@@index` over a `@computed` is refused (a virtual column is the neighbouring case and is already refused), or MAP it to the relation's `fields:` the way `ddl.js` maps the FK itself. `@@unique` over one has the same shape and is worse, being a constraint. | <a id="fjs-1090"></a>FJS-1090 | junction | **Nothing tells an app which origins its API must allow once the page is not same-origin, and for a native shell the answer differs by operating system.** | open | 2026-09-12 | A split-origin web build (`FJS-1089`) and a bundled desktop shell (`FJS-D263`) both call the API cross-origin. `example` gets by on `cors: { origins: ['*'] }` only because it sends a Bearer token, which its own comment in `api/src/app.ts` says; an app with `cookieAuth: true` needs an explicit list and `credentials`, and `csrf: true` borrows that same list and is already refused by name when it is empty or `*` ([app.ts:2079](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts#L2079)). **The list is not obvious**: the web origin (`https://app.example.com`), and for Tauri `tauri://localhost` on Linux and macOS but `http://tauri.localhost` on Windows and Android — so a list written from a Linux spike passes every run there and fails every Windows install. Nothing derives it: the deploy config knows the web domain and the shell's config knows its scheme, and the API reads neither. Owed: derive the list from those declarations, or at least say at boot what a cross-origin page will be refused. Whether `cookieAuth` beside `origins: ['*']` is already refused at boot was not checked when this was filed. **`FJS-1089` made the split shape deployable and left this open on purpose.** A cookie session across `app.` and `api.` was said to break for want of a cookie `Domain`, and that is unmeasured: the two names are one SITE, so a host-only cookie on the API's name may well ride a credentialed request from the web name, and what is certainly required is `credentials: true` with an explicit origin. | <a id="fjs-1086"></a>FJS-1086 | example | **A browser drive running while anyone edits a watched file is RELOADED under its feet, and the failure reads as a bug on whatever screen it had reached.** Found 2026-09-12 chasing [FJS-1084](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1084): eight `[vite] (client) page reload packages/sierra/src/router/*.js` lines landed in the web server's log inside one run, because another session was editing the router, and each one full-reloaded the drive's page. It surfaced three different ways in three runs — `Inspected target navigated or closed` on the very first `goto('/')`, the same on `/plans/1/` where it LOOKS like a native form submit (`Page.frameRequestedNavigation` to the page's own URL right after `#pv-save` is clicked), and a timeout further on — and none of the messages says *the page reloaded*. **It is a real hazard in this repo rather than bad luck**, since two sessions sharing one tree is a stated way of working here, and CLAUDE.md's *a dev server serves the code it started with* describes the opposite case: a `.mesa` module is cached, a plain JS module under a workspace package is watched. **The cheap fix is a detector, not a lock**: the drive already receives every `Page.frameNavigated`, so one arriving while no `goto` is in flight can fail the run naming the cause. Measure it by editing a router file mid-run, with an undisturbed run as the control | open | 2026-09-12 | [verify.mjs](example/web/test/verify.mjs) · [FJS-1084](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1084) |
| <a id="fjs-1078"></a>FJS-1078 | cli | **`serviceForModel` hand-rolls Invariant 2's model name and disagrees with the owner on 7 of 12 real service names — in a function one command calls.** [`FJS-975`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-975) closed 2026-09-07 by giving `@frontierjs/toolbelt/inflect` the shape half and consolidating six copies, and it names `split(/[-_]/)` in the cli as one of them. `core/app-schema.js:302` is a survivor it did not reach, with a regex that handles no separator at all: `addresses` → `Addres`, `people` → `People`, and `journal-entries` → `Journal-entry`, which is the exact `Order-item` failure that row describes — not an identifier. `modelName` answers `Address`, `Person`, `JournalEntry`. **Two facts keep it at S4 and both belong in the row**: one command calls it, `fli admin:generate` (`commands/admin/generate.md:453`), which is corrected 2026-09-13 from *nothing calls it* — a grep over `.js` alone misses a command written as markdown, and every hyphenated service in `example` states `model:` explicitly — `product-variants.service.ts` carries a comment saying it is STATED rather than derived — so no app here reaches the wrong answer today, and `admin:generate` over a hyphenated service without `model:` would. That is also what makes it worth filing rather than fixing in passing: a latent copy of a consolidated fact is how the consolidation comes undone, and the next caller inherits it silently. The fix is one line pointing at `modelName`, since deleting it would break `admin:generate`. | open | 2026-09-12 | [app-schema.js](packages/cli/core/app-schema.js#L302) · [inflect.js](packages/toolbelt/src/inflect/inflect.js#L376) · [FJS-975](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-975) |
| <a id="fjs-1060"></a>FJS-1060 | example | **`verify` failed twice in six runs, both times on the FIRST run against a freshly started API, and at two different assertions.** Observed rather than diagnosed, which is the honest state and the reason it is filed at all. `subDetail.stopRenewing` waited out on `.popover button.danger` once, and `form.customerOptions` on `[role="option"].length > 1` once; the four runs against an already-warm API all passed 61/61. **The underlying features were then verified working by direct probe** — the customer picker offers 8 options on the same page, at the same point in the drive, signed in the same way — so what is flaky is the drive rather than the app, which is the distinction that makes it worth a row instead of a shrug. Both failures are `waitFor` timing out at its 8s default. The suspicion is that a just-started API is answering slowly enough on its first reads to outrun it, which `web/test/lib/preflight.mjs` cannot currently see: it asks reachability and FRESHNESS, and *ready to answer a health check* is not *ready to serve a relation read*. Two data points and two different symptoms is not a diagnosis, so nothing is changed on it. **It matters because it is the app's headline drive**: one failure in three on a cold server is the rate at which people learn to re-run rather than read, which is [FJS-729](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-729)'s complaint one tier up and over a browser instead of a unit suite | open | 2026-09-09 | [verify.mjs](example/web/test/verify.mjs) · [preflight.mjs](example/web/test/lib/preflight.mjs) · [FJS-729](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-729) |
| <a id="fjs-1053"></a>FJS-1053 | litestone | **README § How it compares grades three other tools and only the Litestone column has ever been re-read.** The seven grids were written 2026-04-25 and last given content on **2026-08-05**; the 2026-09-05 touch was a spelling sweep (`@guarded(all)` → `@guarded`). The Litestone column was refreshed 2026-09-08 — fourteen rows added for language that shipped since and was in the grids nowhere, `@@extensible`, `view`/`@@materialized`/`@@refreshOn`, `@@export`, `@@check`, `@@arc`, `valueset`, `Int @big`, `@@id([a, b])`, partial `where:` indexes, `@seals`, `@keep`, `litestone import` and `litestone mutate` — each verified against `src/core/parser.js` and the module that implements it. **The other three columns were not**, and they are the half a wrong cell embarrasses: a ✗ against a competitor is a claim about somebody else's product, where a ✗ against ours is a claim about a tree anyone can grep. Drizzle, Prisma and ZenStack all shipped inside the stale window — footnote ⁶ already dates itself to ZenStack v3 and ⁵ to a Prisma edge story that moves every release. The four new footnotes (⁸–¹¹) carry competitor claims of their own and inherit the same doubt. **Wants their live docs read, not a memory**: nothing in this tree can grade the other three, so the check is a fetch and a date, and the honest fix is to record when each column was last verified rather than to re-verify once and drift again | open | 2026-09-08 | [README.md § How it compares](packages/litestone/README.md) · [parser.js](packages/litestone/src/core/parser.js) |
| <a id="fjs-966"></a>FJS-966 | mesa | **A CDATA section is refused, and in an `<svg>` subtree it is legal HTML that an exporter wrote.** `<![CDATA[ … ]]>` is valid in XML and, per the HTML parsing spec, inside foreign content — an `<svg>` or `<math>` subtree — where it becomes a text node. Mesa refuses it everywhere, and since [FJS-882](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-882) the refusal at least names it and says what to write instead. **The open question is whether it should be accepted in a foreign subtree**, which is where it actually arrives: an SVG pasted out of an exporter. Accepting it is not a parse tweak — it means deciding whether `{` interpolates inside one, and the honest answer is that it should NOT, which makes a CDATA section a literal-text escape hatch and therefore a language feature with a rule of its own. That may well be worth having; it is not a diagnostic. **Cost of NOT doing it is one paste and one error message**, which is why it is filed rather than built. Svelte is no help here — it has no CDATA handling at all, so `<![CDATA[` reaches `tag_invalid_name` and is reported as an invalid element name | open | 2026-09-07 | [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [FJS-882](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-882) |
| <a id="fjs-965"></a>FJS-965 | repo | **`scripts/typecheck.mjs` answers `clean` when it checked nothing, and an unknown flag is ignored in the same direction.** It typechecks the package it is run FROM, so at the workspace ROOT — where there is no package — it prints `[typecheck] clean` and exits 0, and `--only <pkg>` is not a flag it has, so `node scripts/typecheck.mjs --only junction` reads as a scoped check and is a no-op that reports success. **Measured**: it answered clean three times over a junction carrying 10 real errors, which were then found by `bun run ci --fast` and by running the script from the package directory. The CI phase is unaffected — it iterates the members itself — so what this costs is a person or an agent checking one package before pushing, which is the moment the answer is acted on. Two fixes and they are separate: refuse an unrecognized flag by name, and say *no package here* rather than *clean* when the cwd is not a workspace member (naming the packages, or checking them all). The shape is `proof-target`'s — advice that fails when taken is worse than none | open | 2026-09-07 | [typecheck.mjs](scripts/typecheck.mjs) · [FJS-D190](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d190) |
| <a id="fjs-948"></a>FJS-948 | mesa | **An expression in an ATTRIBUTE has no source mapping**, where one in text content now does. `parseAttributes` is handed a sub-reader over the tag, so every offset it could record is relative to the TAG rather than to the file — and a marker built from a tag-relative number points at the wrong line, which is worse than none and is what [FJS-943](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-943) was arranged to avoid. The fix is to plumb the tag's own start into `parseAttributes` and add it back; the marker machinery is already there and the emit site is one line. Left undone rather than half-done: the `srcStart` was wired first and silently produced NOTHING, because `prop.start` is undefined, which is a no-op that reads from the outside exactly like a working feature | open | 2026-09-06 | [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [FJS-943](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-943) |
| <a id="fjs-938"></a>FJS-938 | mesa · email-kit | **One dropped child now warns twice** — Mesa names the component and says it renders no slots, and `@frontierjs/email-kit`'s own `refuseChildren` names the prop to use instead. Both are true and the second is more actionable, which is the whole reason it was not simply deleted when [FJS-926](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-926) landed: Mesa cannot derive *pass `text`*, nor that a slot could not label `Button`'s Outlook VML at all, since that is built as a string. **The fold is for Mesa to name the component's DECLARED props** — the compiler has them where it already has the slot names — after which the kit's message adds only the `Button` reason, which belongs in its doc comment. Until then the duplication is deliberate and recorded here rather than left to be discovered | open | 2026-09-05 | [runtime.js](packages/mesa/src/runtime.js) · [slot-guard.js](packages/email-kit/components/slot-guard.js) · [FJS-926](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-926) |
| <a id="fjs-936"></a>FJS-936 | mesa | **A `debugLabel` build writes `<!--$ undefined -->` for most anchors, so the label that exists to say which block an anchor belongs to says nothing.** `xNode.nodeComment` writes `node.value`, and three of its five call sites construct the node without one — only two pass `value: ''`, and the name is meant to arrive later through the `label.set(...)` reference. Measured on an `{#each}` in a `dev + debugLabel` render: `<!--$ undefined -->`. Harmless in a normal build, since the whole form is emitted only under `debug && debugLabel`, and useless in the build that asks for it. Found while measuring which comments a real render actually leaves, for [FJS-906](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-906) | open | 2026-09-05 | [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [FJS-906](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-906) |
| <a id="fjs-911"></a>FJS-911 | mesa | **Three rules the compiler cites by number have no prose section in `VISION.md` — they exist only as rows of the summary table.** RULES 56, 57 and 58 are missing from the numbered sections while `DECISIONS.md` and three compiler error messages all cite RULE 56 by number, which is why the mesa audit read it as *unimplemented* rather than *uncited*: a reader following the citation lands in a table row and cannot tell whether the sentence they wanted was never written or was deleted. RULE 56 is now correct in the table ([FJS-D211](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d211) — four export forms, and what `var` means), so this is about where the rule LIVES rather than what it says. `test/docs-fences.test.js` cannot see it: a rule with no section has no example to compile. | open | 2026-09-05 | [VISION.md](packages/mesa/docs/VISION.md) · [FJS-D211](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d211) · [Mesa audit](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/05d792af-815d-4b4b-b2d8-9c9cb21efabe) |
| <a id="fjs-907"></a>FJS-907 | mesa | **A derivation chain of a few thousand links overflows the native call stack in the memo pull path, after the flush budget stopped being the limit.** [FJS-851](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-851) made the budget per-node, so a chain settles at any length the flush can reach — measured, 1000 and 1200 now settle where 1000 used to be declared cyclic. At 20000 the flush itself completes and `read` → `_recompute` → `read` overflows instead, somewhere between 2000 and 5000 links. **Measured 2026-09-13 on a plain memo pull, and the ceiling is the ENGINE's**: node overflows between 2000 and 5000 links, bun holds 5000 and overflows by 20000 — so the same app has a different limit on each runtime it is documented to run on. Pre-existing, unrelated to the budget, and reached by nothing anyone has written: filed because the limit is now the stack rather than a number this package chose, and a stack overflow names no node at all where the budget names the one that repeated. | open | 2026-09-05 | [runtime.js](packages/mesa/src/runtime.js) · [FJS-851](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-851) · [Mesa audit](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/05d792af-815d-4b4b-b2d8-9c9cb21efabe) |
| <a id="fjs-774"></a>FJS-774 | junction | **`ctx.locals` carries an index signature, so the typed escape from `$` catches a typo on the app object and not on the call.** [`FJS-D200`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d200) rules `$` a closed set the framework owns and points app state at `ctx.locals`, which is the right seam and is the weaker half of the pattern it names. `ServiceContextLocals` is exported, so an app types its own key by declaration merging exactly as Invariant 5 prescribes for `app.claim()` — verified under `--strict`, `$.locals.branding?.logo` resolves. But the interface also carries `[key: string]: unknown`, so `$.locals.brandng` is legal and answers `unknown`, where `app.brandng` is an error because `App` has no index signature. A misspelled read is silent on the surface the ruling just made the recommended one. **Not fixable by deleting the signature**: `paginate` and `db` are written by framework hooks and app code writes ad-hoc keys today, so removing it breaks every unaugmented app. Wants the shape that keeps both — a declared key typed and an undeclared one still writable — which is a different mechanism from the one `app.claim()` uses. Sits against [`FJS-D199`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d199), which rules that junction refuses an unknown AUTHORING key by name; a locals key is per-call state rather than authoring, so the two are not in conflict, but they are the same question asked of two surfaces and they answer differently | open | 2026-09-04 | [context.ts `ServiceContextLocals`](packages/junction/src/core/context.ts) · [`FJS-706`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-706) · `FJS-D200` · Invariant 5 |
| <a id="fjs-747"></a>FJS-747 | junction | **The suite exits 1 with `0 fail` and no test named, roughly once in ten runs.** Seen three times across 2026-09-02/03, always the same shape: `bun run test` reports `1893 pass · 0 fail`, prints no `(fail)` line anywhere, and exits non-zero — so CI would go red naming nothing. **Two things this row used to say are measured wrong** (2026-09-05, bun 1.3.11). *The stated hypothesis*: an unhandled rejection escaping a test cannot be it — bun installs its own `unhandledRejection` listener, so `process.listenerCount('unhandledRejection')` is **1** inside `bun test`, junction's `listenerCount === 0` guard therefore never installs its rejection handler here at all, and an escaped rejection is attributed by bun to whatever test is running as a `(fail)`, which is the opposite of the observed shape. *The proposed instrument*: a preload registering `process.on('unhandledRejection')` **never fires** — confirmed with the preload loading (it printed) and the rejection still going to bun's own reporter. Following the row as written produces an instrument that is silent for the reason it was built. **What is true instead**: `uncaughtException` has no bun listener, so a started app DOES take that one inside a test process, and `crashStop()` turns a stray throw into an async `process.exit(1)` behind `app.stop()` — a race whose result depends on whether bun force-exits first. All 27 junction test files that `start()` also `stop()`, so nothing is left dangling. **What is ruled out and what is left**: a throw landing while a test runs is reported by bun either way (paired, with and without a started app — identical), and one landing after the last test is swallowed silently at exit 0 because bun force-exits before the timer fires. The remaining window is a throw landing BETWEEN files, where no test is in scope to attribute it to. `process.exitCode = 1` reproduces the reported shape EXACTLY (`1 pass · 0 fail`, no `(fail)` line, exit 1) and nothing in `src/` or `test/` writes it, so it is a shape match rather than the cause — a dependency doing it would look the same. **12 consecutive clean runs** on 2026-09-05, so it is still not reproducible on demand | open | 2026-09-05 | [test/](packages/junction/test) · [app.ts `crashStop`](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-610"></a>FJS-610 | example | **A soft decline is never re-presented, so every invoice is charged exactly once and the distinction the code makes between soft and hard buys nothing.** `declineKind` separates *the money may be there tomorrow* from *this card will never work*, and the whole value of the first is that the clock keeps running and the charge is tried again. Nothing tries it again: `subscription-renew` dispatches `invoice-collect` once, and dunning moves STATES — `Invoice`'s two `@@commitment`s lapse at the grace and cancel at the deadline, `recoverIfClear` recovers when the ledger is clean — and dispatches nothing. So a soft-declined invoice sits `issued` until the deadline cancels the subscription, having been presented one time. **Caravan's retry ladder does not cover it and cannot**: a transport failure throws and retries, but a decline is a domain ANSWER returned as a value, which exits the ladder successfully. The fix is a re-presentment with a clock of its own, under `unique` for [`FJS-609`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-609)'s reason, with the cadence a decision rather than a default — a shop that re-presents daily for 21 days is a shop the card networks notice. Not a defect in anything built: every piece is correct on its own, and this is the half the design implies and nobody wrote. `verify:collect` asserts the soft decline leaves the subscription alone, which is true and is not the same as the money being asked for again | open | 2026-08-30 | [schema.lite `Invoice`](example/db/schema.lite) · [billing.ts `declineKind`](example/api/src/domain/billing/billing.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-373"></a>FJS-373 | cli · css | **`fli check` grades one corner of Invariant 13 and not the one an app breaks.** Corrected 2026-09-13: `css-token-undefined` (error) refuses a styled value naming a token the stylesheets do not define; the raw-color ratio below is still graded by nothing. *Style with a tone and a treatment, never a color* is enforced inside `packages/css` (a literal `font-size` outside `tokens.css` fails its suite) and nowhere in an app. Measured on `KOBAMI/my.maid.tech`, 56k lines written to this framework's ideas: it HAS the semantic layer — `theme-primary`, `theme-danger`, `.btn.outlined`, `.btn.danger`, arrived at independently — and uses **578 raw color utilities against 156 semantic ones**, about 4:1 (`text-gray-500` 74, `bg-gray-100` 54, `text-red-600` 13). The vocabulary was not rejected; it was out-competed by whatever was quicker under deadline, which is what a rule with no check always loses to. `fli check` is the place — it already owns everything derived from the seed while Biome owns generic JavaScript (`FJS-D33`), and it reads `.mesa`, which Biome does not. Open question the rule has to answer before it can exist: an app may legitimately run UnoCSS alongside `@frontierjs/css` (`packages/css/README.md` § Using it with UnoCSS), so the check counts and reports a ratio rather than refusing a class — a warn naming the worst files, in the shape of `package-root-md`, which warns and names rather than failing because the rule cannot tell a stray from the next necessary thing | open | 2026-08-21 | [core/checks.js](packages/cli/core/checks.js) · [vocabulary.js](packages/css/vocabulary.js) · [IDEAS/app-conventions-audit.md](IDEAS/app-conventions-audit.md) § 2.3 |

---

## Needs a decision

These are questions, not defects. A ruling moves to `DECISIONS.md` and closes the
row here. **This table is the questions with an id; most open questions are argued
inside an IDEAS paper's `## Open questions` and stay there.** `fli decisions` reads
both as one queue, and a question becomes pickable — by `fli decide` or on `fli gui`
— once its bullet carries lettered options and a `**Recommend X**` line. A row's
cell cannot hold options, so they go in the paper its Detail links.

## Closed

Kept so a stale document cannot reopen them. Newest first.

**Older closures live in [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).** This
section holds the recent ones and is trimmed past ~40 rows by `fli register:archive`;
the archive holds the rest, verbatim. An id is never reused, so a `FJS-###` named anywhere in the repo
resolves in one file or the other.

| <a id="fjs-2137"></a>FJS-2137 | junction — **connectionsOf and presenceOf answer for this process only, so a second API instance assigns past people connected to the first** | 2026-10-09 | Renamed the three server-side socket reads to say 'on this node' per FJS-D686 A: app.channels.connectionsOf -> localConnectionsOf, presenceOf -> localPresenceOf, presence -> localPresence, and the app shortcuts app.presence/app.presenceOf -> app.localPresence/app.localPresenceOf (App interface typed with the per-node note). 'local' over 'node' because client.nodes is the record graph and Socket.IO spells this scope io.local; presence(channelId) was renamed too since it is the same per-process read and a sibling left unprefixed would teach nothing. Every caller moved (junction tests only), no alias. Proven: test/presence.test.ts + presence-scale.test.ts 18 pass, typecheck clean. · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2168"></a>FJS-2168 | mesa — **`<Child><b slot="default">A</b><p>P</p></Child>` emitted two `default` keys in the slots object, so the explicitly slotted element was overwritten and never rendered; and a block routed to a named slot stripped `slot=` from elements inside a NESTED component call too, sending that call's content to its default slot** | 2026-10-09 | Found while giving slot routing one owner: `routeSlots(node, preserveComments)` in `compiler.js` is now what `makeComponent` builds from and what `lowerComponent` (`ir.js`) lowers, so the DOM and terminal targets cannot route a child differently. It merges `slot="default"` into the default slot in document order and stops its strip at an element, as `blockSlotTarget`'s walk does. No corpus file used either shape (corpus diff identical). Pinned by `test/block-slot-routing.test.js`. · [IDEAS/mesa-ir.md](IDEAS/mesa-ir.md) |
| <a id="fjs-2169"></a>FJS-2169 | mesa — **five attributes on a component call compiled clean and did nothing: `{@attach f}` and `#ref` were dropped, `{*x}` and `*{x}` became a prop literally named `"{*x}"`, and `bind:this={r}` on a non-`let` wired nothing — all without a word, where `on:` and `class:` on the same tag were reported** | 2026-10-09 | Found while classifying component props once: `componentAttributes(node)` in `compiler.js` is the one answer to what each attribute on a component call is, read by `makeComponent` and by `lowerComponent` (`ir.js`). Each of the five is now a `refused` entry with a message that both targets report as a warning and pass nothing for. No corpus file used any of them (corpus diff identical). | <a id="fjs-d673"></a>FJS-D673 | sierra — **Does a plain multi-line String (an address, a note) get a schema word now, or wait for a sighting?** | 2026-10-09 | Ruled [`FJS-D673`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d673): **B** — `@syntax(txt)`: plain text's file extension beside `md`, the catalog's own rule for the argument; `x-syntax: txt` → `textarea` with plain display in `displayFor` (no Markdown rendering). No new attribute, and a natural language is a syntax too. It redefines an existing answer rather than adding one: probed 2026-10-09, `controlFor({ type: 'string', 'x-syntax': 'text' })` answers `{ control: 'code', language: 'text' }` today, an unhighlighted code editor that falls through rather than a designed answer. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |

| <a id="fjs-2149"></a>FJS-2149 | junction — **transactional: does not make a bulk create all or nothing: each row's error is caught inside the transaction and the rest commit, answered 201, though FJS-D11 and litestone.ts both name transactional as the way back to all-or-nothing** | 2026-10-09 | The bulk loops in litestone.ts (create, and bulkByRow for a filtered patch/remove) catch each row's failure to report it, so the method returned normally and the transaction transactionScopeHook opened committed the rows that succeeded. The scope now reads the pipeline's result before it commits: a non-empty errors array throws Unprocessable carrying them in data.errors, which rolls the whole call back and answers 422, so transactional: is the all-or-nothing FJS-D11 names. transactional.test.ts pins it for a bulk create with a duplicate and a filtered patch breaking @unique, with the undeclared control still partial success; lago events.test.ts § a batch now asserts 422 and nothing written. · [events.test.ts § a batch](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/events.test.ts) · [events.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/services/events.service.ts) · [litestone.ts bulk create](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-2150"></a>FJS-2150 | junction — **bearerClaim({ from: header('authorization') }) reads the whole header, scheme included, so an API key sent as 'Authorization: Bearer <key>' (Lago's, Stripe's, every SDK's) is a 401, and every signed-in person, whose session token rides the same header, is refused 'This link does not work' on every service call** | 2026-10-09 | header() handed back Authorization whole, so bearerClaim fingerprinted 'Bearer <key>' and, for a signed-in person, their session token, found no grant and refused both 401. New source authorization() in core/litestone.ts takes the token after 'Bearer ' and returns nothing when ctx.auth.user is set: the transport reads a Bearer as a session ahead of the cookie and x-api-key, so a signed-in caller was signed in BY that token and it is not a grant; derived, so no prefix option. header('authorization') throws by name pointing at it. Proved over a real port in test/bearer-claim.test.ts (a key after the scheme resolves, a session in the same header reaches the service as itself, an unknown bearer is still 401), red with the session pass-through stubbed out; lago and sstime, the two stressors that hand-wrote the workaround, moved to authorization(). The 'link' wording a machine sender reads is FJS-2161. · [principal.ts](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/src/core/principal.ts) · [events.test.ts § bearerClaim's own header()](../fjs-prototypes/lago/api/test/events.test.ts) · [bearerClaim](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) |

Proved by `test/announce-reach.test.ts` (red before, 3/3 after), junction suite 2706 pass, typecheck clean. The cross-process delivery path itself stays FJS-672's ungraded half. · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [audit § 3.7](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |
