| <a id="fjs-2147"></a>FJS-2147 | junction — **A malformed or unknown WebSocket frame is dropped with no protocol_error, a service_call with no id executes, and an object meta.id is stringified into 'id=[object Object] not found'** | 2026-10-09 | The WS message handler in channels.ts dropped an unparseable or unknown frame in silence, ran a service_call with no id, and stringified an object meta.id into a row id. A local refuse() now answers each BY NAME with a protocol_error frame (malformed_frame, unknown_frame, missing_id, invalid_id; invalid_id carries the call's id); a refused call never reaches the service. Proved by test/ws-protocol-error.test.ts (red before, green after, control frames unaffected) and the full junction suite (2703 pass). · [transport/http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [audit § 3.7](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |
| <a id="fjs-1063"></a>FJS-1063 | junction — **`record().ready` resolves `null` for a refusal and for a row that is not there, so every detail screen has to write one vague sentence about two different answers.** `fetch()` inside `record()` catches and returns null with the reason discarded ([client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts)), and `ready` is documented as `Promise<T \| null>` — so this is a GAP against a stated contract rather than a violation of one, which is why it is S3 and not higher. The cost is at every caller: a 403 and a 404 are the same value, so the best a screen can say is *it may not exist, or you may not have access to it*, which is what `core/crud-templates.js` now writes because it cannot do better. **Basecamp's screens are the comparison and they had to solve it outside this API** — `verify:screens`' last section exists to prove that one URL gives *not found* against a 404 and *could not load* against a dead API, two sentences, and it gets them by keeping its own `loadError` off a call that throws. A caller that wants the distinction currently has to make a second request that does. **Two candidate shapes and they are different claims**: `ready` REJECTS and callers catch (matches `find`/`get`, breaks anyone relying on the documented null), or the view carries `error` beside `get()` and `ready` keeps resolving (additive, and the reason survives for the push path too). The second is the one that does not move a documented contract, and choosing is the work | 2026-10-09 | `record()` now carries the reason beside `get()` (`FJS-D695`): `error()` answers `{ status, code }` — 403 a refusal, 404 a row not there (also a `removed` push), 0 nothing answered — and `ready` keeps resolving `null`. It is set by `fetch()` and the composed read, cleared by the next successful read, and re-emitted to subscribers because a refresh or `changed` re-read can be refused after the first load; sierra's offline stub gains `error`, and the generated detail page in `crud-templates.js` now says *not found* / *no access* / *could not load* instead of one vague sentence. Proved by `test/nodes.test.ts` `resource().record(id).error()` (six cases, red before) and the cli `generated-mesa` suite; the example drives failing under `fli prove` are open rows (`FJS-1642`/`FJS-1376` at subDetail.changePlan, `FJS-2027`, `FJS-1382`) and none reaches `record()` · [client/index.ts `record`](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [crud-templates.js](packages/cli/core/crud-templates.js) · [FJS-533](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-533) |
| <a id="fjs-2139"></a>FJS-2139 | litestone — **The model litestone synthesizes for a trail database (db.auditTrail) carries no @@gate, so any caller, a null principal included, reads every trailed write's before- and after-image across every tenant through the client** | 2026-10-09 | makeLoggerAutoModel built the trail model with no @@gate, and the jsonl table every flavor shares ran no plugin either, so even a declared gate on a driver trail model was never graded. The synthesized model now carries @@gate("8"), as the auth models do. buildTableForModel wraps a trail table's reads and creates in the plugin runner's before-hooks, the way makeTable does. The trail's own writes go through ctx.jsonlTableCache, which skips that wrapper. The synthesized gate is left out of schemaDeclaresAccessRules, because no SQL statement reaches the jsonl file and a schema with no gates keeps db.sql. Proved by test/trail-gate.test.ts: a null principal and a level-7 caller are refused, asSystem reads; a declared trail model's own @@gate holds; db.sql survives. Nine litestone.test.ts trail reads made as nobody moved to asSystem(). docs/audit-logging.md now states who may read the trail. · [lago PLAN.md § Phase 1](../fjs-prototypes/lago/PLAN.md) · [app.ts describeDataRealm](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-712"></a>FJS-712 | junction — **The realtime layer's cohorts never collapse, its live stores grow unbounded, and three protocol halves promise a frame the wire never carries.** Umbrella for the remaining realtime findings, `D7`'s second face. **`realtime-9`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-764`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-764)**: the key is the principal's VALUE now, memoized on the session object, and 100 sockets of one person went 606.7 µs → 75.5 µs against a real Data boundary while 100 distinct people did not move. **`realtime-10`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-766`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-766)**: a live store loaded with a `limit` and no `orderBy` grows without bound, because `limit` trims only under an ordering — `load({}, {limit:20})` with 3000 pushes reached `len=3003 stale=2983` and 107 → 221 MB of RSS; wants a trim in arrival order, or a refusal by name for a live load with a limit and no ordering. **`realtime-11`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-765`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-765)**, where the reproduction corrected it: the ungraded half had gone with `FJS-672` already and what was left is the opposite failure — every service but the winner was told NOTHING. As filed: two services over one model announce every write TWICE, the second under the other service's event name and UNGRADED, because the `serviceFor()` index is last-claim-wins and the suppression compares against the winner — `orders.create(customerId=5)` reached an anonymous socket as `orders2 created`. Wants a model → SET of services index, each announcement graded with its own accessor; it is the same index `FJS-700` reads. **`realtime-12`**: the backpressure close code 1013 never reaches the wire — the peer sees 1006 — because closing a socket whose buffer is full tears down abnormally, so any client logic keyed on 1013 is dead, the same class as the dead 4001 branch in `FJS-702`; wants an `overloaded` frame before the close, or the 1013 claim deleted. Isolation itself is good: 40 MB to a sleeping reader left a healthy reader at 200/200. **`realtime-13`**: two replicas share events only if the schema declares `announce crossProcess`, and the default `inProcess` drops them silently with nothing warning — a write on B reached a socket on A after 156 ms under crossProcess and never under inProcess. The cross-process path is also the ungraded one of `FJS-672`. Wants a boot warning when channels are configured under `inProcess`. **`realtime-14`**: malformed and unknown frames are silently discarded with no `protocol_error`; a `service_call` with no `id` executes and answers an id-less result; and a `meta.id` that is an object is `String()`d into `id=[object Object] not found`. Promote any of these to its own id when it is worked | 2026-10-09 | Split, not fixed: the three halves still open were filed as their own rows -- FJS-2145 (realtime-12, 1013 never on the wire; owner ruled: delete the claim), FJS-2146 (realtime-13, inProcess drops cross-replica events silently), FJS-2147 (realtime-14, malformed and id-less frames). realtime-9, -10 and -11 had closed as FJS-764, FJS-766 and FJS-765 · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [transport/http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) · [send-queue.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/send-queue.ts) · [client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [nodes.ts](packages/junction/src/client/nodes.ts) · [FJS-631](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-631) · [audit § 3.7](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |

| <a id="fjs-1756"></a>FJS-1756 | litestone — **Under strategy row, a system read of an @@extensible model that pins the tenant in its where (`{ workspaceId, props: { estimate: 6 } }`) is refused as naming no tenant, though FJS-1290's closure says the filter's pinned tenant is the third place it looks.** | 2026-10-09 | extTenant read the pinned tenant off the top level of the where only, but the read rules (@@softDelete, filters, policy) fold the caller's where under an AND before buildWhereWithEncryption rewrites it, so a system read pinning workspaceId on a model with any such rule found no pin and was refused. | <a id="fjs-1496"></a>FJS-1496 | basecamp · outpost — **Nothing builds an image from a git source.** `buildInitialSteps` gives a git or no-source container app "Build image" and "Push image", which the runner sends as a command-less `/exec`, and outpost acknowledges that as success. `deployments.create` now refuses such an app with a 400 (see basecamp CHANGES 2026-09-28), so the release no longer shows two green steps followed by a Docker Hub pull error. The build itself is still owed: clone, `docker build`, and tag by digest on the machine. | 2026-10-09 | Cause: git container apps were given Build image and Push image steps that reached Outpost as a command-less /exec, which acknowledged them, and create therefore refused such apps. Fix: git container/function apps now run Validate, Build image, Start container, Health check; Build image calls a new outpost POST /build (docker.build, which now throws on a failed git command instead of building over a stale or empty checkout) and records the digest, and /deploy given a digest builds nothing a second time; a rollback to a built release skips the build. Proved by api/test/inline-app.test.ts (steps, empty-source refusal, rollback), outpost test/outpost.test.js (/build, failed clone, deploy-with-digest builds nothing; red with the guard removed), and fli prove: outpost test, verify:docker, basecamp verify:outpost, verify:build green. |

| <a id="fjs-2093"></a>FJS-2093 | mesa — **A parent that both watches a path of a let ($: draft.notes, () => {}) and passes that let down with bind: (<Child bind:record={draft} />) never re-renders a reassignment of it from an event handler (draft = { ...draft, notes: 'n1' }), and the watch sees the OLD value — drop the watch or the bind: and it works** | 2026-10-09 | A let with a $: path watch compiled to a let $$proxy_x that an effect reassigned, and only a path read through it subscribed to anything; a bare read, the prop pushed to a child, kept the object from before a reassignment, and with bind: the child wrote that stale object back. The proxy is now a memo over the let's signal ($$proxy_x()), the watch handler reads its value untracked so a reassignment runs it once, and bindProp unwraps a watch proxy to its raw target so the child's echo stops at the signal's equality check instead of looping to the cycle guard. | <a id="fjs-1969"></a>FJS-1969 | mesa — **A top-level const reading a member of a local handle (`const list = r.list()`) is computed once — `[...list.rows]`, `list.loading && …` — and nothing warns, though its getters read signals** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: FJS-D212 keeps `const list = r.list()` eager, so a static `const` over one of its members (`[...list.rows]`, `list.loading && …`, `list.rows[0]`) is computed once at setup while the handle's getters read signals, and nothing said so. A call through the handle and a derived handle stay quiet. Over 1149 .mesa files in packages, example and fjs-prototypes it fires 4 times, all prototype read-once bugs. Proof: test/const-promotion.test.js § a const reading a member of a call-result handle, red at HEAD; `bun run test` in mesa green, `example: verify:site` green. `example: verify` failed twice on different assertions, with the shared 8110 peer in play, and this change leaves example's compile unchanged (zero new warnings there). |

| <a id="fjs-1485"></a>FJS-1485 | litestone — **Nothing declares a row personal or how long it lives, and a column staff must READ cannot be kept out of the `@@log` trail: an applicant tracker's forget rule, its PII and its report input are all comments, and every applicant's name, email and phone is copied into an append-only store at create.** | 2026-10-09 | The row's three halves were one missing word, and `FJS-D657` supplied it: `@personal` marks a column a reader sees and the trail keeps as `[personal]`, in the create's `after` and in the erasure's own `before` alike, so the 576 trail copies jazzhr measured and the id/`subjectId` joins back to a name now reach nothing personal. A record's lifetime stays app code by the same ruling (Caravan owns the clock, `FJS-D36`); a cascade's missing line is `FJS-1497`. Proved by `test/litestone.test.ts` § *the erasure line keeps no @personal value — update and delete before*, red with the mark removed. The halves the trail cannot reach are filed: freed pages as [FJS-2099](#fjs-2099), a queued payload as [FJS-2100](#fjs-2100). · [schema.lite § Candidate](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L344) · [`IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md`](IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md) · [FJS-1480](#fjs-1480) · [schema.lite § Candidate](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L344) · [§ StageChange](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L453) · [PLAN.md § Q1](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · [compliance-from-the-seed.md](IDEAS/compliance-from-the-seed.md) · [FJS-1480](#fjs-1480) · [`IDEAS/forgetting.md`](IDEAS/forgetting.md) · [PLAN.md § Erasure map](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/PLAN.md) · [FJS-1497](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1497) |
| <a id="fjs-2069"></a>FJS-2069 | litestone — **`asSystem()` skips `@version` even when the caller supplies one, so a job that read a row, did slow work and wrote it back overwrites a person's save in between, with no error** | 2026-10-09 | prepareUpdate in src/core/client.js skipped the whole @version branch under ctx.isSystem, so a version the caller supplied was dropped unread and a job that read, worked and wrote back overwrote a save in between. Per FJS-D659 the branch now runs for a system caller whenever a version is present: a moved row throws VersionConflictError, a non-integer throws VersionRequiredError, and an absent one stays exempt for migrations. test/litestone.test.ts § @version — runtime, two new tests red before the fix; litestone suite 5910 pass. docs/CONSISTENCY.md lost-update row and the README updated. · [FJS-D659](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d659) · [consistency.md](IDEAS/consistency.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1921"></a>FJS-1921 | litestone — **A retention sweep deletes a row and leaves its File objects in storage for ever: $retain() is a raw DELETE, and FileStorage cleans up only from the ORM's delete hooks, so a database's declared retention never reaches the bytes** | 2026-10-09 | runSqliteRetention ran a bare DELETE, and FileStorage releases objects only from onAfterDelete, which the sweep never called. With plugins installed the sweep now adds RETURNING * and gives each table's removed rows to pluginRunner.afterDelete, the hook an ORM delete fires. The boot pass moved after pluginRunner.init and is awaited, and $retain() resolves to the same summary once the objects are gone (index.d.ts, and the example and basecamp jobs, await it). test/retention.test.ts § a swept row takes its files with it covers $retain() and the startup pass against a real local provider: red before, red again with onRemoved stubbed, and the litestone suite passes (5908). · [runSqliteRetention](packages/litestone/src/core/retention.js) · [storage/file-storage.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/file-storage.js) |

## See also

- `ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md` — closed rows aged out of § Closed. Same table, verbatim. Read when tracing a specific id, never cold.
- `CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards — traps that are **correct behavior**; knowing them saves a day. Not issues.
- `DECISIONS.md` — settled rulings. A `FJS-D##` closing here lands there.
- `IDEAS/` — roadmap and design records for work not started. `IDEAS/overview.md`'s wave 0 is the subset of this file that happens to be argued in an ideas file.
- `HANDOFF.md` — per-session narrative, newest first. History, not a ledger.
- `VERIFYING.md` — how to promote a `stale?` row to `open` or `closed`.
